-equivariant structure.
Call it the
\Sigma
-restriction functor and denote it by
(\;\cdot\;)|_{X_{0}}^{\Sigma}
.
It is an exact functor.
Now we have two premorphisms

S\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\theta_{X_{0}}^{\Sigma}}}{{\to}}(S/X_{0})_{\Sigma}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eta_{X_{0}}^{\Sigma}}}{{\to}}S/X_{0},

whose composite is the natural localization premorphism
S\to S/X_{0}

(defined by the functor
X/X_{0}\mapsto X
).
In particular, we have
\For_{X_{0}}^{\Sigma}\mathbin{\circ}|_{X_{0}}^{\Sigma}\cong|_{X_{0}}
.

Let
f\colon S^{\prime}\to S
be a premorphism from another site
S^{\prime}

defined by a functor
f^{-1}
on the underlying categories.
Set
X_{0}^{\prime}=f^{-1}X_{0}
.
We have the localization
f|_{X_{0}}\colon S^{\prime}/X_{0}^{\prime}\to S/X_{0}
of
f
.
It induces a premorphism

f|_{X_{0}}^{\Sigma}\colon(S^{\prime}/X_{0}^{\prime})_{\Sigma}\to(S/X_{0})_{\Sigma}

as above.
The diagram

\begin{CD}S^{\prime}@>{f}>{}>S\\
@V{\theta_{X_{0}^{\prime}}^{\Sigma}}V{}V@V{}V{\theta_{X_{0}}^{\Sigma}}V\\
(S^{\prime}/X_{0}^{\prime})_{\Sigma}@>{}>{f|_{X_{0}}^{\Sigma}}>(S/X_{0})_{\Sigma}\end{CD}

commutes.
In particular, we have a commutative diagram

(3.5.3)

\begin{CD}D(S^{\prime})@>{Rf_{\ast}}>{}>D(S)\\
@V{|_{X_{0}^{\prime}}^{\Sigma}}V{}V@V{}V{|_{X_{0}}^{\Sigma}}V\\
D(S^{\prime}/X_{0}^{\prime})_{\Sigma}@>{}>{R(f|_{X_{0}}^{\Sigma})_{\ast}}>D(S/X_{0})_{\Sigma},\end{CD}

where
D(S/X_{0})_{\Sigma}
means
D((S/X_{0})_{\Sigma})
.

Some restriction is needed for the compatibility with derived pullback:

Proposition 3.5.3
.

The diagram

\begin{CD}D(S)@>{Lf^{\ast}}>{}>D(S^{\prime})\\
@V{|_{X_{0}}^{\Sigma}}V{}V@V{}V{|_{X_{0}}^{\Sigma}}V\\
D(S^{\prime}/X_{0}^{\prime})_{\Sigma}@>{}>{L(f|_{X_{0}}^{\Sigma})^{\ast}}>D(S/X_{0})_{\Sigma}\end{CD}

commutes on the full subcategory of
D(S)

consisting of
f
-compatible objects.

Proof.

Let
G\in D(S)
be
f
-compatible.
We want to show that the natural morphism

L(f|_{X_{0}}^{\Sigma})^{\ast}(G|_{X_{0}}^{\Sigma})\to(Lf^{\ast}G)|_{X_{0}}^{\Sigma}

is an isomorphism.
Applying the forgetful functor
\For_{X_{0}}^{\Sigma}
to this morphism
yields the natural morphism

L(f|_{X_{0}})^{\ast}(G|_{X_{0}})\to(Lf^{\ast}G)|_{X_{0}}

by Proposition
3.5.2
.
This latter morphism is an isomorphism by assumption.
Since
\For_{X_{0}}^{\Sigma}
is conservative
by Proposition
3.5.1
, we get the result.
∎

Now we apply these results to the sites in (
2.3.1
).
For any field
F_{0}\in F^{\mathrm{perar}}
,
the localizations of the sites

\Spec F^{\mathrm{perf}}_{\mathrm{pro}\mathrm{fppf}},\quad\Spec F^{\mathrm{ind}\mathrm{rat}}_{\mathrm{pro}\mathrm{et}},\quad\Spec F^{\mathrm{perar}}_{\mathrm{et}}

at
F_{0}
are

\Spec F^{\mathrm{perf}}_{0,\mathrm{pro}\mathrm{fppf}},\quad\Spec F^{\mathrm{ind}\mathrm{rat}}_{0,\mathrm{pro}\mathrm{et}},\quad\Spec F^{\mathrm{perar}}_{0,\mathrm{et}},

respectively.
See
[
Suz22b
, the paragraphs after Definition 2.1.3]
for related subtleties
around
\Spec F^{\mathrm{ind}\mathrm{rat}}_{\mathrm{pro}\mathrm{et}}
.
Let

h_{F_{0}}\colon\Spec F^{\mathrm{perf}}_{0,\mathrm{pro}\mathrm{fppf}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle f_{F_{0}}}}{{\to}}\Spec F^{\mathrm{ind}\mathrm{rat}}_{0,\mathrm{pro}\mathrm{et}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle g_{F_{0}}}}{{\to}}\Spec F^{\mathrm{perar}}_{0,\mathrm{et}}

be the premorphisms defined by the inclusion functors
and set
\acute{\mathsf{a}}_{F_{0}}=Rf_{F_{0},\ast}Lh_{F_{0}}^{\ast}
.
Let
\Sigma=\Aut(F_{0}/F)
.
Then we have the
\Sigma
-equivariant versions

h_{F_{0}}^{\Sigma}\colon(\Spec F^{\mathrm{perf}}_{0,\mathrm{pro}\mathrm{fppf}})_{\Sigma}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle f_{F_{0}}^{\Sigma}}}{{\to}}(\Spec F^{\mathrm{ind}\mathrm{rat}}_{0,\mathrm{pro}\mathrm{et}})_{\Sigma}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle g_{F_{0}}^{\Sigma}}}{{\to}}(\Spec F^{\mathrm{perar}}_{0,\mathrm{et}})_{\Sigma}

and
\acute{\mathsf{a}}_{F_{0}}^{\Sigma}=Rf_{F_{0},\ast}^{\Sigma}Lh_{F_{0}}^{\Sigma,\ast}
.
We have a commutative diagram

\begin{CD}D(F^{\mathrm{perar}}_{0,\mathrm{et}})_{\Sigma}@>{\acute{\mathsf{a}}_{F_{0}}^{\Sigma}}>{}>D(F^{\mathrm{perf}}_{0,\mathrm{pro}\mathrm{et}})_{\Sigma}\\
@V{\For_{F_{0}}^{\Sigma}}V{}V@V{}V{\For_{F_{0}}^{\Sigma}}V\\
D(F^{\mathrm{perar}}_{0,\mathrm{et}})@>{}>{\acute{\mathsf{a}}_{F_{0}}}>D(F^{\mathrm{perf}}_{0,\mathrm{pro}\mathrm{et}})\end{CD}

by (
3.5.2
) and Proposition
3.5.2
.

Proposition 3.5.4
.

Let
G\in D(F^{\mathrm{perar}}_{\mathrm{et}})
be
h
-compatible
(for example,
G\in\langle\mathcal{W}_{F}\rangle_{F^{\mathrm{perar}}_{\mathrm{et}}}
).
Then

(3.5.4)

(\acute{\mathsf{a}}G)|_{F_{0}}^{\Sigma}\cong\acute{\mathsf{a}}_{F_{0}}^{\Sigma}(G|_{F_{0}}^{\Sigma})

in
D(F^{\mathrm{ind}\mathrm{rat}}_{0,\mathrm{pro}\mathrm{et}})_{\Sigma}
.
In particular,

H^{q}\bigl((\acute{\mathsf{a}}G)|_{F_{0}}\bigr)\cong H^{q}\bigl(\acute{\mathsf{a}}_{F_{0}}(G|_{F_{0}})\bigr)

as
\Sigma
-equivariant sheaves on
\Spec F^{\mathrm{ind}\mathrm{rat}}_{0,\mathrm{pro}\mathrm{et}}
for all
q
,

R\Gamma^{\Sigma}\bigl(F_{0},(\acute{\mathsf{a}}G)|_{F_{0}}^{\Sigma}\bigr)\cong R\Gamma^{\Sigma}\bigl(F_{0},\acute{\mathsf{a}}_{F_{0}}^{\Sigma}(G|_{F_{0}}^{\Sigma})\bigr)

in
D(\Sigma\mathchar 45\relax\mathrm{Mod})
and

H^{q}\bigl(F_{0},\acute{\mathsf{a}}G)\bigr)\cong H^{q}\bigl(F_{0},\acute{\mathsf{a}}_{F_{0}}(G|_{F_{0}})\bigr)

as
\Sigma
-modules for all
q
.

If moreover the isomorphic objects (
3.5.4
) are bounded below
(for example, if
G\in\langle\mathcal{W}_{F}\rangle_{F^{\mathrm{perar}}_{\mathrm{et}}}
),
then we have an isomorphism between the spectral sequence

E_{2}^{ij}=H^{i}\bigl(F_{0},H^{j}(\acute{\mathsf{a}}G)\bigr)\Longrightarrow H^{i+j}(F_{0},\acute{\mathsf{a}}G)

of
\Sigma
-modules and the spectral sequence

E_{2}^{ij}=H^{i}\Bigl(F_{0},H^{j}\bigl(\acute{\mathsf{a}}_{F_{0}}(G|_{F_{0}})\bigr)\Bigr)\Longrightarrow H^{i+j}\bigl(F_{0},\acute{\mathsf{a}}_{F_{0}}(G|_{F_{0}})\bigr)

of
\Sigma
-modules compatible with the
E_{\infty}
-terms.

Proof.

Apply Proposition
3.5.3
for
h
and (
3.5.3
) for
f
.
∎

4.
Relative sites and cup product with support

Let
\mathrm{Sch}
be the category of schemes.

4.1.
Relative sites

The following defines something like “
X\to\Spec F
”
when
X
is not really an
F
-scheme
or, even if
X
is an
F
-scheme,
when we want the base change
X\times_{F}F^{\prime}
to involve completions:

Definition 4.1.1
.

(1)

An
F^{\mathrm{perar}}
-scheme
is
a functor
\mathbf{X}
from the opposite of
F^{\mathrm{perar}}
to
\mathrm{Sch}

satisfying the following two properties:

(a)

\mathbf{X}
commutes with finite coproducts.

(b)

For any étale (resp. faithfully flat étale) morphism
F^{\prime}\to F^{\prime\prime}
in
F^{\mathrm{perar}}
,
the morphism
\mathbf{X}(F^{\prime\prime})\to\mathbf{X}(F^{\prime})
is
finite étale (resp. finite faithfully flat étale),
and we have
\mathbf{X}(F^{\prime\prime}\mathbin{\otimes}_{F^{\prime}}F^{\prime\prime\prime})\overset{\sim}{\to}\mathbf{X}(F^{\prime\prime})\times_{\mathbf{X}(F^{\prime})}\mathbf{X}(F^{\prime\prime\prime})

for any other morphism
F^{\prime}\to F^{\prime\prime\prime}
in
F^{\mathrm{perar}}
.

If
\mathbf{X}(F^{\prime})=\Spec\mathbf{R}(F^{\prime})
is affine for all
F^{\prime}\in F^{\mathrm{perar}}
,
the functor
\mathbf{R}
is called an
F^{\mathrm{perar}}
-algebra
.

(2)

A morphism of
F^{\mathrm{perar}}
-schemes
is a natural transformation
\mathbf{Y}\to\mathbf{X}
of functors such that

\mathbf{Y}(F^{\prime\prime})\overset{\sim}{\to}\mathbf{Y}(F^{\prime})\times_{\mathbf{X}(F^{\prime})}\mathbf{X}(F^{\prime\prime})

for all étale morphisms
F^{\prime}\to F^{\prime\prime}
in
F^{\mathrm{perar}}
.
A morphism of
F^{\mathrm{perar}}
-algebras
\mathbf{R}\to\mathbf{S}
is similarly defined.

The condition (
1b
) ensures that even though a scheme morphism

\mathbf{X}(F)\to\Spec F
does not necessarily exist or

\mathbf{X}(F^{\prime})
is not necessarily
\mathbf{X}(F)\times_{F}F^{\prime}
,
there is a certain morphism of sites
to
\Spec F^{\mathrm{perar}}_{\mathrm{et}}
and
\Spec F^{\mathrm{perar}}_{\mathrm{zar}}

(Proposition
4.1.10
).
We now define an “étale site of
\mathbf{X}
”.

Definition 4.1.2
.

An
étale
\mathbf{X}
-scheme
is a pair
(X^{\prime},F^{\prime})
,
where
F^{\prime}\in F^{\mathrm{perar}}
and
X^{\prime}
is an étale
\mathbf{X}(F^{\prime})
-scheme.
A morphism
(X^{\prime\prime},F^{\prime\prime})\to(X^{\prime},F^{\prime})
of étale
\mathbf{X}
-schemes
consists of an
F
-algebra homomorphism
F^{\prime}\to F^{\prime\prime}

and a scheme morphism
X^{\prime\prime}\to X^{\prime}
such that the diagram

\begin{CD}X^{\prime\prime}@>{}>{}>X^{\prime}\\
@V{}V{}V@V{}V{}V\\
\mathbf{X}(F^{\prime\prime})@>{}>{}>\mathbf{X}(F^{\prime})\end{CD}

commutes.
Composition is defined in the obvious way.

If
\mathbf{X}(F^{\prime})
is an affine scheme,
\Spec\mathbf{R}(F^{\prime})
, for all
F^{\prime}\in F^{\mathrm{perar}}
,
then we define the category of étale
\mathbf{R}
-algebras to be the opposite of
the full subcategory of the category of étale
\mathbf{X}
-schemes
(X^{\prime},F^{\prime})
with
X^{\prime}
affine.
Its objects are pairs
(R^{\prime},F^{\prime})
with
F^{\prime}\in F^{\mathrm{perar}}

and
R^{\prime}
an étale
\mathbf{R}(F^{\prime})
-algebra.

For a scheme
X
,
let
X_{\tau}
be either the small étale site

X_{\mathrm{et}}
(for
\tau=\mathrm{et}
) or
the small Nisnevich site
X_{\mathrm{nis}}
(for
\tau=\mathrm{nis}
).
Let
\Spec F^{\mathrm{perar}}_{\tau}
be
\Spec F^{\mathrm{perar}}_{\mathrm{et}}
if
\tau=\mathrm{et}

and
\Spec F^{\mathrm{perar}}_{\mathrm{zar}}
if
\tau=\mathrm{nis}
.

Definition 4.1.3
.

(1)

A family of morphisms
\{(X^{\prime}_{\lambda},F^{\prime}_{\lambda})\to(X^{\prime},F^{\prime})\}

of étale
\mathbf{X}
-schemes with fixed target
is said to be a
\tau
-covering
if each
F^{\prime}\to F^{\prime}_{\lambda}
is étale
and
\{X^{\prime}_{\lambda}\to X^{\prime}\}
is a covering for the site
X^{\prime}_{\tau}
.

(2)

This class of families defines a pretopology.
Define
\mathbf{X}_{\tau}
to be the category of étale
\mathbf{X}
-schemes
endowed with the topology generated by this pretopology.

Note that
\{F^{\prime}\to F^{\prime}_{\lambda}\}
is not required to be a covering.
The topology of
\mathbf{X}_{\tau}
is really about the
X^{\prime}
components
and not the
F^{\prime}
components.
This is needed for Propositions
4.1.5
and
4.1.6
.
Our default choice of the topology is
\mathrm{et}
.
The Nisnevich topology is used only in
Section
9
in an auxiliary manner.

The covering families for
\mathbf{X}_{\tau}
are generated by
families
\{(X^{\prime}_{\lambda},F^{\prime})\to(X^{\prime},F^{\prime})\}
with
\{X^{\prime}_{\lambda}\to X^{\prime}\}
a
\tau
-covering
and singleton families
\{(X^{\prime\prime},F^{\prime\prime})\to(X^{\prime\prime},F^{\prime})\}
with
F^{\prime}\to F^{\prime\prime}
étale.

Define a morphism of sites

\varepsilon\colon\mathbf{X}_{\mathrm{et}}\to\mathbf{X}_{\mathrm{nis}}

by the identity functor on the underlying categories.

Example 4.1.4
.

We give some examples of sheaves on
\mathbf{X}_{\tau}
.
Let
\mathbb{A}^{1}
be the sheaf of rings on
\mathbf{X}_{\tau}

sending
(X^{\prime},F^{\prime})\mapsto\Gamma(X^{\prime},\mathcal{O}_{X^{\prime}})
.
Let
\mathbf{G}_{a}\in\Ab(\mathbf{X}_{\tau})
be the underlying additive group of
\mathbb{A}^{1}
.
Let
\mathbf{G}_{m}\in\Ab(\mathbf{X}_{\tau})
be
(\mathbb{A}^{1})^{\times}
.
For a quasi-coherent sheaf
M
on
\mathbf{X}(F)
,
the sheaf
(X^{\prime},F^{\prime})\mapsto\Gamma(X^{\prime},M)

(where
M
is pulled back to
X^{\prime}
as an
\mathcal{O}_{X^{\prime}}
-module)
is denoted the same symbol
M
.
For
q\geq 0
, let
\Omega^{r}\in\Ab(\mathbf{X}_{\tau})
be the sheaf

(X^{\prime},F^{\prime})\mapsto\Gamma(X^{\prime},\Omega_{X^{\prime}/\mathbb{Z}}^{r})
.
The differential maps
d\colon\Omega_{X^{\prime}/\mathbb{Z}}^{r}\to\Omega_{X^{\prime}/\mathbb{Z}}^{r+1}

form a morphism of sheaves
d\colon\Omega^{r}\to\Omega^{r+1}
.
Since étale and Nisnevich cohomology with coefficients in
a quasi-coherent
M
and
\Omega_{X^{\prime}/\mathbb{Z}}
agree
(both isomorphic to Zariski cohomology),
we have
R^{q}\varepsilon_{\ast}M=R^{q}\varepsilon_{\ast}\Omega^{r}=0

for all
q\geq 1
.
Let
B\Omega^{r}\subset Z\Omega^{r}\subset\Omega^{r}
be
the image and the kernel of
d
, respectively.

Assume that
\mathbf{X}(F^{\prime})
is regular of characteristic
p
for all
F^{\prime}\in F^{\mathrm{perar}}
.
Then
\nu_{n}(r)
can be viewed as a sheaf

(X^{\prime},F^{\prime})\mapsto\Gamma(X^{\prime},\nu_{n}(r))

on
\mathbf{X}_{\tau}

(where the right-hand side is the sheaf
\nu_{n}(r)
on
X^{\prime}_{\mathrm{et}}
).
The Cartier operator gives a morphism of sheaves

C\colon Z\Omega^{r}\to\Omega^{r}

such that
\nu(r):=\nu_{1}(r)=\Ker(C-1)
.
If
\tau=\mathrm{et}
, the morphism
C-1
is surjective.
If
\tau=\mathrm{nis}
, we set
\xi(r)=\Coker(C-1)
and
\xi=\xi(0)
.

Assume that
\mathbf{X}(F^{\prime})
is regular of dimension
\leq 1

such that
\mathbf{X}(F^{\prime})\times_{\Spec\mathbb{Z}}\Spec\mathbb{Z}[1/p]
is dense for all
F^{\prime}
.
Then
\mathfrak{T}_{n}(r)
can be viewed as a sheaf

(X^{\prime},F^{\prime})\mapsto\Gamma(X^{\prime},\mathfrak{T}_{n}(r))

on
\mathbf{X}_{\tau}
.

Proposition 4.1.5
.

Let
(X^{\prime\prime},F^{\prime\prime})
be an étale
\mathbf{X}
-scheme.
Let
F^{\prime}\to F^{\prime\prime}
be an étale morphism in
F^{\mathrm{perar}}
.
Then the sheafification of the morphism
(X^{\prime\prime},F^{\prime\prime})\to(X^{\prime\prime},F^{\prime})
of representable presheaves of sets
in
\mathbf{X}_{\tau}
is an isomorphism.

Proof.

Let
C\in\Set(\mathbf{X}_{\tau})
be any sheaf.
Since
(X^{\prime\prime},F^{\prime\prime})\to(X^{\prime\prime},F^{\prime})
is a
\tau
-covering,
the sequence

C(X^{\prime\prime},F^{\prime})\to C(X^{\prime\prime},F^{\prime\prime})\rightrightarrows C(X^{\prime\prime},F^{\prime\prime}\mathbin{\otimes}_{F^{\prime}}F^{\prime\prime})

is an equalizer.
Since
F^{\prime}
is étale over
F^{\prime\prime}
,
the multiplication map
F^{\prime\prime}\mathbin{\otimes}_{F^{\prime}}F^{\prime\prime}\twoheadrightarrow F^{\prime\prime}
is a projection onto a direct factor.
In particular, the morphism
(X^{\prime\prime},F^{\prime\prime})\to(X^{\prime\prime},F^{\prime\prime}\mathbin{\otimes}_{F^{\prime}}F^{\prime\prime})
is a
\tau
-covering.
Hence the induced map

C(X^{\prime\prime},F^{\prime\prime}\mathbin{\otimes}_{F^{\prime}}F^{\prime\prime})\to C(X^{\prime\prime},F^{\prime\prime})
is injective.
With this and the above sequence,
we know that the map
C(X^{\prime\prime},F^{\prime})\to C(X^{\prime\prime},F^{\prime\prime})
is bijective.
As
C
is arbitrary, this implies the result.
∎

The cohomology theory of
\mathbf{X}_{\tau}
is the cohomology theories of
\mathbf{X}(F^{\prime})_{\tau}

bundled together:

Proposition 4.1.6
.

Let
(X^{\prime},F^{\prime})
be an étale
\mathbf{X}
-scheme.
Let
\mathbf{X}_{\tau}/(X^{\prime},F^{\prime})
be
the localization of
\mathbf{X}_{\tau}
at
(X^{\prime},F^{\prime})
.
Consider the functor
X^{\prime}_{1}\mapsto(X_{1}^{\prime},F^{\prime})

from the category of étale
X^{\prime}
-schemes
to the category of étale
\mathbf{X}
-schemes over
(X^{\prime},F^{\prime})
.
This defines a morphism of sites

h_{(X^{\prime},F^{\prime})}\colon\mathbf{X}_{\tau}/(X^{\prime},F^{\prime})\to X^{\prime}_{\tau}.

Its pushforward functor is exact.

Proof.

First, the category of étale
X^{\prime}
-schemes has finite inverse limits
and the functor
X^{\prime}_{1}\mapsto(X^{\prime}_{1},F^{\prime})
commutes with these limits.
Hence this functor defines a morphism of sites
(that is, its pullback functor is exact).
Let
C\to D
be an epimorphism of sheaves of sets
on
\mathbf{X}_{\tau}/(X^{\prime},F^{\prime})
.
Let
X^{\prime}_{1}
be an étale
X^{\prime}
-scheme
and let
x\in(h_{(X^{\prime},F^{\prime}),\ast}D)(X^{\prime}_{1})=D(X^{\prime}_{1},F^{\prime})
be an arbitrary element.
There exist a
\tau
-covering
\{(X^{\prime}_{\lambda},F^{\prime}_{\lambda})\to(X^{\prime}_{1},F^{\prime})\}_{\lambda}

and elements
x_{\lambda}\in C(X^{\prime}_{\lambda},F^{\prime}_{\lambda})
such that
the images of
x
and
x_{\lambda}
in
D(X^{\prime}_{\lambda},F^{\prime}_{\lambda})
agree.
By Proposition
4.1.5
,
we may take
F^{\prime}_{\lambda}=F^{\prime}
for all
\lambda
.
Then
x_{\lambda}\in(h_{(X^{\prime},F^{\prime}),\ast}D)(X^{\prime}_{\lambda})
.
As
\{X^{\prime}_{\lambda}\to X^{\prime}_{1}\}_{\lambda}
is a
\tau
-covering,
this shows that
f_{\ast}C\to h_{(X^{\prime},F^{\prime}),\ast}D
is an epimorphism.
∎

Definition 4.1.7
.

In the notation of Proposition
4.1.6
,
for a sheaf
C
on
\mathbf{X}_{\tau}

(of sets or abelian groups),
we call
h_{(X^{\prime},F^{\prime}),\ast}(C|_{(X^{\prime},F^{\prime})})
the
restriction of
C
to
X^{\prime}_{\tau}

and denote it by
C|_{X^{\prime}_{\tau}}
.

If
\mathbf{X}(F^{\prime})=\Spec\mathbf{R}(F^{\prime})
is affine for all
F^{\prime}

and
X^{\prime}=\Spec R^{\prime}
is affine,
then
C|_{X^{\prime}_{\tau}}
is also denoted by
C|_{R^{\prime}_{\tau}}
.
The restrictions as defined above determine the sheaf
C
:

Proposition 4.1.8
.

Consider the following set of data:

(1)

C_{F^{\prime}}\in\Set(\mathbf{X}(F^{\prime})_{\tau})
for each
F^{\prime}\in F^{\mathrm{perar}}
.

(2)

\varphi_{F^{\prime\prime}/F^{\prime}}\in\Hom_{\mathbf{X}(F^{\prime})_{\tau}}(C_{F^{\prime}},f_{F^{\prime\prime}/F^{\prime},\ast}C_{F^{\prime\prime}})

for each morphism
F^{\prime}\to F^{\prime\prime}
in
F^{\mathrm{perar}}
,
where
f_{F^{\prime\prime}/F^{\prime}}\colon\mathbf{X}(F^{\prime\prime})_{\tau}\to\mathbf{X}(F^{\prime})_{\tau}

is the natural morphism.

Assume the following conditions:

(1)

For any morphisms
F^{\prime}\to F^{\prime\prime}\to F^{\prime\prime\prime}
in
F^{\mathrm{perar}}
, the composite

C_{F^{\prime}}\xrightarrow{\varphi_{F^{\prime\prime}/F^{\prime}}}f_{F^{\prime\prime}/F^{\prime},\ast}C_{F^{\prime\prime}}\xrightarrow{f_{F^{\prime\prime}/F^{\prime},\ast}\varphi_{F^{\prime\prime\prime}/F^{\prime\prime}}}f_{F^{\prime\prime\prime}/F^{\prime},\ast}C_{F^{\prime\prime\prime}}

is
\varphi_{F^{\prime\prime\prime}/F^{\prime}}
.

(2)

For any étale morphism
F^{\prime}\to F^{\prime\prime}
in
F^{\mathrm{perar}}
,
the morphism

f_{F^{\prime\prime}/F^{\prime}}^{\ast}C_{F^{\prime}}\to C_{F^{\prime\prime}}

induced by
\varphi_{F^{\prime\prime}/F^{\prime}}
by adjunction is an isomorphism.

Then there exists a unique sheaf
C\in\Set(\mathbf{X}_{\tau})

together with an isomorphism
C|_{\mathbf{X}(F^{\prime})_{\tau}}\cong C_{F^{\prime}}
for each
F^{\prime}\in F^{\mathrm{perar}}

such that
\varphi_{F^{\prime\prime}/F^{\prime}}
corresponds to the morphism

C|_{\mathbf{X}(F^{\prime})_{\tau}}\to f_{F^{\prime\prime}/F^{\prime},\ast}(C|_{\mathbf{X}(F^{\prime\prime})_{\tau}})

given by the natural map
C(X^{\prime},F^{\prime})\to C(X^{\prime}\times_{\mathbf{X}(F^{\prime})}\mathbf{X}(F^{\prime\prime}),F^{\prime\prime})

for any étale
\mathbf{X}(F^{\prime})
-scheme
X^{\prime}
.
Conversely, any
C\in\Set(\mathbf{X}_{\tau})
gives rise to
a data
\{C_{F^{\prime}},\varphi_{F^{\prime\prime}/F^{\prime}}\}
as above by setting

C_{F^{\prime}}=C|_{\mathbf{X}(F^{\prime})_{\tau}}
and

\varphi_{F^{\prime\prime}/F^{\prime}}
the natural morphism
satisfying the above conditions.

Proof.

To define
C
, set
C(X^{\prime},F^{\prime})=C_{F^{\prime}}(X^{\prime})
for any étale
\mathbf{X}
-scheme
(X^{\prime},F^{\prime})
.
For a morphism
(X^{\prime\prime},F^{\prime\prime})\to(X^{\prime},F^{\prime})
,
define a map
C(X^{\prime},F^{\prime})\to C(X^{\prime\prime},F^{\prime\prime})
by the composite

C_{F^{\prime}}(X^{\prime})\xrightarrow{\varphi_{F^{\prime\prime}/F^{\prime},X^{\prime}}}C_{F^{\prime\prime}}(X^{\prime}\times_{\mathbf{X}(F^{\prime})}\mathbf{X}(F^{\prime\prime}))\to C_{F^{\prime\prime}}(X^{\prime\prime}).

Then
C\in\Set(\mathbf{X}_{\tau})

since a covering
\{(X^{\prime}_{i},F^{\prime}_{i})\}
of an object
(X^{\prime},F^{\prime})
decomposed as a covering

\{(X^{\prime}_{i},F^{\prime})\}
of
(X^{\prime},F^{\prime})
and a covering

(X^{\prime}_{i},F^{\prime}_{i})
of
(X^{\prime}_{i},F^{\prime})
for each
i
.
The converse direction is easy.
∎

The topos-theoretic points of
\mathbf{X}_{\tau}
are described by
those of
\mathbf{X}(F^{\prime})_{\tau}
:

Proposition 4.1.9
.

Let
F^{\prime}\in F^{\mathrm{perar}}
be a field.
Let
x^{\prime}
be a point of
\mathbf{X}(F^{\prime})_{\tau}

(that is, the
\Spec
of the henselian or strict henselian local ring of
\mathbf{X}(F^{\prime})
at a point
if
\tau=\mathrm{nis}
or
\tau=\mathrm{et}
, respectively).

(1)

For a sheaf
C\in\Set(\mathbf{X}_{\tau})
,
consider the stalk
C_{x^{\prime}}:=(C|_{\mathbf{X}(F^{\prime})_{\tau}})_{x^{\prime}}

of
C|_{\mathbf{X}(F^{\prime})_{\tau}}\in\Set(\mathbf{X}(F^{\prime})_{\tau})
at
x^{\prime}
.
The functor
C\mapsto C_{x^{\prime}}
gives a morphism of topoi

p_{(x^{\prime},F^{\prime})}\colon\Set\to\Set(\mathbf{X}_{\tau})
,
which is a topos-theoretic point of
\Set(\mathbf{X}_{\tau})
.

(2)

The family of points
\{p_{(x^{\prime},F^{\prime})}\}
over all such pairs
(x^{\prime},F^{\prime})
is conservative.

Proof.

The functor
C\mapsto C|_{\mathbf{X}(F^{\prime})_{\tau}}
commutes with all (small) direct and inverse limits.
Hence the functor
C\mapsto C_{x^{\prime}}
commutes with all direct limits and all finite inverse limits.
Therefore it gives a point.
The conservativity follows from
Proposition
4.1.8
.
∎

Now we define the “structure morphism” for
\mathbf{X}_{\tau}
.
Its relative duality theory, in the case of two-dimensional local rings,
will be the main theorem of this paper.

Proposition 4.1.10
.

The (contravariant) functor
F^{\prime}\mapsto(\mathbf{X}(F^{\prime}),F^{\prime})
defines a morphism of sites

\pi_{\mathbf{X},\tau}\colon\mathbf{X}_{\tau}\to\Spec F^{\mathrm{perar}}_{\tau}.

Proof.

By assumption on
\mathbf{X}
, the stated functor defines a premorphism of sites.
The functor has a left adjoint given by

(X^{\prime},F^{\prime})\mapsto F^{\prime}

(as a functor from the category of étale
\mathbf{X}
-schemes to the opposite of
F^{\mathrm{perar}}
).
Hence its pullback functor is exact.
∎

In particular, we have its right derived pushforward functor

R\pi_{\mathbf{X},\tau,\ast}\colon D(\mathbf{X}_{\tau})\to D(F^{\mathrm{perar}}_{\tau}).

Our default choice of
\tau
is
\mathrm{et}
,
so
\pi_{\mathbf{X},\mathrm{et}}
is also simply denoted by
\pi_{\mathbf{X}}
.
If
\mathbf{X}(F^{\prime})=\Spec\mathbf{R}(F^{\prime})
is affine for all
F^{\prime}
,
then
\pi_{\mathbf{X},\tau}
is also denoted by
\pi_{\mathbf{R},\tau}
.

We will see some functoriality of the above constructions.

Proposition 4.1.11
.

Let
F_{0}\in F^{\mathrm{perar}}
be a field.
Let
\mathbf{X}_{F_{0}}
be the restriction of
\mathbf{X}
to
F_{0}^{\mathrm{perar}}
.
Then
\mathbf{X}_{F_{0}}
is an
F_{0}^{\mathrm{perar}}
-scheme.
We have

\mathbf{X}_{\tau}/(\mathbf{X}(F_{0}),F_{0})\cong\mathbf{X}_{F_{0},\tau},

with which
\pi_{\mathbf{X},\tau}|_{F_{0}}
and
\pi_{\mathbf{X}_{F_{0}}}

(both targeting
\Spec F^{\mathrm{perar}}_{0,\mathrm{et}}
) are compatible.

Proof.

Obvious.
∎

Let
\mathbf{Y}\to\mathbf{X}
be a morphism of
F^{\mathrm{perar}}
-schemes.
For an étale
\mathbf{X}
-scheme
(X^{\prime},F^{\prime})
, define

\mathbf{Y}(X^{\prime},F^{\prime})=X^{\prime}\times_{\mathbf{X}(F^{\prime})}\mathbf{Y}(F^{\prime}).

If
\mathbf{X}(F^{\prime})=\Spec\mathbf{R}(F^{\prime})
and
\mathbf{Y}(F^{\prime})=\Spec\mathbf{S}(F^{\prime})
are
affine for all
F^{\prime}
,
then we also define

\mathbf{S}(R^{\prime},F^{\prime})=R^{\prime}\mathbin{\otimes}_{\mathbf{R}(F^{\prime})}\mathbf{S}(F^{\prime})

for
(R^{\prime},F^{\prime})\in R_{\tau}/F^{\mathrm{perar}}
,
so that
\mathbf{Y}(X^{\prime},F^{\prime})=\Spec\mathbf{S}(R^{\prime},F^{\prime})
.

Proposition 4.1.12
.

The functor
(X^{\prime},F^{\prime})\mapsto(\mathbf{Y}(X^{\prime},F^{\prime}),F^{\prime})

defines a morphism of sites

\pi_{\mathbf{Y}/\mathbf{X},\tau}\colon\mathbf{Y}_{\tau}\to\mathbf{X}_{\tau}

We have
\pi_{\mathbf{X},\tau}\mathbin{\circ}\pi_{\mathbf{Y}/\mathbf{X},\tau}=\pi_{\mathbf{Y},\tau}
.

Proof.

We show that the functor defines a premorphism of sites.
Let
(X^{\prime\prime},F^{\prime\prime})
be an étale
\mathbf{X}
-scheme
and
F^{\prime}\to F^{\prime\prime}
an étale morphism in
F^{\mathrm{perar}}
.
By the definition of morphisms of étale
\mathbf{X}
-schemes,
we have
\mathbf{Y}(X^{\prime\prime},F^{\prime\prime})\overset{\sim}{\to}\mathbf{Y}(X^{\prime\prime},F^{\prime})
.
Hence
(\mathbf{Y}(X^{\prime\prime},F^{\prime\prime}),F^{\prime\prime})\to(\mathbf{Y}(X^{\prime\prime},F^{\prime}),F^{\prime})
is a covering.
For any other morphism
F^{\prime}\to F^{\prime\prime\prime}
in
F^{\mathrm{perar}}
,
we have
\mathbf{Y}(X^{\prime\prime\prime},F^{\prime\prime}\mathbin{\otimes}_{F^{\prime}}F^{\prime\prime\prime})\overset{\sim}{\to}\mathbf{Y}(X^{\prime\prime\prime},F^{\prime\prime\prime})

similarly.
Therefore the diagram

\begin{CD}\bigl(\mathbf{Y}(X^{\prime\prime\prime},F^{\prime\prime}\mathbin{\otimes}_{F^{\prime}}F^{\prime\prime\prime}),F^{\prime\prime}\mathbin{\otimes}_{F^{\prime}}F^{\prime\prime\prime}\bigr)@>{}>{}>(\mathbf{Y}(X^{\prime\prime\prime},F^{\prime\prime\prime}),F^{\prime\prime\prime})\\
@V{}V{}V@V{}V{}V\\
(\mathbf{Y}(X^{\prime\prime},F^{\prime\prime}),F^{\prime\prime})@>{}>{}>(\mathbf{Y}(X^{\prime\prime},F^{\prime}),F^{\prime})\end{CD}

is cartesian.
The case of a covering of the form
\{(X^{\prime}_{\lambda},F^{\prime})\to(X^{\prime},F^{\prime})\}

(where
\{X^{\prime}_{\lambda}\to X^{\prime}\}
is a
\tau
-covering) is easier.
Thus the functor defines a premorphism of sites.

The pullback functor for this premorphism sends a sheaf
C\in\Set(\mathbf{X}_{\tau})

to the sheafification of the presheaf

(Y^{\prime},F^{\prime})\mapsto\varinjlim_{X^{\prime}}C(X^{\prime},F^{\prime})

on
\mathbf{Y}_{\tau}
,
where
X^{\prime}
runs through the diagrams

\begin{CD}Y^{\prime}@>{}>{}>X^{\prime}\\
@V{}V{}V@V{}V{}V\\
\mathbf{Y}(F^{\prime})@>{}>{\pi_{\mathbf{Y}(F^{\prime})/\mathbf{X}(F^{\prime})}}>\mathbf{X}(F^{\prime})\end{CD}

with
X^{\prime}\to\mathbf{X}(F^{\prime})
étale.
These diagrams form (the opposite of) a filtered category,
so its direct limit is exact.
The second statement is obvious.
∎

If
\mathbf{X}(F^{\prime})=\Spec\mathbf{R}(F^{\prime})
and
\mathbf{Y}(F^{\prime})=\Spec\mathbf{S}(F^{\prime})
are
affine for all
F^{\prime}
,
then
\pi_{\mathbf{Y}/\mathbf{X},\tau}
is also denoted by
\pi_{\mathbf{S}/\mathbf{R},\tau}
.

The pullback functor
\pi_{\mathbf{Y}/\mathbf{X},\tau}^{\ast}
is nothing but
the pullback functors for the scheme morphisms

\pi_{\mathbf{Y}(F^{\prime})/\mathbf{X}(F^{\prime})}\colon\mathbf{Y}(F^{\prime})\to\mathbf{X}(F^{\prime})

bundled together:

Proposition 4.1.13
.

For any
C\in\Set(\mathbf{X}_{\tau})
and any
F^{\prime}\in F^{\mathrm{perar}}
,
the natural morphism

\pi_{\mathbf{Y}(F^{\prime})/\mathbf{X}(F^{\prime})}^{\ast}(C|_{\mathbf{X}(F^{\prime})_{\tau}})\to(\pi_{\mathbf{Y}/\mathbf{X},\tau}^{\ast}C)|_{\mathbf{Y}(F^{\prime})_{\tau}}

is an isomorphism.

Proof.

This follows from the description of
\pi_{\mathbf{Y}/\mathbf{X},\tau}^{\ast}C

in the proof of Proposition
4.1.12
.
∎

Set
X=\mathbf{X}(F)
.
Let
Y
be an
X
-scheme.
For any
F^{\prime}\in F^{\mathrm{perar}}
,
define
\mathbf{Y}(F^{\prime})=Y\times_{X}\mathbf{X}(F^{\prime})
.
Then the functor
\mathbf{Y}
is an
F^{\mathrm{perar}}
-scheme.
Hence the natural morphism
\mathbf{Y}\to\mathbf{X}
of
F^{\mathrm{perar}}
-schemes
defines a morphism of sites
\mathbf{Y}_{\tau}\to\mathbf{X}_{\tau}
as above.

From
\mathbf{X}
, we have its henselian local rings as
F^{\mathrm{perar}}
-schemes in the following manner.
Assume that the morphism
\mathbf{X}(F^{\prime})\to X
is affine for all
F^{\prime}\in F^{\mathrm{perar}}
.
Let
x\in X
be a point (of the underlying set).
Then the scheme
\Spec\mathcal{O}_{X,x}\times_{X}\mathbf{X}(F^{\prime})
is affine
and
\Spec\kappa(x)\times_{X}\mathbf{X}(F^{\prime})
is a closed subscheme of it
(where
\kappa(x)
is the residue field of
\mathcal{O}_{X,x}
).
Define
\Spec\mathbf{O}_{X,x}^{h}(F^{\prime})
to be the henselization of the pair

\bigl(\Spec\mathcal{O}_{X,x}\times_{X}\mathbf{X}(F^{\prime}),\Spec\kappa(x)\times_{X}\mathbf{X}(F^{\prime})\bigr).

Then the functor
\mathbf{O}_{X,x}^{h}
is an
F^{\mathrm{perar}}
-algebra.
The natural morphism
\Spec\mathbf{O}_{X,x}^{h}(F^{\prime})\to\mathbf{X}(F^{\prime})
defines
a morphism of
F^{\mathrm{perar}}
-schemes
\Spec\mathbf{O}_{X,x}^{h}\to\mathbf{X}
and hence
a morphism of sites

\pi_{\mathbf{O}_{X,x}^{h}/\mathbf{X},\tau}\colon\Spec\mathbf{O}_{X,x,\tau}^{h}\to\mathbf{X}_{\tau}

as above.

We have a natural transformation

R(\pi_{\mathbf{O}_{X,x}^{h},\tau,\ast}\pi_{\mathbf{O}_{X,x}^{h}/\mathbf{X},\tau}^{\ast})\to R(\pi_{\mathbf{O}_{X,x}^{h},\tau,\ast})\pi_{\mathbf{O}_{X,x}^{h}/\mathbf{X},\tau}^{\ast}\colon D(\mathbf{X}_{\tau})\to D(F^{\mathrm{perar}}_{\tau}).

This is an isomorphism on
D^{+}(\mathbf{X}_{\tau})
by the following:

Proposition 4.1.14
.

The functor

\pi_{\mathbf{O}_{X,x}^{h}/\mathbf{X},\tau}^{\ast}\colon\Ab(\mathbf{X}_{\tau})\to\Ab(\mathbf{O}_{X,x,\tau}^{h})

sends acyclic sheaves to acyclic sheaves.

Proof.

Let
G\in\Ab(\mathbf{X}_{\tau})
be acyclic.
Let
F^{\prime}\in F^{\mathrm{perar}}
.
By Proposition
4.1.6
,
it is enough to show that the restriction

(\pi_{\mathbf{O}_{X,x}^{h}/\mathbf{X},\tau}^{\ast}G)|_{\mathbf{O}_{X,x}^{h}(F^{\prime})_{\tau}}\in\Ab(\mathbf{O}_{X,x}^{h}(F^{\prime})_{\tau})

is acyclic.
Hence by Proposition
4.1.13
,
it is enough to show that

\pi_{\mathbf{O}_{X,x}^{h}(F^{\prime})/\mathbf{X}(F^{\prime})}^{\ast}(G|_{\mathbf{X}(F^{\prime})_{\tau}})\in\Ab(\mathbf{O}_{X,x}^{h}(F^{\prime})_{\tau})

is acyclic.
The acyclicity of
G
implies the acyclicity of

G|_{\mathbf{X}(F^{\prime})_{\tau}}\in\Ab(\mathbf{X}(F^{\prime})_{\tau})
.
Hence

\pi_{\mathbf{O}_{X,x}^{h}(F^{\prime})/\mathbf{X}(F^{\prime})}^{\ast}(G|_{\mathbf{X}(F^{\prime})_{\tau}})

is acyclic
since
\Spec\mathbf{O}_{X,x}^{h}(F^{\prime})
is a filtered inverse limit of
affine étale schemes over
\mathbf{X}(F^{\prime})

and cohomology commutes with such limits
[
AGV72
, Exposé VII, Corollaire 5.9]
.
∎

4.2.
Henselian neighborhoods

We give two kinds of base change results
for closed immersions.
Let
\mathbf{A}
be an
F^{\mathrm{perar}}
-algebra.
Assume that
\mathbf{A}(F^{\prime})
is a finite product of henselian local rings for all
F^{\prime}\in F^{\mathrm{perar}}

and that
\mathbf{A}(F^{\prime})\to\mathbf{A}(F^{\prime\prime})
maps the Jacobson radical into the Jacobson radical
for all morphisms
F^{\prime}\to F^{\prime\prime}
in
F^{\mathrm{perar}}
.
Let
\mathbf{k}(F^{\prime})
be the quotient of
\mathbf{A}(F^{\prime})
by the Jacobson radical
(which is a finite product of fields).

Proposition 4.2.1
.

The functor
\mathbf{k}
is an
F^{\mathrm{perar}}
-algebra.

Proof.

It obviously preserves finite products.
Let
F^{\prime}\to F^{\prime\prime}
be étale in
F^{\mathrm{perar}}
.
Since
\mathbf{A}(F^{\prime})\to\mathbf{A}(F^{\prime\prime})
is finite étale,
the image of the Jacobson radical of
\mathbf{A}(F^{\prime})
generates the Jacobson radical of
\mathbf{A}(F^{\prime\prime})
,
and the induced map
\mathbf{k}(F^{\prime})\to\mathbf{k}(F^{\prime\prime})
is finite étale.
Let
F^{\prime}\to F^{\prime\prime\prime}
be another morphism in
F^{\mathrm{perar}}
.
Then the natural map

\mathbf{A}(F^{\prime\prime})\mathbin{\otimes}_{\mathbf{A}(F^{\prime})}\mathbf{A}(F^{\prime\prime\prime})\to\mathbf{A}(F^{\prime\prime}\mathbin{\otimes}_{F^{\prime}}F^{\prime\prime\prime})

is an isomorphism.
Since
\mathbf{A}(F^{\prime})\to\mathbf{A}(F^{\prime\prime})
is étale,
the ideal of
\mathbf{A}(F^{\prime\prime})\mathbin{\otimes}_{\mathbf{A}(F^{\prime})}\mathbf{A}(F^{\prime\prime\prime})

generated by the image of the Jacobson radical of
\mathbf{A}(F^{\prime\prime\prime})

contains the image of the Jacobson radical of
\mathbf{A}(F^{\prime\prime})
.
On the other hand, the image of the Jacobson radical of
\mathbf{A}(F^{\prime\prime\prime})
in
\mathbf{A}(F^{\prime\prime}\mathbin{\otimes}_{F^{\prime}}F^{\prime\prime\prime})

generates the Jacobson radical
since
F^{\prime\prime\prime}\to F^{\prime\prime}\mathbin{\otimes}_{F^{\prime}}F^{\prime\prime\prime}
is étale.
Therefore, on the quotients, we have

\mathbf{k}(F^{\prime\prime})\mathbin{\otimes}_{\mathbf{k}(F^{\prime})}\mathbf{k}(F^{\prime\prime\prime})\overset{\sim}{\to}\mathbf{k}(F^{\prime\prime}\mathbin{\otimes}_{F^{\prime}}F^{\prime\prime\prime})
.
∎

The natural morphism
\mathbf{A}\twoheadrightarrow\mathbf{k}
of
F^{\mathrm{perar}}
-algebras then defines a morphism of sites

i_{\mathbf{k},\tau}:=\pi_{\mathbf{k}/\mathbf{A},\tau}\colon\Spec\mathbf{k}_{\tau}\to\Spec\mathbf{A}_{\tau}.

On the other hand, for an étale
\mathbf{k}
-algebra
(k^{\prime},F^{\prime})
,
the
\mathbf{k}(F^{\prime})
-algebra
k^{\prime}
is finite étale,
so it admits a canonical finite étale lifting to
\mathbf{A}(F^{\prime})
.
Denote this lifting by
\mathbf{A}(k^{\prime},F^{\prime})
.

Proposition 4.2.2
.

The functor
(k^{\prime},F^{\prime})\mapsto(\mathbf{A}(k^{\prime},F^{\prime}),F^{\prime})
defines a morphism of sites

\pi_{\mathbf{A}/\mathbf{k},\tau}\colon\Spec\mathbf{A}_{\tau}\to\Spec\mathbf{k}_{\tau}.

Proof.

Let
(k^{\prime},F^{\prime})\to(k^{\prime\prime},F^{\prime\prime})
be a morphism of étale
\mathbf{k}
-algebras
that appears in a covering family for the site
\Spec\mathbf{k}_{\tau}
,
that is, that both
F^{\prime}\to F^{\prime\prime}
and
k^{\prime}\to k^{\prime\prime}
are étale.
In the commutative diagram

\begin{CD}\mathbf{A}(F^{\prime})@>{}>{}>\mathbf{A}(F^{\prime\prime})\\
@V{}V{}V@V{}V{}V\\
\mathbf{A}(F^{\prime},k^{\prime})@>{}>{}>\mathbf{A}(F^{\prime\prime},k^{\prime\prime}),\end{CD}

all the morphisms but the lower horizontal one is finite étale.
It follows that the lower horizontal map is finite étale.
Hence
(\mathbf{A}(F^{\prime},k^{\prime}),F^{\prime})\to(\mathbf{A}(F^{\prime\prime},k^{\prime\prime}),F^{\prime\prime})
appears
in a covering family for
\Spec\mathbf{A}_{\tau}
.

The quotient of the map
\mathbf{A}(k^{\prime},F^{\prime})\to\mathbf{A}(k^{\prime\prime},F^{\prime\prime})
by the Jacobson radicals
is
k^{\prime}\to k^{\prime\prime}
.
It follows that if
k^{\prime}\to k^{\prime\prime}
is a
\tau
-covering,
then so is
\mathbf{A}(k^{\prime},F^{\prime})\to\mathbf{A}(k^{\prime\prime},F^{\prime\prime})
.

Let
(k^{\prime},F^{\prime})\to(k^{\prime\prime\prime},F^{\prime\prime\prime})
be another morphism of étale
\mathbf{k}
-algebras.
Then both
\mathbf{A}(k^{\prime\prime},F^{\prime\prime})\mathbin{\otimes}_{\mathbf{A}(k^{\prime},F^{\prime})}\mathbf{A}(k^{\prime\prime\prime},F^{\prime\prime\prime})

and
\mathbf{A}(k^{\prime\prime}\mathbin{\otimes}_{k^{\prime}}k^{\prime\prime\prime},F^{\prime\prime}\mathbin{\otimes}_{F^{\prime}}F^{\prime\prime\prime})

are finite étale liftings of the finite étale
k^{\prime\prime\prime}
-algebra
k^{\prime\prime}\mathbin{\otimes}_{k^{\prime}}k^{\prime\prime\prime}

to
\mathbf{A}(k^{\prime\prime\prime},F^{\prime\prime\prime})
.
Hence the natural morphism between them is an isomorphism.
Thus the stated functor defines a premorphism of sites.

The pullback
\pi_{\mathbf{A}/\mathbf{k},\tau}^{\ast\mathrm{set}}C
of a sheaf
C\in\Set(\mathbf{k}_{\tau})

is given by the
\tau
-sheafification of the presheaf that sends
an étale
\mathbf{A}
-algebra
(A^{\prime},F^{\prime})
to the direct limit of the sets
C(k^{\prime},F^{\prime})
,
where the index category consists of an étale
\mathbf{k}(F^{\prime})
-algebra
k^{\prime}

and an
\mathbf{A}(F^{\prime})
-algebra homomorphism
\mathbf{A}(k^{\prime},F^{\prime})\to A^{\prime}
.
This index category is filtered.
Since filtered direct limits are exact,
we know that
\pi_{\mathbf{A}/\mathbf{k},\tau}^{\ast\mathrm{set}}
is exact.
Thus
\pi_{\mathbf{A}/\mathbf{k},\tau}
is a morphism of sites.
∎

Proposition 4.2.3
.

The right adjoint to the functor
(A^{\prime},F^{\prime})\mapsto(A^{\prime}\mathbin{\otimes}_{\mathbf{A}(F^{\prime})}\mathbf{k}(F^{\prime}),F^{\prime})

from étale
\mathbf{A}
-algebras to étale
\mathbf{k}
-algebras
is given by the functor
(k^{\prime\prime},F^{\prime\prime})\mapsto(\mathbf{A}(k^{\prime\prime},F^{\prime\prime}),F^{\prime\prime})
.
In particular, we have
i_{\mathbf{k},\tau}^{\ast}\cong\pi_{\mathbf{A}/\mathbf{k},\tau,\ast}
,
hence

\pi_{\mathbf{k},\tau,\ast}i_{\mathbf{k},\tau}^{\ast}\cong\pi_{\mathbf{A},\tau,\ast},\quad R\pi_{\mathbf{k},\tau,\ast}i_{\mathbf{k},\tau}^{\ast}\cong R\pi_{\mathbf{A},\tau,\ast}.

Proof.

Morphisms
(A^{\prime}\mathbin{\otimes}_{\mathbf{A}(F^{\prime})}\mathbf{k}(F^{\prime}),F^{\prime})\to(k^{\prime\prime},F^{\prime\prime})

bijectively corresponds to morphisms

A^{\prime}\mathbin{\otimes}_{\mathbf{A}(F^{\prime})}\mathbf{k}(F^{\prime\prime})\to k^{\prime\prime}

of finite étale
\mathbf{k}(F^{\prime\prime})
-algebras.
Morphisms
(A^{\prime},F^{\prime})\to(\mathbf{A}(k^{\prime\prime},F^{\prime\prime}),F^{\prime\prime})

bijectively corresponds to morphisms

A^{\prime}\mathbin{\otimes}_{\mathbf{A}(F^{\prime})}\mathbf{A}(F^{\prime\prime})\to\mathbf{A}(k^{\prime\prime},F^{\prime\prime})

of étale
\mathbf{A}(F^{\prime\prime})
-algebras.
The latter morphisms factor through
the maximal quotient of
A^{\prime}\mathbin{\otimes}_{\mathbf{A}(F^{\prime})}\mathbf{A}(F^{\prime\prime})

finite over
\mathbf{A}(F^{\prime\prime})
.
Hence these morphisms correspond bijectively
by the functoriality of finite étale liftings.
∎

In particular,
i_{\mathbf{k},\tau}^{\ast}
sends K-injectives to K-injectives.

A little more generally,
let
\mathbf{A}
be an
F^{\mathrm{perar}}
-algebra
and
\mathbf{B}
another
F^{\mathrm{perar}}
-algebra.
Let
\mathbf{A}\twoheadrightarrow\mathbf{B}
be a (section-wise) surjection of
F^{\mathrm{perar}}
-algebras
with kernel
\mathbf{I}
.
Assume that
(\mathbf{A}(F^{\prime}),\mathbf{I}(F^{\prime}))
is a henselian pair for all
F^{\prime}\in F^{\mathrm{perar}}
.
Set
i_{\mathbf{B},\mathrm{et}}=\pi_{\mathbf{B}/\mathbf{A},\mathrm{et}}
.
We have a natural morphism

R\pi_{\mathbf{A},\mathrm{et},\ast}\to R\pi_{\mathbf{B},\mathrm{et},\ast}i_{\mathbf{B},\mathrm{et}}^{\ast}\colon D(\mathbf{A}_{\mathrm{et}})\to D(F^{\mathrm{perar}}_{\mathrm{et}}).

Proposition 4.2.4
.

For any
G\in D_{\mathrm{tor}}^{+}(\mathbf{A}_{\mathrm{et}})
,
the above morphism applied to
G
,

R\pi_{\mathbf{A},\mathrm{et},\ast}G\to R\pi_{\mathbf{B},\mathrm{et},\ast}i_{\mathbf{B},\mathrm{et}}^{\ast}G,

is an isomorphism.

Proof.

We may assume that
G
is concentrated in degree zero.
For
F^{\prime}\in F^{\mathrm{perar}}
, we want to show that the morphism

R\Gamma(\mathbf{A}(F^{\prime}),G)\to R\Gamma(\mathbf{B}(F^{\prime}),i_{\mathbf{B},\mathrm{et}}^{\ast}G)

is an isomorphism.
But this is Gabber’s affine analogue of proper base change
[
Gab94
, Theorem 1]
.
∎

4.3.
Shriek functors and cup product

We develop a theory of compact support cohomology and cup product
for relative sites.

Let
\mathbf{X}
be an
F^{\mathrm{perar}}
-scheme.
Set
X=\mathbf{X}(F)
.
Let
i\colon Z\hookrightarrow X
be a closed immersion.
Set
j\colon U=X\setminus Z\hookrightarrow X
.
Then
Z
and
U
define
F^{\mathrm{perar}}
-schemes
\mathbf{Z}
and
\mathbf{U}
, respectively,
as seen after Proposition
4.1.13
.
The morphisms
i
and
j
define natural morphisms

i\colon\mathbf{Z}\to\mathbf{X}
and
j\colon\mathbf{U}\to\mathbf{X}
of
F^{\mathrm{perar}}
-schemes,
which then define morphisms of sites

i\colon\mathbf{Z}_{\mathrm{et}}\to\mathbf{X}_{\mathrm{et}}
and
j\colon\mathbf{U}_{\mathrm{et}}\to\mathbf{X}_{\mathrm{et}}
.
For any
F^{\prime}\in F^{\mathrm{perar}}
,
we have the usual shriek functors

i_{F^{\prime}}^{!}\colon\Ab(\mathbf{X}(F^{\prime})_{\mathrm{et}})\to\Ab(\mathbf{Z}(F^{\prime})_{\mathrm{et}})

and
j_{F^{\prime},!}\colon\Ab(\mathbf{U}(F^{\prime})_{\mathrm{et}})\to\Ab(\mathbf{X}(F^{\prime})_{\mathrm{et}})

(see
[
Sta21
, Tags 0F4Z and 0F59]
for example).
For
G\in\Ab(\mathbf{X}(F^{\prime})_{\mathrm{et}})
and
H\in\Ab(\mathbf{U}(F^{\prime})_{\mathrm{et}})
,
by Proposition
4.1.8
,
the sheaves
i_{F^{\prime}}^{!}(G|_{\mathbf{X}(F^{\prime})})
and
j_{F^{\prime},!}(H|_{\mathbf{U}(F^{\prime})})

together with natural functoriality with respect to varying
F^{\prime}\in F^{\mathrm{perar}}

define objects of
\Ab(\mathbf{Z}_{\mathrm{et}})
and
\Ab(\mathbf{X}_{\mathrm{et}})
, respectively.
We denote them by
i^{!}G
and
j_{!}H
, respectively.
The obtained functors
i^{!}\colon\Ab(\mathbf{X}_{\mathrm{et}})\to\Ab(\mathbf{Z}_{\mathrm{et}})

and
j_{!}\colon\Ab(\mathbf{U}_{\mathrm{et}})\to\Ab(\mathbf{X}_{\mathrm{et}})

are right adjoint and left adjoint, respectively,
to
i_{\ast}\colon\Ab(\mathbf{Z}_{\mathrm{et}})\to\Ab(\mathbf{X}_{\mathrm{et}})

and
j^{\ast}\colon\Ab(\mathbf{X}_{\mathrm{et}})\to\Ab(\mathbf{U}_{\mathrm{et}})
.
The functor
i^{!}
is left exact and the functor
j_{!}
is exact.
We have distinguished triangles

(4.3.1)

i_{\ast}Ri^{!}G\to G\to Rj_{\ast}j^{\ast}G,\quad j_{!}j^{\ast}G\to G\to i_{\ast}i^{\ast}G

in
D(\mathbf{X}_{\mathrm{et}})
functorial in
G\in D(\mathbf{X}_{\mathrm{et}})

(see
[
BS15
, Lemmas 6.1.11 and 6.1.16]
and
[
Sta21
, 0GKL]
for example).
Hence the functors
Ri^{!}
,
j_{!}
are the right derived functors
of the functors of additive categories with translation (
[
KS06
, Definition 10.1.1]
)

\displaystyle[i^{\ast}\to i^{\ast}j_{\ast}j^{\ast}][-1]\colon\mathrm{Ch}(\mathbf{X}_{\mathrm{et}})\to\mathrm{Ch}(\mathbf{Z}_{\mathrm{et}}),

\displaystyle[j_{\ast}\to i_{\ast}i^{\ast}j_{\ast}][-1]\colon\mathrm{Ch}(\mathbf{U}_{\mathrm{et}})\to\mathrm{Ch}(\mathbf{X}_{\mathrm{et}}),

respectively.
See
[
KS06
, Sections 13.3, 14.3]
for derived functors of functors of additive categories with translation.

We define

R\pi_{\mathbf{U},!}:=R\pi_{\mathbf{X},\ast}j_{!}\colon D(\mathbf{U}_{\mathrm{et}})\to D(F^{\mathrm{perar}}_{\mathrm{et}}).

This of course depends not only on
\mathbf{U}

but also on
\mathbf{X}
and the embedding
j\colon U\hookrightarrow X
.
We have distinguished triangles

(4.3.2)

\displaystyle R\pi_{\mathbf{Z},\ast}Ri^{!}G\to R\pi_{\mathbf{X},\ast}G\to R\pi_{\mathbf{U},\ast}j^{\ast}G,

(4.3.3)

\displaystyle R\pi_{\mathbf{U},!}j^{\ast}G\to R\pi_{\mathbf{X},\ast}G\to R\pi_{\mathbf{Z},\ast}i^{\ast}G

in
D(F^{\mathrm{perar}}_{\mathrm{et}})
functorial in
G\in D(\mathbf{X}_{\mathrm{et}})
.
The functor
R\pi_{\mathbf{Z},\ast}Ri^{!}
is the right derived functor of

[\pi_{\mathbf{X},\ast}\to\pi_{\mathbf{U},\ast}j^{\ast}][-1]
.

We have natural morphisms

(4.3.4)

i^{\ast}G\mathbin{\otimes}^{L}Ri^{!}H\to Ri^{!}(G\mathbin{\otimes}^{L}H),\quad Rj_{\ast}G^{\prime}\mathbin{\otimes}^{L}j_{!}H^{\prime}\overset{\sim}{\to}j_{!}(G^{\prime}\mathbin{\otimes}^{L}H^{\prime})

in
D(\mathbf{Z}_{\mathrm{et}})
,
D(\mathbf{X}_{\mathrm{et}})
, respectively,
functorial in
G,H\in D(\mathbf{X}_{\mathrm{et}})
,

G^{\prime},H^{\prime}\in D(\mathbf{U}_{\mathrm{et}})
, respectively.
Applying (
2.2.1
) for
R\pi_{\mathbf{Z},\ast}
and
R\pi_{\mathbf{X},\ast}
,
we obtain canonical morphisms

(4.3.5)

\displaystyle R\pi_{\mathbf{Z},\ast}i^{\ast}G\mathbin{\otimes}^{L}R\pi_{\mathbf{Z},\ast}Ri^{!}H\to R\pi_{\mathbf{Z},\ast}Ri^{!}(G\mathbin{\otimes}^{L}H),

(4.3.6)

\displaystyle R\pi_{\mathbf{U},\ast}G^{\prime}\mathbin{\otimes}^{L}R\pi_{\mathbf{U},!}H^{\prime}\to R\pi_{\mathbf{U},!}(G^{\prime}\mathbin{\otimes}^{L}H^{\prime})

in
D(F^{\mathrm{perar}}_{\mathrm{et}})
.

We will see how the localization triangles (
4.3.2
) and (
4.3.3
)
interact with the cup product morphisms (
4.3.5
) and (
4.3.6
).
This will be useful to relate duality statements for
U

and duality statements for a smaller open set
U^{\prime}

(see the proof of Proposition
6.5.2

and the proof of Proposition
10.1.5
in Section
10.6
).

Proposition 4.3.1
.

Let
G,H\in D(\mathbf{X}_{\mathrm{et}})
.
Consider the morphisms

\displaystyle i_{\ast}Ri^{!}R\sheafhom_{\mathbf{X}_{\mathrm{et}}}(G,H)

\displaystyle\to i_{\ast}R\sheafhom_{\mathbf{Z}_{\mathrm{et}}}(i^{\ast}G,Ri^{!}H)

\displaystyle\to R\sheafhom_{\mathbf{X}_{\mathrm{et}}}(i_{\ast}i^{\ast}G,i_{\ast}Ri^{!}H)

\displaystyle\to R\sheafhom_{\mathbf{X}_{\mathrm{et}}}(i_{\ast}i^{\ast}G,H),

\displaystyle Rj_{\ast}j^{\ast}R\sheafhom_{\mathbf{X}_{\mathrm{et}}}(G,H)

\displaystyle\cong Rj_{\ast}R\sheafhom_{\mathbf{U}_{\mathrm{et}}}(j^{\ast}G,j^{\ast}H)

\displaystyle\to R\sheafhom_{\mathbf{X}_{\mathrm{et}}}(j_{!}j^{\ast}G,j_{!}j^{\ast}H)

\displaystyle\to R\sheafhom_{\mathbf{X}_{\mathrm{et}}}(j_{!}j^{\ast}G,H)

in
D(\mathbf{X}_{\mathrm{et}})
obtained by applying (
4.3.4
).
They form a morphism of distinguished triangles

\begin{CD}i_{\ast}Ri^{!}R\sheafhom_{\mathbf{X}_{\mathrm{et}}}(G,H)@>{}>{}>R\sheafhom_{\mathbf{X}_{\mathrm{et}}}(G,H)@>{}>{}>Rj_{\ast}j^{\ast}R\sheafhom_{\mathbf{X}_{\mathrm{et}}}(G,H)\\
@V{}V{}V\Big\|@V{}V{}V\\
R\sheafhom_{\mathbf{X}_{\mathrm{et}}}(i_{\ast}i^{\ast}G,H)@>{}>{}>R\sheafhom_{\mathbf{X}_{\mathrm{et}}}(G,H)@>{}>{}>R\sheafhom_{\mathbf{X}_{\mathrm{et}}}(j_{!}j^{\ast}G,H),\end{CD}

where the rows are the triangles (
4.3.1
).

Proof.

This is formal.
∎

Assume that
i\colon Z\hookrightarrow X
factorizes as closed immersions

i^{\prime\prime}\colon Z\hookrightarrow Z^{\prime}
and
i^{\prime}\colon Z^{\prime}\hookrightarrow X
.
Set
U^{\prime}=X\setminus Z^{\prime}
.
Let
j^{\prime\prime}\colon U^{\prime}\hookrightarrow U
and
j^{\prime}=j\mathbin{\circ}j^{\prime\prime}\colon U^{\prime}\hookrightarrow X
be the inclusions.
Set
W=U\cap Z^{\prime}
.
Let
i^{\prime}|_{U}\colon W\hookrightarrow U
and
j|_{Z^{\prime}}\colon W\hookrightarrow Z^{\prime}
be the inclusions.
Note that

R\pi_{\mathbf{U},!}(i^{\prime}|_{U})_{\ast}=R\pi_{\mathbf{X},\ast}j_{!}(i^{\prime}|_{U})_{\ast}=R\pi_{\mathbf{X},\ast}i^{\prime}_{\ast}(j|_{Z^{\prime}})_{!}=R\pi_{\mathbf{Z^{\prime}},\ast}(j|_{Z^{\prime}})_{!}=R\pi_{\mathbf{W},!},

where
R\pi_{\mathbf{W},!}
is defined
with respect to the embedding
j|_{Z^{\prime}}\colon W\hookrightarrow Z^{\prime}
.
Hence applying
R\pi_{\mathbf{U},!}
to the distinguished triangle

j^{\prime\prime}_{!}j^{\prime\prime\ast}G\to G\to(i^{\prime}|_{U})_{\ast}(i^{\prime}|_{U})^{\ast}G

yields a distinguished triangle

(4.3.7)

R\pi_{\mathbf{U}^{\prime},!}j^{\prime\prime\ast}G\to R\pi_{\mathbf{U},!}G\to R\pi_{\mathbf{W},!}(i^{\prime}|_{U})^{\ast}G

in
D(F^{\mathrm{perar}}_{\mathrm{et}})
functorial in
G\in D(\mathbf{U}_{\mathrm{et}})
.
Also we have a distinguished triangle

(4.3.8)

R\pi_{\mathbf{W},\ast}R(i^{\prime}|_{U})^{!}G\to R\pi_{\mathbf{U},\ast}G\to R\pi_{\mathbf{U}^{\prime},\ast}j^{\prime\prime\ast}G.

Applying Proposition
4.3.1
to

\mathbf{U}^{\prime}_{\mathrm{et}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle j^{\prime\prime}}}{{\to}}\mathbf{U}_{\mathrm{et}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle i^{\prime}|_{U}}}{{\leftarrow}}\mathbf{W}
,
we obtain a morphism of distinguished triangles

\begin{CD}(i^{\prime}|_{U})_{\ast}R(i^{\prime}|_{U})^{!}[G,H]_{U}@>{}>{}>[G,H]_{U}@>{}>{}>Rj^{\prime\prime}_{\ast}j^{\prime\prime\ast}[G,H]_{U}\\
@V{}V{}V\Big\|@V{}V{}V\\
[(i^{\prime}|_{U})_{\ast}(i^{\prime}|_{U})^{\ast}G,H]_{U}@>{}>{}>[G,H]_{U}@>{}>{}>[j^{\prime\prime}_{!}j^{\prime\prime\ast}G,H]_{U}.\end{CD}

in
D(\mathbf{U}_{\mathrm{et}})
functorial in
G,H\in D(\mathbf{U}_{\mathrm{et}})
,
where we abbreviated
R\sheafhom_{\mathbf{U}_{\mathrm{et}}}
as
[\;\cdot\;,\;\cdot\;]_{U}
.
Applying
R\pi_{\mathbf{U},\ast}
and using (
4.3.6
),
we obtain:

Proposition 4.3.2
.

We have a morphism of distinguished triangles

(4.3.9)

\begin{CD}R\pi_{\mathbf{W},\ast}R(i^{\prime}|_{U})^{!}[G,H]_{U}@>{}>{}>R\pi_{\mathbf{U},\ast}[G,H]_{U}@>{}>{}>R\pi_{\mathbf{U}^{\prime},\ast}[j^{\prime\prime\ast}G,j^{\prime\prime\ast}H]_{U^{\prime}}\\
@V{}V{}V@V{}V{}V@V{}V{}V\\
[R\pi_{\mathbf{W},!}(i^{\prime}|_{U})^{\ast}G,R\pi_{\mathbf{U},!}H]_{F}@>{}>{}>[R\pi_{\mathbf{U},!}G,R\pi_{\mathbf{U},!}H]_{F}@>{}>{}>[R\pi_{\mathbf{U}^{\prime},!}j^{\prime\prime\ast}G,R\pi_{\mathbf{U},!}H]_{F}\end{CD}

in
D(F^{\mathrm{perar}}_{\mathrm{et}})
,
where we abbreviated
R\sheafhom_{F^{\mathrm{perar}}_{\mathrm{et}}}
as
[\;\cdot\;,\;\cdot\;]_{F}
.
Here the upper triangle is (
4.3.8
) and the lower (
4.3.7
).
The vertical morphisms are (
4.3.5
) and (
4.3.6
)
followed by the natural morphisms

R\pi_{\mathbf{W},!}R(i^{\prime}|_{U})^{!}H\to R\pi_{\mathbf{U},!}H

and
R\pi_{\mathbf{U}^{\prime},!}j^{\prime\prime\ast}H\to R\pi_{\mathbf{U},!}H
.

We will bring the above constructions to
D(F^{\mathrm{ind}\mathrm{rat}}_{\mathrm{pro}\mathrm{et}})
.
Define

\displaystyle R\mathbf{\Gamma}(\mathbf{U},\;\cdot\;):=\acute{\mathsf{a}}R\pi_{\mathbf{U},\ast},\quad R\mathbf{\Gamma}_{c}(\mathbf{U},\;\cdot\;):=\acute{\mathsf{a}}R\pi_{\mathbf{U},!}\colon

\displaystyle D(\mathbf{U}_{\mathrm{et}})\to D(F^{\mathrm{ind}\mathrm{rat}}_{\mathrm{pro}\mathrm{et}}).

Set
\mathbf{H}^{q}=H^{q}R\mathbf{\Gamma}
and
\mathbf{H}_{c}^{q}=H^{q}R\mathbf{\Gamma}_{c}
.
Let
G,H\in D(\mathbf{U}_{\mathrm{et}})
be such that

R\pi_{\mathbf{U},\ast}G
and
R\pi_{\mathbf{U},!}H
are
h
-acyclic and
h
-compatible
(which is the case if
R\pi_{\mathbf{U},\ast}G
and
R\pi_{\mathbf{U},!}H
are
in
\langle\mathcal{W}_{F}\rangle_{F^{\mathrm{perar}}_{\mathrm{et}}}

by Proposition
3.1.4
).
Then for any
F^{\prime}\in F^{\mathrm{perar}}
, we have

R\Gamma(F^{\prime},R\mathbf{\Gamma}(\mathbf{U},G))\cong R\Gamma(\mathbf{U}(F^{\prime}),G),\quad R\Gamma(F^{\prime},R\mathbf{\Gamma}_{c}(\mathbf{U},H))\cong R\Gamma_{c}(\mathbf{U}(F^{\prime}),H)

in
D(\Ab)
functorial in such
F^{\prime}
,
G
and
H
,
where
R\Gamma_{c}(\mathbf{U}(F^{\prime}),\;\cdot\;)
is
defined as the composite of
R\Gamma(\mathbf{U}(F^{\prime}),\;\cdot\;)

and the extension-by-zero functor
D(\mathbf{U}(F^{\prime})_{\mathrm{et}})\to D(\mathbf{X}(F^{\prime})_{\mathrm{et}})
.
The morphism (
4.3.6
) induces a morphism

R\mathbf{\Gamma}(\mathbf{U},G)\mathbin{\otimes}^{L}R\mathbf{\Gamma}_{c}(\mathbf{U},H)\to R\mathbf{\Gamma}_{c}(\mathbf{U},G\mathbin{\otimes}^{L}H)

in
D(F^{\mathrm{ind}\mathrm{rat}}_{\mathrm{pro}\mathrm{et}})
functorial in such
G
and
H

by (
2.3.2
).
For any
F^{\prime}\in F^{\mathrm{perar}}
, applying the functor
R\Gamma(F^{\prime},\;\cdot\;)
yields a morphism

R\Gamma(\mathbf{U}(F^{\prime}),G)\mathbin{\otimes}^{L}R\Gamma_{c}(\mathbf{U}(F^{\prime}),H)\to R\Gamma_{c}(\mathbf{U}(F^{\prime}),G\mathbin{\otimes}^{L}H),

which agrees with the usual cup product morphism.

We will see how the above constructions behave under base change
with the associated equivariant structures.
For a field
F_{0}\in F^{\mathrm{perar}}
with
\Sigma=\Aut(F_{0}/F)
,
we have the restrictions
\mathbf{X}_{F_{0}}
and
\mathbf{U}_{F_{0}}

of
\mathbf{X}
and
\mathbf{U}
, respectively, to
F_{0}^{\mathrm{perar}}
.
They are
F_{0}^{\mathrm{perar}}
-schemes by Proposition
4.1.11
.
Hence we have the restrictions of the above constructions

\displaystyle\pi_{\mathbf{U}_{F_{0}}}\colon\mathbf{U}_{F_{0},\mathrm{et}}\to\Spec F^{\mathrm{perar}}_{0,\mathrm{et}},

\displaystyle R\pi_{\mathbf{U}_{F_{0}},!}\colon D(\mathbf{U}_{F_{0},\mathrm{et}})\to D(F^{\mathrm{perar}}_{0,\mathrm{et}})

and their
\Sigma
-equivariant versions (Section
3.5
)

\displaystyle\pi_{\mathbf{U}_{F_{0}}}^{\Sigma}\colon(\mathbf{U}_{F_{0},\mathrm{et}})_{\Sigma}\to(\Spec F^{\mathrm{perar}}_{0,\mathrm{et}})_{\Sigma},

\displaystyle R\pi_{\mathbf{U}_{F_{0}},!}^{\Sigma}\colon D(\mathbf{U}_{F_{0},\mathrm{et}})_{\Sigma}\to D(F^{\mathrm{perar}}_{0,\mathrm{et}})_{\Sigma}.

Define

\displaystyle R\mathbf{\Gamma}^{\Sigma}(\mathbf{U}_{F_{0}},\;\cdot\;):=\acute{\mathsf{a}}_{F_{0}}^{\Sigma}R\pi_{\mathbf{U}_{F_{0}},\ast}^{\Sigma},\quad R\mathbf{\Gamma}_{c}^{\Sigma}(\mathbf{U}_{F_{0}},\;\cdot\;):=\acute{\mathsf{a}}_{F_{0}}^{\Sigma}R\pi_{\mathbf{U}_{F_{0}},!}^{\Sigma}\colon

\displaystyle D(\mathbf{U}_{F_{0},\mathrm{et}})_{\Sigma}\to D(F^{\mathrm{ind}\mathrm{rat}}_{0,\mathrm{pro}\mathrm{et}})_{\Sigma}.

Denote the restriction functor

D(\mathbf{U}_{\mathrm{et}})\to D(\mathbf{U}_{F_{0},\mathrm{et}})
by
(\;\cdot\;)|_{\mathbf{U}_{F_{0}}}

and its
\Sigma
-equivariant version

D(\mathbf{U}_{\mathrm{et}})\to D(\mathbf{U}_{F_{0},\mathrm{et}})_{\Sigma}
by
(\;\cdot\;)|_{\mathbf{U}_{F_{0}}}^{\Sigma}
.

Proposition 4.3.3
.

Let
G\in D(\mathbf{U}_{\mathrm{et}})
.
Assume that
R\pi_{\mathbf{U},\ast}G
and
R\pi_{\mathbf{U},!}G
are

h
-compatible
(for example, that they are objects of
\langle\mathcal{W}_{F}\rangle_{F^{\mathrm{perar}}_{\mathrm{et}}}
).
Then

(4.3.10)

\begin{gathered}R\mathbf{\Gamma}(\mathbf{U},G)|_{F_{0}}^{\Sigma}\cong R\mathbf{\Gamma}^{\Sigma}(\mathbf{U}_{F_{0}},G|_{\mathbf{U}_{F_{0}}}^{\Sigma}),\\
R\mathbf{\Gamma}_{c}(\mathbf{U},G)|_{F_{0}}^{\Sigma}\cong R\mathbf{\Gamma}_{c}^{\Sigma}(\mathbf{U}_{F_{0}},G|_{\mathbf{U}_{F_{0}}}^{\Sigma})\end{gathered}

in
D(F^{\mathrm{ind}\mathrm{rat}}_{0,\mathrm{pro}\mathrm{et}})_{\Sigma}
.
In particular,

\displaystyle\mathbf{H}^{q}(\mathbf{U},G)|_{F_{0}}\cong\mathbf{H}^{q}\bigl(\mathbf{U}_{F_{0}},G|_{\mathbf{U}_{F_{0}}}\bigr),

\displaystyle\mathbf{H}_{c}^{q}(\mathbf{U},G)|_{F_{0}}\cong\mathbf{H}_{c}^{q}\bigl(\mathbf{U}_{F_{0}},G|_{\mathbf{U}_{F_{0}}}\bigr)

as
\Sigma
-equivariant sheaves on
\Spec F^{\mathrm{ind}\mathrm{rat}}_{0,\mathrm{pro}\mathrm{et}}
for all
q
,

\displaystyle R\Gamma^{\Sigma}\bigl(F_{0},R\mathbf{\Gamma}(\mathbf{U},G)|_{F_{0}}^{\Sigma}\bigr)\cong R\Gamma^{\Sigma}\bigl(F_{0},R\mathbf{\Gamma}^{\Sigma}(\mathbf{U}_{F_{0}},G|_{\mathbf{U}_{F_{0}}}^{\Sigma})\bigr),

\displaystyle R\Gamma^{\Sigma}\bigl(F_{0},R\mathbf{\Gamma}_{c}(\mathbf{U},G)|_{F_{0}}^{\Sigma}\bigr)\cong R\Gamma^{\Sigma}\bigl(F_{0},R\mathbf{\Gamma}_{c}^{\Sigma}(\mathbf{U}_{F_{0}},G|_{\mathbf{U}_{F_{0}}}^{\Sigma})\bigr)

in
D(\Sigma\mathchar 45\relax\mathrm{Mod})
and

\displaystyle H^{q}\bigl(F_{0},R\mathbf{\Gamma}(\mathbf{U},G)\bigr)\cong H^{q}\bigl(F_{0},R\mathbf{\Gamma}(\mathbf{U}_{F_{0}},G|_{\mathbf{U}_{F_{0}}})\bigr),

\displaystyle H^{q}\bigl(F_{0},R\mathbf{\Gamma}_{c}(\mathbf{U},G)\bigr)\cong H^{q}\bigl(F_{0},R\mathbf{\Gamma}_{c}(\mathbf{U}_{F_{0}},G|_{\mathbf{U}_{F_{0}}})\bigr)

as
\Sigma
-modules for all
q
.

If moreover the isomorphic objects (
4.3.10
) are bounded below
(for example, if
R\pi_{\mathbf{U},\ast}G
and
R\pi_{\mathbf{U},!}G
are
objects of
\langle\mathcal{W}_{F}\rangle_{F^{\mathrm{perar}}_{\mathrm{et}}}
),
then we have an isomorphism between the spectral sequence

E_{2}^{ij}=H^{i}\bigl(F_{0},\mathbf{H}^{j}(\mathbf{U},G)\bigr)\Longrightarrow H^{i+j}\bigl(F_{0},R\mathbf{\Gamma}(\mathbf{U},G)\bigr)

of
\Sigma
-modules and the spectral sequence

E_{2}^{ij}=H^{i}\bigl(F_{0},\mathbf{H}^{j}(\mathbf{U}_{F_{0}},G|_{\mathbf{U}_{F_{0}}})\bigr)\Longrightarrow H^{i+j}\bigl(F_{0},R\mathbf{\Gamma}(\mathbf{U}_{F_{0}},G|_{\mathbf{U}_{F_{0}}})\bigr)

of
\Sigma
-modules compatible with the
E_{\infty}
-terms,
and also we have an isomorphism between the spectral sequence

E_{2}^{ij}=H^{i}\bigl(F_{0},\mathbf{H}_{c}^{j}(\mathbf{U},G)\bigr)\Longrightarrow H^{i+j}\bigl(F_{0},R\mathbf{\Gamma}_{c}(\mathbf{U},G)\bigr)

of
\Sigma
-modules and the spectral sequence

E_{2}^{ij}=H^{i}\bigl(F_{0},\mathbf{H}_{c}^{j}(\mathbf{U}_{F_{0}},G|_{\mathbf{U}_{F_{0}}})\bigr)\Longrightarrow H^{i+j}\bigl(F_{0},R\mathbf{\Gamma}_{c}(\mathbf{U}_{F_{0}},G|_{\mathbf{U}_{F_{0}}})\bigr)

of
\Sigma
-modules compatible with the
E_{\infty}
-terms.

Proof.

This follows from Proposition
3.5.4
.
∎

4.4.
The case of finite boundary

We continue the notation of Section
4.3
.
Assume moreover the following two conditions:

(1)

\mathbf{Z}(F^{\prime})
is a finite set of closed points of
\mathbf{X}(F^{\prime})
for all
F^{\prime}
, and

(2)

\mathbf{X}(F^{\prime})\to\mathbf{X}(F^{\prime\prime})
is affine for all morphisms
F^{\prime}\to F^{\prime\prime}
in
F^{\mathrm{perar}}
.

We only need this case in this paper.
We will give more explicit descriptions of the constructions of the previous subsection.

For
x\in Z
, set
\mathbf{X}_{x}^{h}=\Spec\mathbf{O}_{X,x}^{h}
,
and
\mathbf{U}_{x}^{h}=\mathbf{X}_{x}^{h}\times_{\mathbf{X}}\mathbf{U}

(which are well-defined by the second condition above).
The inclusion
x\hookrightarrow X
defines an
F^{\mathrm{perar}}
-scheme
\mathbf{x}

and morphisms
i_{x}\colon\mathbf{x}\to\mathbf{X}

and
i_{x}^{h}\colon\mathbf{x}\to\mathbf{X}_{x}^{h}
.
We have a commutative diagram

\begin{CD}\mathbf{U}_{x,\mathrm{et}}^{h}@>{j_{x}^{h}}>{}>\mathbf{X}_{x,\mathrm{et}}^{h}@<{i_{x}^{h}}<{}<\mathbf{x}_{\mathrm{et}}\\
@V{\pi_{\mathbf{U}_{x}^{h}/\mathbf{U}}}V{}V@V{}V{\pi_{\mathbf{X}_{x}^{h}/\mathbf{X}}}V\Big\|\\
\mathbf{U}_{\mathrm{et}}@>{}>{j}>\mathbf{X}_{\mathrm{et}}@<{}<{i_{x}}<\mathbf{x}_{\mathrm{et}}\end{CD}

of morphisms of sites,
with the composite morphism

\pi_{\mathbf{U}_{x}^{h}/\mathbf{X}}\colon\mathbf{U}_{x,\mathrm{et}}^{h}\to\mathbf{X}_{\mathrm{et}}
.
By Proposition
4.2.3
, we have

R\pi_{\mathbf{X}_{x},\ast}\cong R\pi_{\mathbf{x},\ast}i_{x}^{h,\ast}.

Define

(4.4.1)

R\pi_{\mathbf{X}_{x},!}:=R\pi_{\mathbf{x},\ast}Ri_{x}^{h,!}\colon D(\mathbf{X}_{x,\mathrm{et}})\to D(F^{\mathrm{perar}}_{\mathrm{et}}).

Then (
4.3.2
) gives a distinguished triangle

(4.4.2)

R\pi_{\mathbf{X}_{x},!}G\to R\pi_{\mathbf{X}_{x},\ast}G\to R\pi_{\mathbf{U}_{x},\ast}j_{x}^{h,\ast}G

and (
4.3.5
) gives a morphism

(4.4.3)

R\pi_{\mathbf{X}_{x},!}G\mathbin{\otimes}^{L}R\pi_{\mathbf{X}_{x},\ast}H\to R\pi_{\mathbf{X}_{x},!}(G\mathbin{\otimes}^{L}H).

The functor
Ri^{!}G
decomposes into local factors:

Proposition 4.4.1
.

For
G\in D^{+}(\mathbf{X}_{\mathrm{et}})
, the natural morphism

i_{\ast}Ri^{!}G\to\bigoplus_{x\in Z}R\pi_{\mathbf{X}_{x}^{h}/\mathbf{X},\ast}i_{x,\ast}^{h}Ri_{x}^{h,!}\pi_{\mathbf{X}_{x}^{h}/\mathbf{X}}^{\ast}G

in
D(\mathbf{X}_{\mathrm{et}})
is an isomorphism.
In particular, we have

R\pi_{\mathbf{Z},\ast}Ri^{!}G\overset{\sim}{\to}\bigoplus_{x\in Z}R\pi_{\mathbf{X}_{x}^{h},!}\pi_{\mathbf{X}_{x}^{h}/\mathbf{X}}^{\ast}G

in
D(F^{\mathrm{perar}}_{\mathrm{et}})
.

Proof.

We may assume
G\in\Ab(\mathbf{X}_{\mathrm{et}})
.
By restricting to
\mathbf{X}(F^{\prime})_{\mathrm{et}}
for all
F^{\prime}\in F^{\mathrm{perar}}
,
this reduces to the usual excision isomorphism for étale cohomology

[
Mil80
, Chapter III, Corollary 1.28]
.
∎

Therefore (
4.3.2
) can be written as a distinguished triangle

\bigoplus_{x\in Z}R\pi_{\mathbf{X}_{x}^{h},!}\pi_{\mathbf{X}_{x}^{h}/\mathbf{X}}^{\ast}G\to R\pi_{\mathbf{X},\ast}G\to R\pi_{\mathbf{U},\ast}j^{\ast}G

in
D(F^{\mathrm{perar}}_{\mathrm{et}})
functorial in
G\in D^{+}(\mathbf{X}_{\mathrm{et}})
.

Proposition 4.4.2
.

The functor
i^{\ast}\colon\Ab(\mathbf{X}_{\mathrm{et}})\to\Ab(\mathbf{Z}_{\mathrm{et}})

sends acyclic sheaves to acyclic sheaves.

Proof.

For each
x\in Z
, the pullback of
i^{\ast}
to
\Ab(\mathbf{x}_{\mathrm{et}})
can be written as

i_{x}^{\ast}=i_{x}^{h,\ast}\pi_{\mathbf{X}_{x}^{h}/\mathbf{X}}^{\ast}
.
The functor
\pi_{\mathbf{X}_{x}^{h}/\mathbf{X}}^{\ast}
sends acyclics to acyclics
by Proposition
4.1.14

and the functor
i_{x}^{h,\ast}\cong\pi_{\mathbf{X}_{x}^{h}/\mathbf{x},\ast}
sends acyclics to acyclics
by Proposition
4.2.3
.
∎

Therefore
R\pi_{\mathbf{U},!}
restricted to
D^{+}(\mathbf{U}_{\mathrm{et}})
is the right derived functor of

[\pi_{\mathbf{U},\ast}\to\pi_{\mathbf{Z},\ast}i^{\ast}j_{\ast}][-1].

We have

\pi_{\mathbf{Z},\ast}i^{\ast}\cong\bigoplus_{x\in Z}\pi_{\mathbf{x},\ast}i_{x}^{\ast}\cong\bigoplus_{x\in Z}\pi_{\mathbf{x},\ast}i_{x}^{h,\ast}\pi_{\mathbf{X}_{x}^{h}/\mathbf{X}}^{\ast}\cong\bigoplus_{x\in Z}\pi_{\mathbf{X}_{x}^{h},\ast}\pi_{\mathbf{X}_{x}^{h}/\mathbf{X}}^{\ast},

where the last isomorphism is by Proposition
4.2.3
.
Hence, on
\Ab(\mathbf{U}_{\mathrm{et}})
, we have

\pi_{\mathbf{Z},\ast}i^{\ast}j_{\ast}\cong\bigoplus_{x\in Z}\pi_{\mathbf{X}_{x}^{h},\ast}\pi_{\mathbf{X}_{x}^{h}/\mathbf{X}}^{\ast}j_{\ast}\cong\bigoplus_{x\in Z}\pi_{\mathbf{X}_{x}^{h},\ast}j_{x,\ast}^{h}\pi_{\mathbf{U}_{x}^{h}/\mathbf{U}}^{\ast}\cong\bigoplus_{x\in Z}\pi_{\mathbf{U}_{x}^{h},\ast}\pi_{\mathbf{U}_{x}^{h}/\mathbf{U}}^{\ast}.

Therefore we have the following explicit description
of
R\pi_{\mathbf{U},!}
:

Proposition 4.4.3
.

The functor
R\pi_{\mathbf{U},!}
restricted to
D^{+}(\mathbf{U}_{\mathrm{et}})
is
the right derived functor of

(4.4.4)

\left[\pi_{\mathbf{U},\ast}\to\bigoplus_{x\in Z}\pi_{\mathbf{U}_{x}^{h},\ast}\pi_{\mathbf{U}_{x}^{h}/\mathbf{U}}^{\ast}\right][-1].

We thus have a distinguished triangle

(4.4.5)

R\pi_{\mathbf{U},!}G\to R\pi_{\mathbf{U},\ast}G\to\bigoplus_{x\in Z}R\pi_{\mathbf{U}_{x}^{h},\ast}\pi_{\mathbf{U}_{x}^{h}/\mathbf{U}}^{\ast}G

in
D(F^{\mathrm{perar}}_{\mathrm{et}})
functorial in
G\in D^{+}(\mathbf{U}_{\mathrm{et}})
.
The presentation (
4.4.4
) gives a priori another cup product morphism,
but it is the same as the previous cup product morphism
(under the bounded below condition):

Proposition 4.4.4
.

Let
\pi_{\mathbf{U},!}^{\prime}
be the functor (
4.4.4
).
For
G,H\in\mathrm{Ch}^{+}(\mathbf{U}_{\mathrm{et}})
, let

\pi_{\mathbf{U},\ast}\sheafhom_{\mathbf{U}_{\mathrm{et}}}(G,H)\to\sheafhom_{F^{\mathrm{perar}}_{\mathrm{et}}}(\pi_{\mathbf{U},!}^{\prime}G,\pi_{\mathbf{U},!}^{\prime}H)

be the morphism in
\mathrm{Ch}(F^{\mathrm{perar}}_{\mathrm{et}})

defined by the functoriality of
\pi_{\mathbf{U},!}^{\prime}
.
Then the induced morphism

(4.4.6)

R\pi_{\mathbf{U},\ast}R\sheafhom_{\mathbf{U}_{\mathrm{et}}}(G,H)\to R\sheafhom_{F^{\mathrm{perar}}_{\mathrm{et}}}(R\pi_{\mathbf{U},!}^{\prime}G,R\pi_{\mathbf{U},!}^{\prime}H)

in
D(F^{\mathrm{perar}}_{\mathrm{et}})
for
G,H\in D^{+}(\mathbf{U}_{\mathrm{et}})

agrees with the morphism

R\pi_{\mathbf{U},\ast}R\sheafhom_{\mathbf{U}_{\mathrm{et}}}(G,H)\to R\sheafhom_{F^{\mathrm{perar}}_{\mathrm{et}}}(R\pi_{\mathbf{U},!}G,R\pi_{\mathbf{U},!}H)

coming from (
4.3.6
) via the above isomorphism

R\pi_{\mathbf{U},!}\cong R\pi_{\mathbf{U},!}^{\prime}
.

Proof.

This is formal.
∎

Lemma 4.4.5
.

Let
S
be a site.
Let
G,H\in D^{+}(S)
.
Then
G\mathbin{\otimes}^{L}H\in D^{+}(S)
.

Proof.

This is obvious if
G,H\in\Ab(S)

since
\Tor_{\geq 2}^{\Ab}=0
and hence
\Tor_{\geq 2}^{\Ab(S)}=0
.
This implies the general case
by a two-variable version of
[
Sta21
, Tag 07K8 (4c)]
.
∎

Proposition 4.4.6
.

Let
G,H\in D^{+}(\mathbf{U}_{\mathrm{et}})
.
Then the morphism

R\pi_{\mathbf{U},\ast}^{\prime}G^{\prime}\mathbin{\otimes}^{L}R\pi_{\mathbf{U},!}^{\prime}H^{\prime}\to R\pi_{\mathbf{U},!}^{\prime}(G^{\prime}\mathbin{\otimes}^{L}H^{\prime})

defined by (
4.4.6
) agrees with the morphism (
4.3.6
)
via the above isomorphism

R\pi_{\mathbf{U},!}\cong R\pi_{\mathbf{U},!}^{\prime}
.

Proof.

This follows from Proposition
4.4.4
and Lemma
4.4.5
.
∎

4.5.
Fibered sites and cup product

We want a version of (
4.4.4
) where henselian localizations are replaced by completions.
But this is not obvious since the pullback functor for
the morphism
\hat{U}_{x,\mathrm{et}}\to U_{\mathrm{et}}
from the punctured completion
does not send acyclic sheaves to acyclic sheaves
and hence we cannot naturally take derived functors.
We deal with this problem by considering the category of tuples

(G,(G_{x})_{x\in Z},(\varphi_{x})_{x\in Z})
,
where
G
is a sheaf on
U_{\mathrm{et}}
,

G_{x}
is a sheaf on
\hat{U}_{x,\mathrm{et}}

and
\varphi_{x}
is a morphism
\pi_{\hat{U}_{x}/U}^{\ast}G\to G_{x}
.
An injective resolution in this category consists of
an injective resolution for
G
in
\Ab(U_{\mathrm{et}})
and
an injective resolution for
G_{x}
in
\Ab(\hat{U}_{x,\mathrm{et}})
for each
x

with a compatibility between them,
so that there is no problem in taking derived functors.
Actually the category of such tuples is the category of sheaves on the “total site”
of a certain fibered site.
We will develop this machinery,
especially its compact support and cup product formalism.

Let
I
be a finite poset (viewed as a category).
Let
\{S_{i}\}_{i\in I}=(\{S_{i}\}_{i\in I},\{f_{ji}\}_{i\leq j})
be
a fibered site (
[
AGV72
, Exposé VI, §7.2.1]
).
This means that
S_{i}
for each
i\in I
is a site and

f_{ji}\colon S_{j}\to S_{i}
for each
j\geq i
is a morphism of sites
such that
f_{ji}\mathbin{\circ}f_{kj}\cong f_{ki}
naturally
(where we more precisely assume this on the level of the underlying functors

f_{kj}^{-1}\mathbin{\circ}f_{ji}^{-1}\cong f_{ki}^{-1}
).
Assume for simplicity that

S_{i}
contains an initial object
\emptyset
,
the empty family
\{\,\}
is a covering of
\emptyset

(so that the sheafification of
\emptyset
is the initial sheaf)
and the underlying functor
f_{ji}^{-1}
of
f_{ji}
sends
\emptyset
to
\emptyset
.
(Note that we can always add an initial object to a site without changing its topos.)

We denote the total site of
\{S_{i}\}_{i\in I}

(
[
AGV72
, Exposé VI, §Définition 7.4.1]
)
by
(S_{i})_{i\in I}
.
More explicitly, an object is a tuple
(\{X_{i}\}_{i\in I},\{\varphi_{ij}\}_{i\leq j})
,
where
X_{i}\in S_{i}
and

\varphi_{ij}\colon f_{ji}^{-1}X_{i}\to X_{j}
is a morphism in
S_{j}

satisfying the cocycle condition:

\varphi_{jk}\mathbin{\circ}(f_{kj}^{-1}\varphi_{ij})=\varphi_{ik}

for
i\leq j\leq k
.
A morphism from
(\{X_{i}^{\prime}\},\{\varphi_{ij}^{\prime}\})
to
(\{X_{i}\},\{\varphi_{ij}\})

is a set of morphisms
X_{i}^{\prime}\to X_{i}
over
i\in I
that is compatible with

\{\varphi_{ij}^{\prime}\}
and
\{\varphi_{ij}\}
.
A covering of an object
(\{X_{i}\},\{\varphi_{ij}\})
is a family of morphisms

(\{X_{\lambda i}\},\{\varphi_{\lambda ij}\})\to(\{X_{i}\},\{\varphi_{ij}\})

indexed by
\lambda\in\Lambda

such that the family
X_{\lambda i}\to X_{i}
indexed by
\lambda
is a covering for all
i
.
(In
[
AGV72
, Exposé VI, §Définition 7.4.1]
,
the total site is defined with a slightly different underlying category,
but the above site defines an equivalent topos,
which can be seen from the description below.)

An object of the topos
\Set(S_{i})_{i\in I}=\Set((S_{i})_{i\in I})
consists of a tuple

F=(\{F_{i}\}_{i\in I},\{\varphi_{ij}\}_{i\leq j})
,
where
F_{i}\in\Set(S_{i})
and

\varphi_{ij}\colon f_{ji}^{\ast}F_{i}\to F_{j}
is a morphism in
\Set(S_{j})

(or equivalently,
\varphi_{ij}\colon F_{i}\to f_{ji,\ast}F_{j}
in
\Set(S_{i})
)
satisfying the cocycle condition:
\varphi_{jk}\mathbin{\circ}(f_{kj}^{\ast}\varphi_{ij})=\varphi_{ik}

for
i\leq j\leq k
.
A morphism from
(\{F_{i}^{\prime}\},\{\varphi_{ij}^{\prime}\})
to
(\{F_{i}\},\{\varphi_{ij}\})

is a set of morphisms
F_{i}^{\prime}\to F_{i}
over
i\in I
that is compatible with

\{\varphi_{ij}^{\prime}\}
and
\{\varphi_{ij}\}
.
We call
F_{i}
the
i
-th projection of
F
.

For each
i^{\prime}\in I
and
X_{i^{\prime}}\in S_{i^{\prime}}
,
consider the object
q_{i^{\prime}}^{-1}X_{i^{\prime}}:=(\{X_{i}\},\{\varphi_{ij}\})

of
(S_{i})_{i\in I}
defined by

X_{i}=f_{ii^{\prime}}^{-1}X_{i^{\prime}}
for
i\geq i^{\prime}
and
X_{i}=\emptyset
else
and
\varphi_{ij}
is the natural isomorphism

f_{ji}^{-1}f_{ii^{\prime}}^{-1}X_{i^{\prime}}\overset{\sim}{\to}f_{ji^{\prime}}^{-1}X_{i^{\prime}}
if
i\geq i^{\prime}

and
\emptyset
else.
Then this functor
q_{i^{\prime}}^{-1}
defines a premorphism of sites

q_{i^{\prime}}\colon(S_{i})_{i\in I}\to S_{i^{\prime}}
.
We call
q_{i^{\prime}}
the
i^{\prime}
-th projection premorphism.
For
(F_{i})_{i\in I}\in\Set(S_{i})_{i\in I}
,
we have
q_{i^{\prime},\ast}(F_{i})_{i\in I}=F_{i^{\prime}}
.
Hence for
j^{\prime}\geq i^{\prime}
, the
\varphi
part of the data gives a natural transformation

(4.5.1)

\varphi_{ij}\colon q_{i^{\prime},\ast}\to f_{j^{\prime}i^{\prime},\ast}q_{j^{\prime},\ast}\colon\Set(S_{i})_{i\in I}\to\Set(S_{i^{\prime}}).

For
F_{i^{\prime}}\in\Set(S_{i^{\prime}})
,
the sheaf
q_{i^{\prime}}^{\ast\mathrm{set}}F_{i^{\prime}}=(F_{i})_{i\in I}
is given by

F_{i}=f_{ii^{\prime}}^{\ast}F_{i^{\prime}}
if
i\geq i^{\prime}
and
F_{i}=\emptyset
else.
In particular,
q_{i^{\prime}}
is a morphism of sites if
i^{\prime}
is the minimum element of
I
.
(But it is not if
i^{\prime}
is not the minimum
since
q_{i^{\prime}}^{\ast\mathrm{set}}
does not preserve the final object then,
and the minimum element might not exist.)
For
G_{i^{\prime}}\in\Ab(S_{i^{\prime}})
,
the abelian sheaf pullback
q_{i^{\prime}}^{\ast}G_{i^{\prime}}=(G_{i})_{i\in I}
is given by

G_{i}=f_{ii^{\prime}}^{\ast}G_{i^{\prime}}
if
i\geq i^{\prime}
and
G_{i}=0
else.
Hence
q_{i^{\prime}}^{\ast}
is exact for any
i^{\prime}
,
so
q_{i^{\prime},\ast}\colon\Ab(S_{i})_{i\in I}\to\Ab(S_{i^{\prime}})
sends
(K-)injectives to (K-)injectives and (K-)acyclics to (K-)acyclics.

Here we already used the description of
\Ab(S_{i})_{i\in I}

as the (abelian) category of tuples

(\{G_{i}\}_{i\in I},\{\varphi_{ij}\}_{i\leq j})
,
where
G_{i}\in\Ab(S_{i})
and

\varphi_{ij}\colon f_{ji}^{\ast}G_{i}\to G_{j}
is a morphism in
\Ab(S_{j})

satisfying the cocycle condition.
Similarly, the category of complexes
\mathrm{Ch}(S_{i})_{i\in I}
is the category of tuples

(G_{i}\in\mathrm{Ch}(S_{i}),f_{ji}^{\ast}G_{i}\to G_{j}\in\mathrm{Ch}(S_{j}))
.
We have a canonical morphism

\varphi_{ij}\colon f_{ji}^{\ast}G_{i}\to G_{j}
in
D(S_{j})
by (
4.5.1
)
or, equivalently,
\varphi_{ij}\colon G_{i}\to Rf_{ij,\ast}G_{j}
in
D(S_{i})
.
The cohomology sheaves of
G\in D(S_{i})_{i\in I}
in terms of tuples is given as follows.

Proposition 4.5.1
.

Let
G\in D(S_{i})_{i\in I}
,
G_{i}
and
\varphi_{ij}
be as above.
Then for any
q\in\mathbb{Z}
, the sheaf
H^{q}G\in\Ab(S_{i})_{i\in I}
is given by the tuple

(\{H^{q}G_{i}\},\{H^{q}\varphi_{ij}\})
,
where
H^{q}\varphi_{ij}
is viewed as
f_{ji}^{\ast}H^{q}G_{i}\to H^{q}G_{j}
,
or
H^{q}G_{i}\to f_{ji,\ast}H^{q}G_{j}
.

Proof.

Represent
G
by a K-injective complex and then take the
q
-th cohomology.
∎

As a special case, take
I=\{\bullet\leq\bullet\}
to be
the two-element set with one non-trivial relation.
Then
\{S_{i}\}_{i\in I}
corresponds to a single morphism of sites
f\colon T\to S
.
Denote the total site
(T\to S)
by
S_{c}

(where
c
stands for “compactified by
T
”).
Let
\pi_{S}\colon S\to C
be a morphism of sites to another site
C
.
We have the projection premorphisms

q_{S}\colon S_{c}\to S
and
q_{T}\colon S_{c}\to T
.
Set
\pi_{T}=\pi_{S}\mathbin{\circ}f\colon T\to C
,

\bar{\pi}_{S}=\pi_{S}\mathbin{\circ}q_{S}\colon S_{c}\to C

and
\bar{\pi}_{T}=\pi_{T}\mathbin{\circ}q_{T}\colon S_{c}\to C
.
From (
4.5.1
), we have a natural transformation

\bar{\pi}_{S,\ast}\to\bar{\pi}_{T,\ast}
.
Define

(4.5.2)

\bar{\pi}_{S,!}:=[\bar{\pi}_{S,\ast}\to\bar{\pi}_{T,\ast}][-1]\colon\mathrm{Ch}(S_{c})\to\mathrm{Ch}(C).

We have its right derived functor

(4.5.3)

R\bar{\pi}_{S,!}\colon D(S_{c})\to D(C).

We have a distinguished triangle

R\bar{\pi}_{S,!}G\to R\bar{\pi}_{S,\ast}G\to R\bar{\pi}_{T,\ast}G

in
D(C)
functorial in
G\in D(S_{c})
.
The functoriality of
\bar{\pi}_{S,!}
gives a canonical morphism

\bar{\pi}_{S,\ast}\sheafhom_{S_{c}}(G,H)\to\sheafhom_{C}(\bar{\pi}_{S,!}G,\bar{\pi}_{S,!}H)

in
\mathrm{Ch}(C)
functorial in
G,H\in\mathrm{Ch}(S_{c})
.
By Proposition
2.2.1
, this morphism induces a morphism

R\bar{\pi}_{S,\ast}R\sheafhom_{S_{c}}(G,H)\to R\sheafhom_{C}(R\bar{\pi}_{S,!}G,R\bar{\pi}_{S,!}H)

in
D(C)
functorial in
G,H\in D(S_{c})
.
Hence we have a canonical morphism

(4.5.4)

R\bar{\pi}_{S,\ast}G\mathbin{\otimes}^{L}R\bar{\pi}_{S,!}H\to R\bar{\pi}_{S,!}(G\mathbin{\otimes}^{L}H)

in
D(C)
functorial in
G,H\in D(S_{c})
.

We will see how the above is functorial in
T\to S
.
Let

\begin{CD}T^{\prime}@>{g_{T}}>{}>T\\
@V{f^{\prime}}V{}V@V{}V{f}V\\
S^{\prime}@>{g_{S}}>{}>S\end{CD}

be a commutative diagram of morphisms of sites.
Denote the total site
(T^{\prime}\to S^{\prime})
by
S^{\prime}_{c}
.
The diagram induces a morphism of sites
S_{c}^{\prime}\to S_{c}
,
which we denote by
g
.
Set
\pi_{S^{\prime}}=\pi_{S}\mathbin{\circ}g_{S}\colon S^{\prime}\to C
.
We can do the above constructions for

T^{\prime}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle f^{\prime}}}{{\to}}S^{\prime}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\pi_{S^{\prime}}}}{{\to}}C
,
resulting
q_{S^{\prime}}\colon S_{c}^{\prime}\to S^{\prime}
,
q_{T^{\prime}}\colon S_{c}^{\prime}\to T^{\prime}
,

\bar{\pi}_{S^{\prime}},\bar{\pi}_{T^{\prime}}\colon S_{c}^{\prime}\to C
and

\bar{\pi}_{S^{\prime},!}\colon\mathrm{Ch}(S_{c}^{\prime})\to\mathrm{Ch}(C)
.
We have
\bar{\pi}_{S,\ast}g_{\ast}\cong\bar{\pi}_{S^{\prime},\ast}
,
so
R\bar{\pi}_{S,\ast}Rg_{\ast}\cong R\bar{\pi}_{S^{\prime},\ast}
,
and we have a natural transformation

(4.5.5)

R\bar{\pi}_{S,\ast}\to R\bar{\pi}_{S^{\prime},\ast}g^{\ast}.

Also
\bar{\pi}_{S,!}g_{\ast}\cong\bar{\pi}_{S^{\prime},!}
,
so
R\bar{\pi}_{S,!}Rg_{\ast}\cong R\bar{\pi}_{S^{\prime},!}
,
and we have a natural transformation

(4.5.6)

R\bar{\pi}_{S,!}\to R\bar{\pi}_{S^{\prime},!}g^{\ast}.

The functors
Rg_{\ast}
and
g^{\ast}
are described as follows.

Proposition 4.5.2
.

(1)

Let
G^{\prime}\in D(S_{c}^{\prime})
.
Set
G^{\prime}_{S}=q_{S^{\prime},\ast}G^{\prime}
,
G^{\prime}_{T}=q_{T^{\prime},\ast}G^{\prime}

and
\varphi^{\prime}\colon G^{\prime}_{S}\to Rf^{\prime}_{\ast}G^{\prime}_{T}
the induced morphism.
Then for any
q\in\mathbb{Z}
, the sheaf
R^{q}g_{\ast}G^{\prime}
is given by the triple

(R^{q}g_{S,\ast}G^{\prime}_{S},R^{q}g_{T,\ast}G^{\prime}_{T},R^{q}g_{S,\ast}\varphi^{\prime}),

where we view the morphism
\varphi^{\prime}
as
f^{\prime\ast}G^{\prime}_{S}\to G^{\prime}_{T}
,
the morphism
Rg_{S,\ast}\varphi^{\prime}
as

f^{\ast}Rg_{S,\ast}G^{\prime}_{S}\to Rg_{T,\ast}G^{\prime}_{T}

and hence the morphism
R^{q}g_{S,\ast}\varphi^{\prime}
as

R^{q}g_{S,\ast}G^{\prime}_{S}\to f_{\ast}R^{q}g_{T,\ast}G^{\prime}_{T}
.

(2)

Let
G\in D(S_{c})
.
Set
G_{S}=q_{S,\ast}G
,
G_{T}=q_{T,\ast}G

and
\varphi\colon G_{S}\to Rf_{\ast}G_{T}
the induced morphism.
Then for any
q\in\mathbb{Z}
, the sheaf
H^{q}g^{\ast}G
is given by the triple

(g_{S}^{\ast}H^{q}G_{S},g_{T}^{\ast}H^{q}G_{T},g_{S}^{\ast}H^{q}\varphi),

where we view the morphism
\varphi
as
f^{\ast}G_{S}\to G_{T}
,
the morphism
g_{T}^{\ast}H^{q}\varphi
as

f^{\prime\ast}g_{S}^{\ast}H^{q}G_{S}\to g_{T}^{\ast}H^{q}G_{T}

and hence the morphism
g_{S}^{\ast}H^{q}\varphi
as

g_{S}^{\ast}H^{q}G_{S}\to f^{\prime}_{\ast}g_{T}^{\ast}H^{q}G_{T}
.

Proof.

This follows from Proposition
4.5.1
.
∎

The cup product is functorial:

Proposition 4.5.3
.

Let
G^{\prime},H^{\prime}\in D(S_{c}^{\prime})
.
Then the diagram

\begin{CD}R\bar{\pi}_{S,\ast}Rg_{\ast}G^{\prime}\mathbin{\otimes}^{L}R\bar{\pi}_{S,!}Rg_{\ast}H^{\prime}@>{}>{}>R\bar{\pi}_{S,!}Rg_{\ast}(G^{\prime}\mathbin{\otimes}^{L}H^{\prime})\\
\Big\|\Big\|\\
R\bar{\pi}_{S^{\prime},\ast}G^{\prime}\mathbin{\otimes}^{L}R\bar{\pi}_{S^{\prime},!}H^{\prime}@>{}>{}>R\bar{\pi}_{S^{\prime},\ast}(G^{\prime}\mathbin{\otimes}^{L}H^{\prime})\end{CD}

in
D(C)
is commutative,
where the upper horizontal morphism is the composite of
(
4.5.4
) for
T\to S\to C
and (
2.2.1
) for
g

and the lower horizontal morphism is (
4.5.4
) for
T^{\prime}\to S^{\prime}\to C
.

Proof.

It is enough to show that the similarly defined diagram

\begin{CD}R\bar{\pi}_{S,\ast}Rg_{\ast}R\sheafhom_{S_{c}^{\prime}}(G^{\prime},H^{\prime})@>{}>{}>R\sheafhom_{C}(R\bar{\pi}_{S,!}Rg_{\ast}G^{\prime},R\bar{\pi}_{S,!}Rg_{\ast}H^{\prime})\\
\Big\|\Big\|\\
R\bar{\pi}_{S^{\prime},\ast}R\sheafhom_{S_{c}^{\prime}}(G^{\prime},H^{\prime})@>{}>{}>R\sheafhom_{C}(R\bar{\pi}_{S^{\prime},!}G^{\prime},R\bar{\pi}_{S^{\prime},!}H^{\prime})\end{CD}

is commutative.
This reduces to the commutativity of the corresponding underived diagram,
which is easy.
∎

Proposition 4.5.4
.

Let
G,H\in D(S_{c})
.
Then the digram

\begin{CD}R\bar{\pi}_{S,\ast}G\mathbin{\otimes}^{L}R\bar{\pi}_{S,!}H@>{}>{}>R\bar{\pi}_{S,!}(G\mathbin{\otimes}^{L}H)\\
@V{}V{}V@V{}V{}V\\
R\bar{\pi}_{S^{\prime},\ast}g^{\ast}G\mathbin{\otimes}^{L}R\bar{\pi}_{S^{\prime},!}g^{\ast}H@>{}>{}>R\bar{\pi}_{S^{\prime},!}g^{\ast}(G\mathbin{\otimes}^{L}H)\end{CD}

is commutative,
where the upper horizontal morphism is (
4.5.4
) for
T\to S\to C

and the lower horizontal morphism is the composite of
(
4.5.4
) for
T^{\prime}\to S^{\prime}\to C
and the commutativity of
g^{\ast}
with
\mathbin{\otimes}^{L}
.

Proof.

This follows from Proposition
4.5.3
.
∎

Assume that
T
can be written as a disjoint union

T_{1}\sqcup\dots\sqcup T_{n}
of sites
T_{1},\dots,T_{n}
.
Write
f\colon T\to S
as a disjoint union of
f_{i}\colon T_{i}\to S
.
Set
\pi_{T_{i}}=\pi_{S}\mathbin{\circ}f_{i}\colon T_{i}\to C
.
Let
q_{T_{i}}\colon S_{c}\to T_{i}
be the premorphism of sites
defined by the inclusion functor on the underlying categories.
Set
\bar{\pi}_{T_{i}}=\pi_{T_{i}}\mathbin{\circ}q_{T_{i}}\colon S_{c}\to C
.
Then
\bar{\pi}_{T,\ast}\cong\bigoplus_{i}\bar{\pi}_{T_{i},\ast}
, and hence

\bar{\pi}_{S,!}\cong\left[\bar{\pi}_{S,\ast}\to\bigoplus_{i}\bar{\pi}_{T_{i},\ast}\right][-1].

Therefore we have a distinguished triangle

R\bar{\pi}_{S,!}G\to R\bar{\pi}_{S,\ast}G\to\bigoplus_{i}\bar{\pi}_{T_{i},\ast}G

in
D(C)
functorial in
G\in D(S_{c})
.

Now consider the setting of Section
4.4
,
so we have morphisms

\bigsqcup_{x\in Z}\mathbf{U}_{x,\mathrm{et}}^{h}\to\mathbf{U}_{\mathrm{et}}\to\Spec F^{\mathrm{perar}}_{\mathrm{et}}.

Denote the total site

(\bigsqcup_{x\in Z}\mathbf{U}_{x,\mathrm{et}}^{h}\to\mathbf{U}_{\mathrm{et}})

by
\mathbf{U}_{c,\mathrm{et}}
.
We will see how the formalism of this subsection is compatible with
that of the previous subsection.

We have a natural projection morphism
q_{\mathbf{U}}\colon\mathbf{U}_{c,\mathrm{et}}\to\mathbf{U}_{\mathrm{et}}
.
The morphisms
\pi_{\mathbf{U}}
and
\pi_{\mathbf{U}_{x}^{h}}
induce morphisms

\bar{\pi}_{\mathbf{U}},\bar{\pi}_{\mathbf{U}_{x}^{h}}\colon\Spec\mathbf{U}_{c,\mathrm{et}}\to\Spec F^{\mathrm{perar}}_{\mathrm{et}}.

We have a functor

\bar{\pi}_{\mathbf{U},!}=\left[\bar{\pi}_{\mathbf{U},\ast}\to\bigoplus_{x\in Z}\bar{\pi}_{\mathbf{U}_{x}^{h},\ast}\right][-1]\colon\mathrm{Ch}(\mathbf{U}_{c,\mathrm{et}})\to\mathrm{Ch}(F^{\mathrm{perar}}_{\mathrm{et}}).

We have a morphism

(4.5.7)

R\bar{\pi}_{\mathbf{U},\ast}G\mathbin{\otimes}^{L}R\bar{\pi}_{\mathbf{U},!}H\to R\bar{\pi}_{\mathbf{U},!}(G\mathbin{\otimes}^{L}H)

in
D(F^{\mathrm{perar}}_{\tau})
functorial in
G,H\in D(\mathbf{U}_{c,\mathrm{et}})
.

Proposition 4.5.5
.

Let
G,H\in D^{+}(\mathbf{U}_{\mathrm{et}})
.
Then

R\bar{\pi}_{\mathbf{U},\ast}q_{\mathbf{U}}^{\ast}G\cong R\pi_{\mathbf{U},\ast}G,\quad R\bar{\pi}_{\mathbf{U},!}q_{\mathbf{U}}^{\ast}G\cong R\pi_{\mathbf{U},!}G.

Via these isomorphisms, the morphism

R\bar{\pi}_{\mathbf{U},\ast}q_{\mathbf{U}}^{\ast}G\mathbin{\otimes}^{L}R\bar{\pi}_{\mathbf{U},!}q_{\mathbf{U}}^{\ast}H\to R\bar{\pi}_{\mathbf{U},!}q_{\mathbf{U}}^{\ast}(G\mathbin{\otimes}^{L}H)

defined by (
4.5.7
) agrees with the morphism (
4.3.6
).

Proof.

This follows from Propositions
4.4.3
and
4.4.6
.
∎

4.6.
More fibered sites

We consider the situation of the beginning of Section
4.5
,
so we have a fibered site over a finite poset
I
.
We take
I
to be the commutative diagram

\begin{CD}\bullet @<{}<{}<\bullet\\
@A{}A{}A@A{}A{}A\\
\bullet @<{}<{}<\bullet @<{}<{}<\bullet @<{}<{}<\bullet.\end{CD}

Let

(4.6.1)

\begin{CD}T_{3}@>{f_{32}}>{}>T_{2}\\
@V{f_{31}}V{}V@V{}V{f_{2}}V\\
T@>{}>{f_{01}}>T_{1}@>{}>{f_{1}}>S@>{}>{\pi_{S}}>C,\end{CD}

be our fibered site.
(One might want to denote
T
by
T_{0}
for notational consistency.)
Let
\pi_{T}\colon T\to C
,
\pi_{T_{i}}\colon T_{i}\to C
,

f\colon T\to S
,
f_{i}\colon T_{i}\to S
be the appropriate composite morphisms.
Let

\displaystyle S_{c}=(T\to S),

\displaystyle T_{\bullet}=(T_{1}\leftarrow T_{3}\to T_{2}),

\displaystyle T_{\bullet,c}=(T\to T_{1}\leftarrow T_{3}\to T_{2})

be the total sites.
The above commutative diagram induces morphisms

f_{\bullet}\colon T_{\bullet}\to S
and
\bar{f}_{\bullet}\colon T_{\bullet,c}\to S_{c}
.
Let
f_{0\bullet}\colon T\to T_{\bullet}
be the morphism defined by the functor sending

(X_{1},X_{2},X_{3},f_{31}^{-1}X_{1}\to X_{3}\leftarrow f_{32}^{-1}X_{2})

to
f_{01}^{-1}X_{1}
.
Then
T_{\bullet,c}\cong(T\to T_{\bullet})
.

Example 4.6.1
.

A toy example is the étale sites of the spectra of the
F
-algebra homomorphisms

\begin{CD}F((u))@<{}<{}<F[u^{\pm}]\\
@A{}A{}A@A{}A{}A\\
F((t))@<{}<{}<F[[t,u]]/(ut)@<{}<{}<F[[t]][u]/(ut)@<{}<{}<F.\end{CD}

This diagram is aimed at describing the cohomology of
\Spec F[[t]][u]/(ut)

with support on the closed subscheme
\Spec F[u]
(whose complement is
\Spec F((t))
)
in terms of the simpler pieces
\Spec F[u^{\pm}]
(which is global and regular) and

\Spec F[[t,u]]/(ut)
(which is local)
with gluing data
\Spec F((u))
.
Actual examples we will see in Sections
10.4
and
10.5

will be more involved
p
-adic versions of this diagram.

We want to describe
\pi_{S,\ast}
and

\bar{\pi}_{S,!}=[\bar{\pi}_{S,\ast}\to\bar{\pi}_{T,\ast}][-1]

in terms of
\pi_{T_{i}}
.
For
i=1,2,3
, we denote the composite of the natural projection
T_{\bullet}\to T_{i}

and
\pi_{T_{i}}\colon T_{i}\to C
simply by
\pi_{T_{i}}
unless confusion occurs.
Hence we have natural transformations

\pi_{T_{1},\ast}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\varphi_{13}}}{{\to}}\pi_{T_{3},\ast}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\varphi_{23}}}{{\leftarrow}}\pi_{T_{2},\ast}\colon\mathrm{Ch}(T_{\bullet})\to\mathrm{Ch}(C)

Define

(4.6.2)
