be a perfect field extension of
F
.
Let
K_{F^{\prime}}
be the fraction field of
\mathbf{A}(F^{\prime})
.
Let
P_{F^{\prime}}
be the set of height one prime ideals of
\mathbf{A}(F^{\prime})
.
We have a distinguished triangle

R\Gamma(\mathbf{X}(F^{\prime}),\mathfrak{T}_{n}(2))\to R\Gamma(K_{F^{\prime}},\Lambda_{n}(2))\to\bigoplus_{\mathfrak{p}^{\prime}\in P_{F^{\prime}}}R\Gamma_{\mathfrak{p}^{\prime}}(\mathbf{A}(F^{\prime})_{\mathfrak{p}^{\prime}}^{h},\mathfrak{T}_{n}(2))[1].

We have

R\Gamma_{\mathfrak{p}^{\prime}}(\mathbf{A}(F^{\prime})_{\mathfrak{p}^{\prime}}^{h},\mathfrak{T}_{n}(2))[1]\cong\begin{cases}R\Gamma(\kappa(\mathfrak{p}^{\prime}),\nu_{n}(1))[-2]&\text{if }\mathfrak{p}^{\prime}\mid p,\\
R\Gamma(\kappa(\mathfrak{p}^{\prime}),\Lambda_{n}(1))[-1]&\text{if }\mathfrak{p}^{\prime}\nmid p,\end{cases}

whose cohomologies are:

\kappa(\mathfrak{p}^{\prime})^{\times}/\kappa(\mathfrak{p}^{\prime})^{\times p^{n}}

in degree
2
;

H^{1}(F^{\prime}_{\mathfrak{p}^{\prime}},\Lambda_{n})
in degree
3
;
and zero in degrees
\geq 4
.
Notice that this cohomology in degree
3
sheafifies (in
F^{\prime}
) to zero.
Let
K_{F^{\prime}}^{sh}
be the fraction field of the strict henselization of
\mathbf{A}(F^{\prime})
.
It has cohomological dimension
2
by
[
Sai86
, Theorem (5.1)]
.
It follows that

H^{q}(K_{F^{\prime}},\Lambda_{n}(2))\cong\begin{cases}K_{2}(K_{F^{\prime}})/p^{n}K_{2}(K_{F^{\prime}})&\text{if }q=2,\\
H^{1}\bigl(\Gal(\overline{F^{\prime}}/F^{\prime}),K_{2}(K_{F^{\prime}}^{sh})/p^{n}K_{2}(K_{F^{\prime}}^{sh})\bigr)&\text{if }q=3,\\
0&\text{if }q\geq 4.\end{cases}

Notice again that this cohomology in degree
3
sheafifies to zero.
It follows that

R^{q}\pi_{\mathbf{X},\ast}\mathfrak{T}_{n}(2)=0
for
q\geq 4
.
It also yields an exact sequence

\displaystyle K_{2}(K_{F^{\prime}})/p^{n}K_{2}(K_{F^{\prime}})\to\bigoplus_{\mathfrak{p}^{\prime}\in P_{F^{\prime}}}\kappa(\mathfrak{p}^{\prime})^{\times}/\kappa(\mathfrak{p}^{\prime})^{\times p^{n}}

\displaystyle\quad\to H^{3}(\mathbf{X}(F^{\prime}),\mathfrak{T}_{n}(2))\to H^{1}\bigl(\Gal(\overline{F^{\prime}}/F^{\prime}),K_{2}(K_{F^{\prime}}^{sh})/p^{n}K_{2}(K_{F^{\prime}}^{sh})\bigr).

The first map is given by the tame symbol maps.
The localization sequence in K-theory
(
[
Wei13
, Chapter V, Proposition 9.2]
,

[
Sai87
, (0.3)]
) shows that the sum of the normalized valuation maps

\bigoplus_{\mathfrak{p}^{\prime}\in P_{F^{\prime}}}\kappa(\mathfrak{p}^{\prime})^{\times}/\kappa(\mathfrak{p}^{\prime})^{\times p^{n}}\to\Lambda_{n}

is zero on the image of
K_{2}(K_{F^{\prime}})/p^{n}K_{2}(K_{F^{\prime}})
.
This defines a map from the kernel of

H^{3}(\mathbf{X}(F^{\prime}),\mathfrak{T}_{n}(2))\to H^{1}\bigl(\Gal(\overline{F^{\prime}}/F^{\prime}),K_{2}(K_{F^{\prime}}^{sh})/p^{n}K_{2}(K_{F^{\prime}}^{sh})\bigr)

to
\Lambda_{n}
functorial in
F^{\prime}
.
After étale sheafification in
F^{\prime}
,
this defines the desired morphism (
10.1.3
).
∎

The summary of the above construction of the trace morphism (
10.1.3
) is that
if
F
is algebraically closed, then we have an exact sequence

K_{2}(K)/p^{n}K_{2}(K)\to\bigoplus_{\mathfrak{p}\in P}\kappa(\mathfrak{p})^{\times}/\kappa(\mathfrak{p})^{\times p^{n}}\to H^{3}(X,\mathfrak{T}_{n}(2))\to 0

and the map
H^{3}(X,\mathfrak{T}_{n}(2))\to\Lambda_{n}
is induced by the sum of the normalized valuation maps
on
\kappa(\mathfrak{p})^{\times}
.
By varying
F
and taking Galois actions into account, this also characterizes (
10.1.3
).

Proposition 10.1.4
.

(1)

We have

R^{q}\pi_{\mathbf{U}_{S},\ast}\mathfrak{T}_{n}(2)=R^{q}\pi_{\mathbf{U}_{S},!}\mathfrak{T}_{n}(2)=0

for
q\geq 4
.

(2)

The composite

\bigoplus_{\mathfrak{p}\in S}R^{2}\pi_{\mathbf{K}_{\mathfrak{p}}^{h},\ast}\Lambda_{n}(2)\to R^{3}\pi_{\mathbf{U},!}\mathfrak{T}_{n}(2)\to R^{3}\pi_{\mathbf{X},\ast}\mathfrak{T}_{n}(2)\to\Lambda_{n}

is equal to the composite

\bigoplus_{\mathfrak{p}\in S}R^{2}\pi_{\mathbf{K}_{\mathfrak{p}}^{h},\ast}\Lambda_{n}(2)\to\bigoplus_{\mathfrak{p}\in S}\mathfrak{R}_{F_{\mathfrak{p}}/F}\Lambda_{n}\to\Lambda_{n}

of the morphisms (
6.2.1
) (for factors with
\mathfrak{p}\mid p
)
and (
7.2.1
) (for factors with
\mathfrak{p}\nmid p
)
and the sum of the norm maps.

Proof.

(
1
)
This follows from the distinguished triangles

\displaystyle\bigoplus_{\mathfrak{p}\in S}R\pi_{\mathbf{A}_{\mathfrak{p}}^{h},!}\mathfrak{T}_{n}(2)\to R\pi_{\mathbf{X},\ast}\mathfrak{T}_{n}(2)\to R\pi_{\mathbf{U}_{S},\ast}\mathfrak{T}_{n}(2),

\displaystyle R\pi_{\mathbf{U}_{S},!}\mathfrak{T}_{n}(2)\to R\pi_{\mathbf{X},\ast}\mathfrak{T}_{n}(2)\to\bigoplus_{\mathfrak{p}\in S}R\pi_{\mathbf{A}_{\mathfrak{p}}^{h},\ast}\mathfrak{T}_{n}(2)

and Proposition
6.5.1
.

(
2
)
This follows from the construction.
∎

As a consequence, we have canonical morphisms

(10.1.4)

R\pi_{\mathbf{U}_{S},!}\mathfrak{T}_{n}(2)\to R\pi_{\mathbf{X},\ast}\mathfrak{T}_{n}(2)\to\Lambda_{n}[-3]\to\Lambda_{\infty}[-3].

Now we can state the duality for
A
over
\Spec F^{\mathrm{perar}}_{\mathrm{et}}
:

Proposition 10.1.5
.

Assume that
A
is complete and
take
\mathbf{A}=\hat{\mathbf{A}}
to be the canonical lifting system.
Let
n\geq 1
and
r,r^{\prime}\in\mathbb{Z}
with
r+r^{\prime}=2
.

(1)

The objects
R\pi_{\mathbf{U}_{S},\ast}\mathfrak{T}_{n}(r)
and

R\pi_{\mathbf{U}_{S},!}\mathfrak{T}_{n}(r)

belong to
\langle\mathcal{W}_{F}\rangle_{F^{\mathrm{perar}}_{\mathrm{et}}}
.

(2)

The morphism

R\pi_{\mathbf{U}_{S},\ast}\mathfrak{T}_{n}(r)\mathbin{\otimes}^{L}R\pi_{\mathbf{U}_{S},!}\mathfrak{T}_{n}(r^{\prime})\to R\pi_{\mathbf{U}_{S},!}\mathfrak{T}_{n}(2)\to\Lambda_{\infty}[-3]

in
D(F^{\mathrm{perar}}_{\mathrm{et}})
is a perfect pairing.

We will prove the proposition below.
The proof for the case where
n=1
,
\zeta_{p}\in A
and
U_{S}=\Spec A[1/p]
will finish
at the end of
10.5
.
The general case will be proved at the beginning of Section
10.6
.

10.2.
Invariance under completion

We continue the notation of Section
10.1
.
In particular,
A
is not necessarily complete
and
\mathbf{A}
is not necessarily
\hat{\mathbf{A}}
.
We first translate the duality setup to total sites of fibered sites.

Consider the total site

\mathbf{U}_{S,c,\mathrm{et}}=\left(\bigsqcup_{\mathfrak{p}\in S}\Spec\mathbf{K}_{\mathfrak{p},\mathrm{et}}^{h}\to\mathbf{U}_{S,\mathrm{et}}\right)

and the functors

\bar{\pi}_{\mathbf{U}_{S},\ast},\bar{\pi}_{\mathbf{U}_{S},!}\colon\mathrm{Ch}(\mathbf{U}_{S,c,\mathrm{et}})\to\mathrm{Ch}(F^{\mathrm{perar}}_{\mathrm{et}})

defined at the end of Section
4.5
,
where
\bar{\pi}_{\mathbf{U}_{S}}
factors as

\mathbf{U}_{S,c,\mathrm{et}}\to\mathbf{U}_{S,\mathrm{et}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\pi_{\mathbf{U}_{S}}}}{{\to}}\Spec F^{\mathrm{perar}}_{\mathrm{et}}

and we have

\bar{\pi}_{\mathbf{U}_{S},!}=\left[\bar{\pi}_{\mathbf{U}_{S},\ast}\to\bigoplus_{\mathfrak{p}\in S}\bar{\pi}_{\mathbf{K}_{\mathfrak{p}}^{h},\ast}\right][-1].

For any
n\geq 1
and
r,r^{\prime}\in\mathbb{Z}
, we have isomorphisms

R\pi_{\mathbf{U}_{S},\ast}\mathfrak{T}_{n}(r)\cong R\bar{\pi}_{\mathbf{U}_{S},\ast}\mathfrak{T}_{n}(r),\quad R\pi_{\mathbf{U}_{S},!}\mathfrak{T}_{n}(r)\cong R\bar{\pi}_{\mathbf{U}_{S},!}\mathfrak{T}_{n}(r)

by Proposition
4.5.5
.
The morphism

R\pi_{\mathbf{U}_{S},\ast}\mathfrak{T}_{n}(r)\mathbin{\otimes}^{L}R\pi_{\mathbf{U}_{S},!}\mathfrak{T}_{n}(r^{\prime})\to R\pi_{\mathbf{U}_{S},!}\mathfrak{T}_{n}(r+r^{\prime})

coming from (
10.1.1
) can be identified with the morphism

R\bar{\pi}_{\mathbf{U}_{S},\ast}\mathfrak{T}_{n}(r)\mathbin{\otimes}^{L}R\bar{\pi}_{\mathbf{U}_{S},!}\mathfrak{T}_{n}(r^{\prime})\to R\bar{\pi}_{\mathbf{U}_{S},!}\mathfrak{T}_{n}(r+r^{\prime})

coming from (
4.5.7
),
by Proposition
4.5.5
.

Next we provide a completion version of this setup.
Set
\hat{U}_{S}=U_{S}\times_{\Spec A}\Spec\hat{A}
.
For
F^{\prime}\in F^{\mathrm{perar}}
,
let
\hat{\mathbf{U}}_{S}(F^{\prime})=U_{S}\times_{\Spec A}\Spec\hat{\mathbf{A}}(F^{\prime})
.
Then we have an
F^{\mathrm{perar}}
-scheme
\hat{\mathbf{U}}_{S}
,
a site
\hat{\mathbf{U}}_{S,\mathrm{et}}
and a morphism of sites

\pi_{\hat{\mathbf{U}}_{S}}\colon\Spec\hat{\mathbf{U}}_{S,\mathrm{et}}\to\Spec F^{\mathrm{perar}}_{\mathrm{et}}

by applying the same procedure as
U
.
For
\mathfrak{p}\in S
,
let
\hat{A}_{\mathfrak{p}}
be the complete local ring of
\hat{A}
at
\mathfrak{p}\hat{A}

(which is different from the completion of
A_{\mathfrak{p}}^{h}

since the residue field of this
\hat{A}_{\mathfrak{p}}
is the completion of
\kappa(\mathfrak{p})
).
Let
\hat{K}_{\mathfrak{p}}
be the fraction field of
\hat{A}_{\mathfrak{p}}
.
For
F^{\prime}\in F^{\mathrm{perar}}
,
let
\hat{\mathbf{A}}_{\mathfrak{p}}(F^{\prime})
be the completion of

\hat{A}_{\mathfrak{p}}\mathbin{\otimes}_{\hat{A}}\hat{\mathbf{A}}(F^{\prime})
with respect to the ideal

\mathfrak{p}\hat{A}_{\mathfrak{p}}\mathbin{\otimes}_{\hat{A}}\hat{\mathbf{A}}(F^{\prime})
.
It is a lifting system for
\hat{A}_{\mathfrak{p}}

in the sense of Definition
5.1.1
.
Set

\hat{\mathbf{K}}_{\mathfrak{p}}(F^{\prime})=\hat{\mathbf{A}}_{\mathfrak{p}}(F^{\prime})\mathbin{\otimes}_{\hat{A}_{\mathfrak{p}}}\hat{K}_{\mathfrak{p}}
.
We have a natural morphism
\Spec\hat{\mathbf{K}}_{\mathfrak{p}}\to\hat{\mathbf{U}}_{S}

of
F^{\mathrm{perar}}
-schemes.
Hence we have morphisms of sites

\bigsqcup_{\mathfrak{p}\in S}\Spec\hat{\mathbf{K}}_{\mathfrak{p},\mathrm{et}}\to\hat{\mathbf{U}}_{S,\mathrm{et}}\to\Spec F^{\mathrm{perar}}_{\mathrm{et}}.

Applying the methods of Section
4.5
, we obtain the total site

\hat{\mathbf{U}}_{S,\hat{c},\mathrm{et}}=\left(\bigsqcup_{\mathfrak{p}\in S}\Spec\hat{\mathbf{K}}_{\mathfrak{p},\mathrm{et}}\to\hat{\mathbf{U}}_{S,\mathrm{et}}\right)

and the functors

\bar{\pi}_{\hat{\mathbf{U}}_{S},\ast},\bar{\pi}_{\hat{\mathbf{U}}_{S},\hat{!}}\colon\mathrm{Ch}(\hat{\mathbf{U}}_{S,\hat{c},\mathrm{et}})\to\mathrm{Ch}(F^{\mathrm{perar}}_{\mathrm{et}}),

where
\bar{\pi}_{\hat{\mathbf{U}}_{S}}
factors as

\hat{\mathbf{U}}_{S,\hat{c},\mathrm{et}}\to\hat{\mathbf{U}}_{S,\mathrm{et}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\pi_{\hat{\mathbf{U}}_{S}}}}{{\to}}\Spec F^{\mathrm{perar}}_{\mathrm{et}}

and we have

\bar{\pi}_{\hat{\mathbf{U}}_{S},\hat{!}}=\left[\bar{\pi}_{\hat{\mathbf{U}}_{S},\ast}\to\bigoplus_{\mathfrak{p}\in S}\bar{\pi}_{\hat{\mathbf{K}}_{\mathfrak{p}},\ast}\right][-1].

Now we compare the two versions.
We have a commutative diagram

\begin{CD}\bigsqcup_{\mathfrak{p}\in S}\Spec\hat{\mathbf{K}}_{\mathfrak{p},\mathrm{et}}@>{\bigsqcup g_{\mathfrak{p}}}>{}>\bigsqcup_{\mathfrak{p}\in S}\Spec\mathbf{K}_{\mathfrak{p},\mathrm{et}}^{h}\\
@V{}V{}V@V{}V{}V\\
\hat{\mathbf{U}}_{S,\hat{c},\mathrm{et}}@>{}>{g}>\mathbf{U}_{S,c,\mathrm{et}}\end{CD}

of morphisms of sites.
We have morphism
g^{\ast}\mathfrak{T}_{n}(r)\to\mathfrak{T}_{n}(r)

and
g_{\mathfrak{p}}^{\ast}\mathfrak{T}_{n}(r)\to\mathfrak{T}_{n}(r)
.
Hence (
4.5.5
) and (
4.5.6
) give morphisms

(10.2.1)

\displaystyle R\bar{\pi}_{\mathbf{U}_{S},\ast}\mathfrak{T}_{n}(r)\to R\bar{\pi}_{\hat{\mathbf{U}}_{S},\ast}\mathfrak{T}_{n}(r),

(10.2.2)

\displaystyle R\bar{\pi}_{\mathbf{U}_{S},!}\mathfrak{T}_{n}(r)\to R\bar{\pi}_{\hat{\mathbf{U}}_{S},\hat{!}}\mathfrak{T}_{n}(r).

By Proposition
4.5.4
, these morphisms fit in a commutative diagram

(10.2.3)

\begin{CD}R\bar{\pi}_{\mathbf{U}_{S},\ast}\mathfrak{T}_{n}(r)\mathbin{\otimes}^{L}R\bar{\pi}_{\mathbf{U}_{S},!}\mathfrak{T}_{n}(r^{\prime})@>{}>{}>R\bar{\pi}_{\mathbf{U}_{S},!}\mathfrak{T}_{n}(r+r^{\prime})\\
@V{}V{}V@V{}V{}V\\
R\bar{\pi}_{\hat{\mathbf{U}}_{S},\ast}\mathfrak{T}_{n}(r)\mathbin{\otimes}^{L}R\bar{\pi}_{\hat{\mathbf{U}}_{S},\hat{!}}\mathfrak{T}_{n}(r^{\prime})@>{}>{}>R\bar{\pi}_{\hat{\mathbf{U}}_{S},\hat{!}}\mathfrak{T}_{n}(r+r^{\prime}).\end{CD}

Proposition 10.2.1
.

Assume that
A
is complete and
\mathbf{A}=\hat{\mathbf{A}}
.
Then the morphisms (
10.2.1
) and (
10.2.2
) are isomorphisms.
In particular, the vertical morphisms in (
10.2.3
) are isomorphisms.

Proof.

The first morphism is obviously an isomorphism.
For the second isomorphism, it is enough to show that
for all
\mathfrak{p}\in S
, the morphism

R\pi_{\mathbf{K}_{\mathfrak{p}}^{h},\ast}\Lambda_{n}(r)\to R\pi_{\hat{\mathbf{K}}_{\mathfrak{p}},\ast}\Lambda_{n}(r)

is an isomorphism.
But this is Proposition
5.2.1
.
∎

In particular, under the assumptions of the proposition, we have a canonical morphism

R\bar{\pi}_{\hat{\mathbf{U}}_{S},\hat{!}}\mathfrak{T}_{n}(2)\to\Lambda_{n}[-3]

by (
10.1.4
).

Proposition 10.2.2
.

Assume that
A
is complete and
\mathbf{A}=\hat{\mathbf{A}}
.
Let
r+r^{\prime}=2
.
Then the morphism in Proposition
10.1.5
is a perfect pairing
if and only if the morphism

R\bar{\pi}_{\hat{\mathbf{U}}_{S},\ast}\mathfrak{T}_{n}(r)\mathbin{\otimes}^{L}R\bar{\pi}_{\hat{\mathbf{U}}_{S},\hat{!}}\mathfrak{T}_{n}(r^{\prime})\to R\bar{\pi}_{\hat{\mathbf{U}}_{S},\hat{!}}\mathfrak{T}_{n}(2)\to\Lambda_{n}[-3]

above is a perfect pairing.

Proof.

This follows from Proposition
10.2.1
.
∎

10.3.
Enough resolved case

Assume that
A
satisfies the conditions listed at the beginning of
Section
8
.
We use the notation of Section
8
,
particularly Section
8.3
.
Let
S\subset P
be the subset consisting of primes dividing
p
.
Set
R=A[1/p]
and
n=1
and let
U=U_{S}=\Spec R
.
We prove Proposition
10.1.5

in this case.
We only treat the case (
4b
)
as the case (
4a
) is similar and easier.
Therefore we assume (
4b
).
For the moment,
\mathbf{A}
is a general lifting system and not necessarily
\hat{\mathbf{A}}
.

For
\nu=\alpha
or
\beta
, set

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{R}=R\pi_{\mathbf{R},\ast}\Lambda,\quad\mathcal{E}_{R}^{\prime}=R\pi_{\mathbf{R},!}\Lambda,\quad\mathcal{E}_{K_{\nu}^{h}}=R\pi_{\mathbf{K}_{\nu}^{h},\ast}\Lambda,

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}_{R}=R\varepsilon_{\ast}\mathcal{E}_{R},\quad\mathcal{F}_{R}^{\prime}=R\varepsilon_{\ast}\mathcal{E}_{R}^{\prime},\quad\mathcal{F}_{K_{\nu}^{h}}=R\varepsilon_{\ast}\mathcal{E}_{K_{\nu}^{h}}.

The distinguished triangle (
10.1.2
) gives distinguished triangles

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{R}\to\bigoplus_{\nu=\alpha,\beta}\mathcal{E}_{K_{\nu}^{h}}\to\mathcal{E}_{R}^{\prime}[1],

(10.3.1)

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}_{R}\to\bigoplus_{\nu=\alpha,\beta}\mathcal{F}_{K_{\nu}^{h}}\to\mathcal{F}_{R}^{\prime}[1]

in
D(F^{\mathrm{perar}}_{\mathrm{et}})
,
D(F^{\mathrm{perar}}_{\mathrm{zar}})
, respectively.

Proposition 10.3.1
.

The distinguished triangle (
10.3.1
) induces an exact sequence

0\to H^{q}\mathcal{F}_{R}\to\bigoplus_{\nu=\alpha,\beta}H^{q}\mathcal{F}_{K_{\nu}^{h}}\to H^{q+1}\mathcal{F}_{R}^{\prime}\to 0

for all
q
.
We have

H^{q}\mathcal{F}_{R}=H^{q+1}\mathcal{F}_{R}^{\prime}=0

for
q\geq 4
.

Proof.

This follows from Propositions

8.1.1
,

8.1.4
,
and
8.3.4
.
∎

Define
(H^{1}\mathcal{F}_{R})_{\alpha}

to be the kernel of
the morphism from
H^{1}\mathcal{F}_{R}

to
H^{1}\mathcal{F}_{K_{\beta}^{h}}
.
Define
(H^{1}\mathcal{R})_{\beta}

to be the quotient of
H^{1}\mathcal{F}_{R}

by
(H^{1}\mathcal{F}_{R})_{\alpha}
.
Define
(H^{2}\mathcal{F}_{R})_{\beta}

to be the kernel of
the morphism from
H^{2}\mathcal{F}_{R}

to
H^{2}\mathcal{F}_{K_{\alpha}^{h}}
.
Define
(H^{2}\mathcal{F}_{R})_{\alpha}

to be the quotient of
H^{2}\mathcal{F}_{R}

by
(H^{2}\mathcal{F}_{R})_{\beta}
.

For
q=1,2
and
\nu=\alpha
or
\beta
,
define
(H^{q}\mathcal{F}_{R}^{\prime})_{\nu}
to be the cokernel of the morphism

(H^{q}\mathcal{F}_{R})_{\nu}\to H^{q}\mathcal{F}_{K_{\nu}^{h}}

For
m\geq 0

define
U^{m}(H^{q}\mathcal{F}_{R})_{\nu}

to be the subsheaf of
(H^{q}\mathcal{F}_{R})_{\nu}
given by

F^{\prime}\mapsto U^{m}H^{q}(\mathbf{R}(F^{\prime}),\Lambda)_{\nu},

where
U^{m}H^{q}(\mathbf{R}(F^{\prime}),\Lambda)_{\nu}
is as defined in
Section
8.2
.
Define
U^{m}(H^{q}\mathcal{F}_{R}^{\prime})_{\nu}
to be the image of

U^{m}H^{q}\mathcal{F}_{K_{\nu}^{h}}

in
(H^{q}\mathcal{F}_{R}^{\prime})_{\nu}
.

For
\nu=\alpha
or
\beta
and
F^{\prime}\in F^{\mathrm{perar}}
,
let
\mathbf{k}_{\nu}(F^{\prime})=\mathbf{A}(F^{\prime})\mathbin{\otimes}_{A}k_{\nu}

and
\bar{\mathbf{A}}_{\nu}(F^{\prime})=\mathbf{A}(F^{\prime})\mathbin{\otimes}_{A}\bar{A}_{\nu}
.
Let
\Omega_{\bar{\mathbf{A}}_{\nu}}^{1}=\bar{\mathbf{A}}_{\nu}\mathbin{\otimes}_{\bar{A}_{\nu}}\Omega_{\bar{A}_{\nu}}^{1}
.
Set
\nu^{\prime}=\alpha
if
\nu=\beta
and
\nu^{\prime}=\beta
if
\nu=\alpha
.
For
j\geq 1
, let
\tilde{\mathbf{U}}_{k_{\nu}}^{(j)}
be the image of

1+\varpi_{\nu^{\prime}}^{j}\bar{\mathbf{A}}_{\nu}
in

\mathbf{k}_{\nu}^{\times}/\mathbf{k}_{\nu}^{\times p}
.
Let
\tilde{\mathbf{A}}_{\alpha}(j)
be the image of

\varpi_{\nu^{\prime}}^{-j}\bar{\mathbf{A}}_{\nu}
in
\mathbf{k}_{\nu}/\wp\mathbf{k}_{\nu}
.

The isomorphisms in Propositions

8.3.1
and
8.3.2

are functorial in perfect field extensions of
F
.
Therefore we have the following:

Proposition 10.3.2
.

The image of

\gr^{m}(H^{q}\mathcal{F}_{R})_{\nu}\hookrightarrow\gr^{m}H^{q}\mathcal{F}_{K_{\nu}^{h}}

is given as follows:

\gr^{m}(H^{2}\mathcal{F}_{R})_{\alpha}\cong\begin{cases}\mathbf{k}_{\alpha}^{\times}/\mathbf{k}_{\alpha}^{\times p}&\text{if }m=0,\\
\bar{\mathbf{A}}_{\alpha}/\bar{\mathbf{A}}_{\alpha}^{p}&\text{if }0<m<f_{\alpha},\ p\mid m,\\
\mathbf{\Omega}_{\bar{A}_{\alpha}}^{1}&\text{if }0<m<f_{\alpha},\ p\nmid m,\\
\xi\oplus\tilde{\mathbf{A}}_{\alpha}(f_{\beta})&\text{if }m=f_{\alpha},\end{cases}

\gr^{m}(H^{1}\mathcal{F}_{R})_{\alpha}\cong\begin{cases}0\oplus\tilde{\mathbf{U}}_{k_{\alpha}}^{(f_{\beta}+1)}&\text{if }m=0,\\
\bar{\mathbf{A}}_{\alpha}/\bar{\mathbf{A}}_{\alpha}^{p}&\text{if }0<m<f_{\alpha},\ p\mid m,\\
\bar{\mathbf{A}}_{\alpha}&\text{if }0<m<f_{\alpha},\ p\nmid m,\\
0&\text{if }m=f_{\alpha},\end{cases}

\gr^{m}(H^{2}\mathcal{F}_{R})_{\beta}\cong\begin{cases}\tilde{\mathbf{U}}_{k_{\beta}}^{(f_{\alpha}+1)}&\text{if }m=0,\\
\bar{\mathbf{A}}_{\beta}/\bar{\mathbf{A}}_{\beta}^{p}&\text{if }0<m<f_{\beta},\ p\mid m,\\
\mathbf{\Omega}_{\bar{A}_{\beta}}^{1}&\text{if }0<m<f_{\beta},\ p\nmid m,\\
0&\text{if }m=f_{\beta},\end{cases}

\gr^{m}(H^{1}\mathcal{F}_{R})_{\beta}\cong\begin{cases}\Lambda\oplus\mathbf{k}_{\beta}^{\times}/\mathbf{k}_{\beta}^{\times p}&\text{if }m=0,\\
\bar{\mathbf{A}}_{\beta}/\bar{\mathbf{A}}_{\beta}^{p}&\text{if }0<m<f_{\beta},\ p\mid m,\\
\bar{\mathbf{A}}_{\beta}&\text{if }0<m<f_{\beta},\ p\nmid m,\\
\tilde{\mathbf{A}}_{\beta}(f_{\alpha})&\text{if }m=f_{\beta}.\end{cases}

Consider the morphism

\mathcal{F}_{R}\mathbin{\otimes}^{L}\mathcal{F}_{R}^{\prime}[1]\to R\varepsilon_{\ast}\Lambda[-2]\to\xi[-3].

For
q+q^{\prime}=3
, this gives a morphism

(10.3.2)

H^{q}\mathcal{F}_{R}\mathbin{\otimes}^{L}H^{q^{\prime}+1}\mathcal{F}_{R}^{\prime}\to\xi.

Proposition 10.3.3
.

The morphism (
10.3.2
)
is a perfect pairing if
q=0
or
3
.

Proof.

By Proposition
8.3.4
,
the pairing can be identified with the multiplication pairing

\Lambda\times\xi\to\xi
or
\xi\times\Lambda\to\xi
.
Hence the result follows from
Proposition
3.4.6
.
∎

Proposition 10.3.4
.

Assume
A
is complete and
\mathbf{A}=\hat{\mathbf{A}}
.
The morphism (
10.3.2
) is a perfect pairing if
q=1
or
2
.

Proof.

First note that
\mathbf{k}_{\nu}
for
\nu=\alpha
or
\beta
agrees with
the
F^{\mathrm{perar}}
-algebra (
5.1.1
) for
k_{\nu}

since
A
(and hence
k_{\nu}
) is complete and
\mathbf{A}=\hat{\mathbf{A}}
.

For
m\geq 0
,
q=1
or
2
and
\nu=\alpha
or
\beta
,
we have an exact sequence

(10.3.3)

0\to\gr^{m}(H^{q}\mathcal{F}_{R})_{\nu}\to\gr^{m}H^{q}\mathcal{F}_{K_{\nu}^{h}}\to\gr^{m}(H^{q+1}\mathcal{F}_{R}^{\prime})_{\nu}\to 0.

By Section
6.3

and Proposition
10.3.2
,
we know that all these sheaves are finite direct sums of copies of

\Lambda
,
\xi
and Tate vector groups.
Hence the values of the functor

\sheafext_{F^{\mathrm{perar}}_{\mathrm{zar}}}^{i}(\;\cdot\;,\xi)

on these sheaves are zero for
i\geq 1

by Propositions
3.4.5

and
3.4.6
.
Denote the functor
\sheafhom_{F^{\mathrm{perar}}_{\mathrm{zar}}}(\;\cdot\;,\xi)
by
(\;\cdot\;)^{\vee}
.
We thus have an exact sequence

0\to\bigl(\gr^{m}(H^{q+1}\mathcal{F}_{R}^{\prime})_{\nu}\bigr)^{\vee}\to\bigl(\gr^{m}H^{q}\mathcal{F}_{K_{\nu}^{h}}\bigr)^{\vee}\to\bigl(\gr^{m}(H^{q}\mathcal{F}_{R})_{\nu}\bigr)^{\vee}\to 0.

Let
m^{\prime}=f_{\nu}-m
.
Compare this sequence with the exact sequence

(10.3.4)

0\to\gr^{m^{\prime}}(H^{q^{\prime}}\mathcal{F}_{R})_{\nu}\to\gr^{m^{\prime}}H^{q^{\prime}}\mathcal{F}_{K_{\nu}^{h}}\to\gr^{m^{\prime}}(H^{q^{\prime}+1}\mathcal{F}_{R}^{\prime})_{\nu}\to 0.

Proposition
6.3.1

gives an isomorphism between the middle terms.

It is enough to show that this isomorphism between the middle terms extends
to an isomorphism of the sequences.
By Section
6.3

and Proposition
10.3.2
,
Write the sequence (
10.3.3
)
as
0\to C\to D\to E\to 0
and
the sequence (
10.3.4
)
as
0\to C^{\prime}\to D^{\prime}\to E^{\prime}\to 0
,
so that we have a pairing
D\times D^{\prime}\to\xi
inducing an isomorphism

D^{\prime}\overset{\sim}{\to}D^{\vee}
.
It is enough to show that
the kernel of the composite
D^{\prime}\overset{\sim}{\to}D^{\vee}\twoheadrightarrow C^{\vee}
is
C^{\prime}
.
Let
\nu^{\prime}=\alpha
if
\nu=\beta
and
\nu^{\prime}=\beta
if
\nu=\alpha
.
There are three cases to consider.

(1)

C\hookrightarrow D
is

\tilde{\mathbf{U}}_{k_{\nu}}^{(f_{\nu^{\prime}}+1)}\hookrightarrow\mathbf{k}_{\nu}^{\times}/\mathbf{k}_{\nu}^{\times p}
;

C^{\prime}\hookrightarrow D^{\prime}
is

\tilde{\mathbf{A}}_{\nu}(f_{\nu^{\prime}})\hookrightarrow\mathbf{k}_{\nu}/\wp\mathbf{k}_{\nu}
;
and
D\times D^{\prime}\to\xi
is
(x,y)\mapsto\Res(y\dlog x)
.

(2)

C\hookrightarrow D
is

\bar{\mathbf{A}}_{\nu}\hookrightarrow\mathbf{k}_{\nu}
;

C^{\prime}\hookrightarrow D^{\prime}
is

\Omega_{\bar{\mathbf{A}}_{\nu}}^{1}\hookrightarrow\Omega_{\mathbf{k}_{\nu}}^{1}
;
and
D\times D^{\prime}\to\xi
is
(x,\omega)\mapsto\Res(x\omega)
.

(3)

Both
C\hookrightarrow D
and
C^{\prime}\hookrightarrow D^{\prime}
are

\bar{\mathbf{A}}_{\nu}/\bar{\mathbf{A}}_{\nu}^{p}\hookrightarrow\mathbf{k}_{\nu}/\mathbf{k}_{\nu}^{p}
;
and
D\times D^{\prime}\to\xi
is
(x,y)\mapsto\Res(xdy)
.

In all these cases, it is straightforward to check by explicit calculations that
the kernel of the composite
D^{\prime}\overset{\sim}{\to}D^{\vee}\twoheadrightarrow C^{\vee}
is indeed
C^{\prime}
.
∎

Proposition 10.3.5
.

Assume
A
is complete and
\mathbf{A}=\hat{\mathbf{A}}
.
Then the statement of Proposition
10.1.5
is true
(under the assumptions of this subsection).
The object
R^{q}\pi_{\mathbf{R},\ast}\Lambda
is in
\mathcal{W}_{F}
for all
q

and zero for
q\geq 3
.
The image of the composite

R^{2}\pi_{\mathbf{R},\ast}\Lambda\to\bigoplus_{\mathfrak{q}\in P\setminus P^{\prime}}R^{2}\pi_{\mathbf{K}_{\mathfrak{q}}^{h},\ast}\Lambda\twoheadrightarrow\bigoplus_{\mathfrak{q}\in P\setminus P^{\prime}}\Lambda

of the natural morphism and the morphism (
6.2.1
) is
the subgroup
(\bigoplus_{\mathfrak{q}\in P\setminus P^{\prime}}\Lambda)_{0}
consisting of elements of zero sum.

Proof.

This follows from the previous propositions,
Propositions
3.4.4
,
8.2.4
and
8.3.3
.
∎

We record here the completion invariance of
R\bar{\pi}_{\mathbf{U}_{S},!}

without assuming the completeness of
A
.
(In this case, the completion invariance of the other one

R\bar{\pi}_{\mathbf{U}_{S},\ast}
does not hold.)
This is a result of explicit calculations of the graded pieces.
It will be used in Section
10.5
.

Proposition 10.3.6
.

The morphism (
10.2.2
) is an isomorphism (under the assumption of this subsection).

Proof.

This follows from
Proposition
8.3.5

(and Proposition
8.2.6

when in Case (
4a
)).
∎

10.4.
Localization over resolution of singularities

We return to general
A
,
\hat{A}
and
S
.
We recall some of the notation in
[
Sai86
, §1]

with some slight additions and modifications.
Let
\pi_{\mathfrak{X}/A}\colon\mathfrak{X}\to\Spec A
be a resolution of singularities
such that
Y\cup\overline{S}\subset\mathfrak{X}
is
supported on a strict normal crossing divisor,
where
Y
is the reduced part of
\mathfrak{X}\times_{A}F
(
[
Sta21
, Tag 0BIC]
).
Let
Y_{0}
(resp.
Y_{1}
) be the set of closed (resp. generic) points of
Y
.
For
\eta\in Y_{1}
, let
\eta_{0}=\overline{\{\eta\}}\cap Y_{0}

and
F_{\eta}
the constant field of
\overline{\{\eta\}}
.
For
x\in Y_{0}
, let
A_{x}^{h}
be the henselian local ring of
\mathfrak{X}
at
x

with maximal ideal
\mathfrak{m}_{A_{x}^{h}}
and residue field
F_{x}

and let
Y_{1}^{x}
be the set of height one primes of
A_{x}^{h}

lying over some element of
Y_{1}
(via the morphism
\Spec A_{x}^{h}\to\mathfrak{X}
).
For
\eta\in Y_{1}
and
x\in\eta_{0}
,
there is a unique
\eta_{x}\in Y_{1}^{x}
lying over
\eta
.
Let
K_{\eta_{x}}^{h}
be the henselian local field of
A_{x}^{h}
at
\eta_{x}
,

\mathcal{O}_{K_{\eta_{x}}}^{h}
its ring of integers
and
\kappa(\eta_{x})^{h}
its residue field.
For each
x\in Y_{0}
,
let
B_{x}^{h}=\mathcal{O}(\Spec A_{x}^{h}\times_{\mathfrak{X}}Y)
,

R_{x}^{h}=\mathcal{O}(\Spec A_{x}^{h}\times_{\mathfrak{X}}X)

and
R_{x,S}^{h}=\mathcal{O}(\Spec A_{x}^{h}\times_{\mathfrak{X}}U_{S})
.

We will take henselian neighborhoods of small enough affine opens
of irreducible components of
Y
.
For each
\eta\in Y_{1}
,
choose an affine open neighborhood
W_{\eta}\subset\mathfrak{X}
of
\eta

small enough so that:

•

W_{\eta}\cap\overline{\{\eta^{\prime}\}}=\emptyset

for any
\eta^{\prime}\in Y_{1}\setminus\{\eta\}
,

•

W_{\eta}
does not contain the specialization of any element of
S
in
Y
and

•

W_{\eta}\cap Y\subset W_{\eta}
is a principal divisor.

Set
T=Y\setminus\bigcup_{\eta\in Y_{1}}(W_{\eta}\cap Y)

and
B_{\eta,T}=\mathcal{O}(W_{\eta}\cap Y)
.
For each
\eta\in Y_{1}
,
define
A_{\eta,T}^{h}
to be the the henselization of the pair

(\mathcal{O}(W_{\eta}),\mathfrak{m}\mathcal{O}(W_{\eta}))
.
Write
\Spec R_{\eta,T}^{h}=\Spec A_{\eta,T}^{h}\setminus\Spec B_{\eta,T}
.
For any
\eta\in Y_{1}
and
x\in\eta_{0}
,
we have a canonical
A
-algebra homomorphism

A_{\eta,T}^{h}\to\mathcal{O}_{K_{\eta_{x}}}^{h}

inducing the natural inclusion map

B_{\eta,T}\hookrightarrow\kappa(\eta_{x})^{h}
on the quotients.

Now we make the above rings into
F^{\mathrm{perar}}
-algebras.
For any
F^{\prime}\in F^{\mathrm{perar}}
,
define
\bm{\mathfrak{X}}(F^{\prime})=\mathfrak{X}\times_{A}\mathbf{A}(F^{\prime})

and
\mathbf{Y}(F^{\prime})=Y\times_{F}F^{\prime}
.
For
x\in Y
, define
\mathbf{A}_{x}^{h}(F^{\prime})
to be the henselization of the pair

(A_{x}^{h}\mathbin{\otimes}_{A}\mathbf{A}(F^{\prime}),\mathfrak{m}_{A_{x}^{h}}\mathbin{\otimes}_{A}\mathbf{A}(F^{\prime}))
,
and set
\mathbf{R}_{x}^{h}(F^{\prime})=\mathbf{A}_{x}^{h}(F^{\prime})\mathbin{\otimes}_{A_{x}^{h}}R_{x}^{h}
,

\mathbf{R}_{x,S}^{h}(F^{\prime})=\mathbf{A}_{x}^{h}(F^{\prime})\mathbin{\otimes}_{A_{x}^{h}}R_{x,S}^{h}
,

\mathbf{B}_{x}^{h}(F^{\prime})=\mathbf{A}_{x}^{h}(F^{\prime})\mathbin{\otimes}_{A_{x}^{h}}B_{x}^{h}

and
\bm{\mathfrak{\kappa}}(x)^{h}(F^{\prime})=\mathbf{B}_{x}^{h}(F^{\prime})\mathbin{\otimes}_{B_{x}^{h}}\kappa(x)^{h}
.
For
\eta\in Y_{1}
,
define
\mathbf{A}_{\eta,T}^{h}(F^{\prime})
to be the henselization of the pair

(A_{\eta,T}^{h}\mathbin{\otimes}_{A}\mathbf{A}(F^{\prime}),\mathfrak{m}A_{\eta,T}^{h}\mathbin{\otimes}_{A}\mathbf{A}(F^{\prime}))
,
and set
\mathbf{R}_{\eta,T}^{h}(F^{\prime})=\mathbf{A}_{\eta,T}^{h}(F^{\prime})\mathbin{\otimes}_{A_{\eta,T}^{h}}R_{\eta,T}^{h}

and
\mathbf{B}_{\eta,T}(F^{\prime})=\mathbf{A}_{\eta,T}^{h}(F^{\prime})\mathbin{\otimes}_{A_{\eta,T}^{h}}B_{\eta,T}
.
For
x\in T
and
\eta_{x}\in Y_{1}^{x}
,
let
\mathbf{O}_{K_{\eta_{x}}}^{h}(F^{\prime})
be the henselization of the pair

(\mathcal{O}_{K_{\eta_{x}}}^{h}\mathbin{\otimes}_{A}\mathbf{A}(F^{\prime}),\mathfrak{m}\mathcal{O}_{K_{\eta_{x}}}^{h}\mathbin{\otimes}_{A}\mathbf{A}(F^{\prime}))
.
Let
\mathbf{K}_{\eta_{x}}^{h}(F^{\prime})=\mathbf{O}_{K_{\eta_{x}}}^{h}(F^{\prime})\mathbin{\otimes}_{\mathcal{O}_{K_{\eta_{x}}}^{h}}K_{\eta_{x}}^{h}

and
\bm{\mathfrak{\kappa}}(\eta_{x})^{h}(F^{\prime})=\mathbf{O}_{K_{\eta_{x}}}^{h}(F^{\prime})\mathbin{\otimes}_{\mathcal{O}_{K_{\eta_{x}}}^{h}}\kappa(\eta_{x})^{h}
.

We have a commutative diagram

(10.4.1)

\begin{CD}\displaystyle\bigsqcup_{\begin{subarray}{c}x\in T\\
\eta_{x}\in Y_{1}^{x}\end{subarray}}\Spec\mathbf{K}_{\eta_{x},\mathrm{et}}^{h}@>{}>{}>\displaystyle\bigsqcup_{\eta\in Y_{1}}\Spec\mathbf{R}_{\eta,T,\mathrm{et}}^{h}\\
@V{}V{}V@V{}V{}V\\
\displaystyle\bigsqcup_{\mathfrak{p}\in S}\Spec\mathbf{K}_{\mathfrak{p},\mathrm{et}}^{h}@>{}>{}>\displaystyle\bigsqcup_{x\in T}\Spec\mathbf{R}_{x,S,\mathrm{et}}^{h}@>{}>{}>\mathbf{U}_{S,\mathrm{et}}@>{}>{}>\Spec F^{\mathrm{perar}}_{\mathrm{et}}\end{CD}

of morphisms of sites.
Let

(10.4.2)

\displaystyle\mathbf{U}_{T,\mathrm{et}}=\left(\bigsqcup_{\eta\in Y_{1}}\Spec\mathbf{R}_{\eta,T,\mathrm{et}}^{h}\leftarrow\bigsqcup_{\begin{subarray}{c}x\in T\\
\eta_{x}\in Y_{1}^{x}\end{subarray}}\Spec\mathbf{K}_{\eta_{x},\mathrm{et}}^{h}\to\bigsqcup_{x\in T}\Spec\mathbf{R}_{x,S,\mathrm{et}}^{h}\right),

\displaystyle\mathbf{U}_{T,c,\mathrm{et}}=\left(\bigsqcup_{\mathfrak{p}\in S}\Spec\mathbf{K}_{\mathfrak{p},\mathrm{et}}^{h}\to\mathbf{U}_{T,\mathrm{et}}\right)

be the total sites.
Then we are in the situation of Section
4.6
.
Let
\pi_{\mathbf{U}_{T}/\mathbf{U}_{S}}\colon\mathbf{U}_{T,\mathrm{et}}\to\mathbf{U}_{S,\mathrm{et}}
and

\bar{\pi}_{\mathbf{U}_{T}/\mathbf{U}_{S}}\colon\mathbf{U}_{T,c,\mathrm{et}}\to\mathbf{U}_{S,c,\mathrm{et}}

be the natural morphisms.
Let

(10.4.3)

\begin{gathered}\pi_{\mathbf{U}_{T},\ast}=\left[\bigoplus_{x\in T}\pi_{\mathbf{R}_{x,S}^{h},\ast}\oplus\bigoplus_{\eta\in Y_{1}}\pi_{\mathbf{R}_{\eta,T}^{h},\ast}\to\bigoplus_{\begin{subarray}{c}x\in T\\
\eta_{x}\in Y_{1}^{x}\end{subarray}}\pi_{\mathbf{K}_{\eta_{x}}^{h},\ast}\right][-1]\colon\\
\mathrm{Ch}(\mathbf{U}_{T,\mathrm{et}})\to\mathrm{Ch}(F^{\mathrm{perar}}_{\mathrm{et}}),\end{gathered}

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \bar{\pi}_{\mathbf{U}_{T},!}&=\left[\bar{\pi}_{\mathbf{U}_{T},\ast}\to\bigoplus_{\mathfrak{p}\in S}\bar{\pi}_{\mathbf{K}_{\mathfrak{p}}^{h},\ast}\right][-1]\\
&\cong\left[\bigoplus_{x\in T}\bar{\pi}_{\mathbf{R}_{x,S}^{h},\ast}\oplus\bigoplus_{\eta\in Y_{1}}\bar{\pi}_{\mathbf{R}_{\eta,T}^{h},\ast}\to\bigoplus_{\begin{subarray}{c}x\in T\\
\eta_{x}\in Y_{1}^{x}\end{subarray}}\bar{\pi}_{\mathbf{K}_{\eta_{x}}^{h},\ast}\oplus\bigoplus_{\mathfrak{p}\in S}\bar{\pi}_{\mathbf{K}_{\mathfrak{p}}^{h},\ast}\right][-1]\colon\end{aligned}

\displaystyle\mathrm{Ch}(\mathbf{U}_{T,c,\mathrm{et}})\to\mathrm{Ch}(F^{\mathrm{perar}}_{\mathrm{et}})

be the functors as defined in (
4.6.2
) and (
4.6.7
),
where the functors pulled back from
D(\mathbf{U}_{T,\mathrm{et}})
to
D(\mathbf{U}_{T,c,\mathrm{et}})

are denoted by putting overlines.

We compare these functors with the previously defined functors

\pi_{\mathbf{U}_{S},\ast}
and
\pi_{\mathbf{U}_{S},!}
.
By (
4.6.3
) and (
4.6.8
), we have natural transformations

\displaystyle\pi_{\mathbf{U}_{S},\ast}\to\pi_{\mathbf{U}_{T},\ast}\pi_{\mathbf{U}_{T}/\mathbf{U}_{S}}^{\ast}\colon\mathrm{Ch}(\mathbf{U}_{S,\mathrm{et}})\to\mathrm{Ch}(F^{\mathrm{perar}}_{\mathrm{et}}),

\displaystyle\bar{\pi}_{\mathbf{U}_{S},!}\to\bar{\pi}_{\mathbf{U}_{T},!}\bar{\pi}_{\mathbf{U}_{T}/\mathbf{U}_{S}}^{\ast}.\colon\mathrm{Ch}(\mathbf{U}_{S,c,\mathrm{et}})\to\mathrm{Ch}(F^{\mathrm{perar}}_{c,\mathrm{et}}).

Proposition 10.4.1
.

Let
G\in D_{\mathrm{tor}}^{+}(\mathbf{U}_{\mathrm{et}})
.
Then the above morphisms applied to
G
,

(10.4.4)

\displaystyle R\pi_{\mathbf{U}_{S},\ast}G\to R\pi_{\mathbf{U}_{T},\ast}\pi_{\mathbf{U}_{T}/\mathbf{U}_{S}}^{\ast}G,

(10.4.5)

\displaystyle R\bar{\pi}_{\mathbf{U}_{S},!}G\to R\bar{\pi}_{\mathbf{U}_{T},!}\bar{\pi}_{\mathbf{U}_{T}/\mathbf{U}_{S}}^{\ast}G,

in
D(F^{\mathrm{perar}}_{\mathrm{et}})
are isomorphisms.

Proof.

We have commutative diagrams of morphisms of sites

\begin{CD}\displaystyle\bigsqcup_{\begin{subarray}{c}x\in T\\
\eta_{x}\in Y_{1}^{x}\end{subarray}}\Spec\mathbf{K}_{\eta_{x},\mathrm{et}}^{h}@>{}>{}>\displaystyle\bigsqcup_{\eta\in Y_{1}}\Spec\mathbf{R}_{\eta,T,\mathrm{et}}^{h}\\
@V{}V{}V@V{}V{}V\\
\displaystyle\bigsqcup_{x\in T}\Spec\mathbf{R}_{x,\mathrm{et}}^{h}@>{}>{}>\mathbf{X}_{\mathrm{et}},\end{CD}

\begin{CD}\displaystyle\bigsqcup_{\begin{subarray}{c}x\in T\\
\eta_{x}\in Y_{1}^{x}\end{subarray}}\Spec\mathbf{O}_{K_{\eta_{x}},\mathrm{et}}^{h}@>{}>{}>\displaystyle\bigsqcup_{\eta\in Y_{1}}\Spec\mathbf{A}_{\eta,T,\mathrm{et}}^{h}\\
@V{}V{}V@V{}V{}V\\
\displaystyle\bigsqcup_{x\in T}\Spec\mathbf{A}_{x,\mathrm{et}}^{h}@>{}>{}>\bm{\mathfrak{X}}_{\mathrm{et}},\end{CD}

\begin{CD}\displaystyle\bigsqcup_{\begin{subarray}{c}x\in T\\
\eta_{x}\in Y_{1}^{x}\end{subarray}}\Spec\bm{\mathfrak{\kappa}}(\eta_{x})_{\mathrm{et}}^{h}@>{}>{}>\displaystyle\bigsqcup_{\eta\in Y_{1}}\Spec\mathbf{B}_{\eta,T,\mathrm{et}}\\
@V{}V{}V@V{}V{}V\\
\displaystyle\bigsqcup_{x\in T}\Spec\mathbf{B}_{x,\mathrm{et}}^{h}@>{}>{}>\mathbf{Y}_{\mathrm{et}}.\end{CD}

In the definitions of
\mathbf{U}_{T,\mathrm{et}}
and
\pi_{\mathbf{U}_{T},\ast}

in (
10.4.2
) and (
10.4.3
),
using these squares instead of the square in (
10.4.1
),
we define sites and functors

\displaystyle\mathbf{X}_{T,\mathrm{et}},\bm{\mathfrak{X}}_{T,\mathrm{et}},\mathbf{Y}_{T,\mathrm{et}},

\displaystyle\pi_{\mathbf{X}_{T},\ast}\colon\mathrm{Ch}(\mathbf{X}_{T,\mathrm{et}})\to\mathrm{Ch}(F^{\mathrm{perar}}_{\mathrm{et}}),

\displaystyle\pi_{\bm{\mathfrak{X}}_{T},\ast}\colon\mathrm{Ch}(\bm{\mathfrak{X}}_{T,\mathrm{et}})\to\mathrm{Ch}(F^{\mathrm{perar}}_{\mathrm{et}}),

\displaystyle\pi_{\mathbf{Y}_{T},\ast}\colon\mathrm{Ch}(\mathbf{Y}_{T,\mathrm{et}})\to\mathrm{Ch}(F^{\mathrm{perar}}_{\mathrm{et}}).

We have a commutative diagram of morphisms of sites

\begin{CD}\mathbf{U}_{T,\mathrm{et}}@>{\lambda_{T}}>{}>\mathbf{X}_{T,\mathrm{et}}@>{j_{\bm{\mathfrak{X}}_{T}}}>{}>\bm{\mathfrak{X}}_{T,\mathrm{et}}@<{i_{\bm{\mathfrak{X}}_{T}}}<{}<\mathbf{Y}_{T,\mathrm{et}}\\
@V{\pi_{\mathbf{U}_{T}/\mathbf{U}_{S}}}V{}V@V{\pi_{\mathbf{X}_{T}/\mathbf{X}}}V{}V@V{\pi_{\bm{\mathfrak{X}}_{T}/\bm{\mathfrak{X}}}}V{}V@V{\pi_{\mathbf{Y}_{T}/\mathbf{Y}}}V{}V\\
\mathbf{U}_{S,\mathrm{et}}@>{}>{\lambda_{S}}>\mathbf{X}_{\mathrm{et}}@>{}>{j_{\bm{\mathfrak{X}}}}>\bm{\mathfrak{X}}_{\mathrm{et}}@<{}<{i_{\bm{\mathfrak{X}}}}<\mathbf{Y}_{\mathrm{et}}\end{CD}

Let

\displaystyle\Spec\mathbf{R}_{x,\mathrm{et}}^{h}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle j_{x}}}{{\to}}\Spec\mathbf{A}_{x,\mathrm{et}}^{h}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle i_{x}}}{{\leftarrow}}\Spec\mathbf{B}_{x,\mathrm{et}}^{h},

\displaystyle\Spec\mathbf{R}_{\eta,T,\mathrm{et}}^{h}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle j_{\eta}}}{{\to}}\Spec\mathbf{A}_{\eta,T,\mathrm{et}}^{h}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle i_{\eta}}}{{\leftarrow}}\Spec\mathbf{B}_{\eta,T,\mathrm{et}}^{h},

\displaystyle\Spec\mathbf{K}_{\eta_{x},\mathrm{et}}^{h}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle j_{\eta_{x}}}}{{\to}}\Spec\Spec\mathbf{O}_{K_{\eta_{x}},\mathrm{et}}^{h}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle i_{\eta_{x}}}}{{\leftarrow}}\Spec\bm{\mathfrak{\kappa}}(x)_{\mathrm{et}}^{h}

be the components of
j_{\bm{\mathfrak{X}}_{T}}
and
i_{\bm{\mathfrak{X}}_{T}}
.

Then for any
G\in D^{+}(\mathbf{Y}_{\mathrm{et}})
,
the natural morphism

(10.4.6)

R\pi_{\mathbf{Y},\ast}G\to R\pi_{\mathbf{Y}_{T},\ast}\pi_{\mathbf{Y}_{T}/\mathbf{Y}}^{\ast}G

in
D(F^{\mathrm{perar}}_{\mathrm{et}})
is an isomorphism by excision
(see the proof of Proposition
4.4.1
).

Consider the commutative diagram

\begin{CD}\bm{\mathfrak{X}}_{\mathrm{et}}@<{i_{\bm{\mathfrak{X}}}}<{}<\mathbf{Y}_{\mathrm{et}}\\
@V{\pi_{\bm{\mathfrak{X}}/\mathbf{A}}}V{}V@V{\pi_{\mathbf{Y}}}V{}V\\
\Spec\mathbf{A}_{\mathrm{et}}@<{i_{\mathbf{A}}}<{}<\Spec F^{\mathrm{perar}}_{\mathrm{et}}.\end{CD}

Since
\bm{\mathfrak{X}}(F^{\prime})\to\Spec\mathbf{A}(F^{\prime})
is proper for any
F^{\prime}\in F^{\mathrm{perar}}
,
we have

R\pi_{\bm{\mathfrak{X}},\ast}G\overset{\sim}{\to}R\pi_{\mathbf{Y},\ast}i_{\bm{\mathfrak{X}}}^{\ast}G

for any
G\in D_{\mathrm{tor}}^{+}(\bm{\mathfrak{X}}_{\mathrm{et}})

by the proper base change theorem.

For any
G_{x}\in D(\mathbf{A}_{x,\mathrm{et}}^{h})
,

G_{\eta}\in D_{\mathrm{tor}}^{+}(\mathbf{A}_{\eta,T,\mathrm{et}}^{h})
and

G_{\eta_{x}}\in D(\mathbf{O}_{K_{\eta_{x}},\mathrm{et}}^{h})
,
the natural morphisms

\displaystyle R\pi_{\mathbf{A}_{x}^{h},\ast}G_{x}\to R\pi_{\mathbf{B}_{x}^{h},\ast}i_{x}^{\ast}G_{x},

\displaystyle R\pi_{\mathbf{A}_{\eta,T}^{h},\ast}G_{\eta}\to R\pi_{\mathbf{B}_{\eta,T}^{h},\ast}i_{\eta}^{\ast}G_{\eta},

\displaystyle R\pi_{\mathbf{O}_{K_{\eta_{x}}}^{h},\ast}G_{\eta_{x}}\to R\pi_{\bm{\mathfrak{\kappa}}(\eta_{x})^{h},\ast}i_{\eta_{x}}^{\ast}G_{\eta_{x}}

are isomorphisms by Propositions
4.2.3
,
4.2.4
and
4.2.3
, respectively.

Therefore for any
G\in D_{\mathrm{tor}}^{+}(\bm{\mathfrak{X}}_{\mathrm{et}})
,
the isomorphism (
10.4.6
) applied to
i_{\bm{\mathfrak{X}}}^{\ast}G

shows that the morphism

R\pi_{\bm{\mathfrak{X}},\ast}G\to R\pi_{\bm{\mathfrak{X}}_{T},\ast}\pi_{\bm{\mathfrak{X}}_{T}/\bm{\mathfrak{X}}}^{\ast}G

is an isomorphism.
For any
G\in D_{\mathrm{tor}}^{+}(\mathbf{U}_{S,\mathrm{et}})
,
applying this to
Rj_{\mathfrak{X},\ast}R\lambda_{S,\ast}G
,
we obtain the desired isomorphism (
10.4.4
).
The isomorphism (
10.4.5
) follows from this by the diagram (
4.6.9
).
∎

For any
G,H\in D(\mathbf{U}_{T,c,\mathrm{et}})
, we have a canonical morphism

R\bar{\pi}_{\mathbf{U}_{T},!}G\mathbin{\otimes}^{L}R\bar{\pi}_{\mathbf{U}_{T},\ast}H\to R\bar{\pi}_{\mathbf{U}_{T},!}(G\mathbin{\otimes}^{L}H)

by (
4.6.11
).
This morphism and the morphism (
10.1.1
) fit in a commutative diagram

\begin{CD}R\bar{\pi}_{\mathbf{U},!}G\mathbin{\otimes}^{L}R\bar{\pi}_{\mathbf{U},\ast}H@>{}>{}>R\bar{\pi}_{\mathbf{U}_{S},!}(G\mathbin{\otimes}^{L}H)\\
@V{}V{}V@V{}V{}V\\
R\bar{\pi}_{\mathbf{U}_{T},!}\bar{\pi}_{\mathbf{U}_{T}/\mathbf{U}_{S}}^{\ast}G\mathbin{\otimes}^{L}R\bar{\pi}_{\mathbf{U}_{T},\ast}\bar{\pi}_{\mathbf{U}_{T}/\mathbf{U}_{S}}^{\ast}H@>{}>{}>R\bar{\pi}_{\mathbf{U}_{T},!}\bar{\pi}_{\mathbf{U}_{T}/\mathbf{U}_{S}}^{\ast}(G\mathbin{\otimes}^{L}H)\end{CD}

by Proposition
4.6.3
.
With Proposition
10.4.1
, we have

\begin{CD}R\bar{\pi}_{\mathbf{U}_{S},!}\mathfrak{T}_{n}(r)\mathbin{\otimes}^{L}R\bar{\pi}_{\mathbf{U}_{S},\ast}\mathfrak{T}_{n}(r^{\prime})@>{}>{}>R\bar{\pi}_{\mathbf{U}_{S},!}\mathfrak{T}_{n}(r+r^{\prime})\\
\Big\|\Big\|\\
R\bar{\pi}_{\mathbf{U}_{T},!}\mathfrak{T}_{n}(r)\mathbin{\otimes}^{L}R\bar{\pi}_{\mathbf{U}_{T},\ast}\mathfrak{T}_{n}(r^{\prime})@>{}>{}>R\bar{\pi}_{\mathbf{U}_{T},!}\mathfrak{T}_{n}(r+r^{\prime})\end{CD}

for
n\geq 1
and
r,r^{\prime}\in\mathbb{Z}
.
The morphism (
10.1.4
) gives a canonical morphism

R\bar{\pi}_{\mathbf{U}_{S},\ast}\mathfrak{T}_{n}(2)\cong R\bar{\pi}_{\mathbf{U}_{T},!}\mathfrak{T}_{n}(2)\to\Lambda[-3].

The digram (
4.6.10
) gives a morphism of distinguished triangles

(10.4.7)

\begin{CD}R\bar{\pi}_{\mathbf{U}_{S},!}\mathfrak{T}_{n}(r)@>{}>{}>R\bar{\pi}_{\mathbf{U}_{S},\ast}\mathfrak{T}_{n}(r)@>{}>{}>\displaystyle\bigoplus_{\mathfrak{p}\in S}R\bar{\pi}_{\mathbf{K}_{\mathfrak{p}}^{h},\ast}\mathfrak{T}_{n}(r)\\
\Big\|@V{}V{}V@V{}V{}V\\
R\bar{\pi}_{\mathbf{U}_{T},!}\mathfrak{T}_{n}(r)@>{}>{}>\begin{array}[]{c}\bigoplus_{x\in T}R\bar{\pi}_{\mathbf{R}_{x,S}^{h},\ast}\mathfrak{T}_{n}(r)\\
\oplus\bigoplus_{\eta\in Y_{1}}R\bar{\pi}_{\mathbf{R}_{\eta,T}^{h},\ast}\mathfrak{T}_{n}(r)\end{array}@>{}>{}>\begin{array}[]{c}\bigoplus_{\begin{subarray}{c}x\in T\\
\eta_{x}\in Y_{1}^{x}\end{subarray}}R\bar{\pi}_{\mathbf{K}_{\eta_{x}}^{h},\ast}\mathfrak{T}_{n}(r)\\
\oplus\bigoplus_{\mathfrak{p}\in S}R\bar{\pi}_{\mathbf{K}_{\mathfrak{p}}^{h},\ast}\mathfrak{T}_{n}(r).\end{array}\end{CD}

10.5.
Completions

We continue the assumption and notation from the previous subsection.
We make the completion version of the constructions of the previous section
and compare these two versions.

For each
\eta\in Y_{1}
,
let
\hat{A}_{\eta,T}
be the
\mathfrak{m}A_{\eta,T}^{h}
-adic completion of
A_{\eta,T}^{h}

and set
\hat{R}_{\eta,T}=\hat{A}_{\eta,T}\mathbin{\otimes}_{A_{\eta,T}^{h}}R_{\eta,T}^{h}
.
For each
x\in Y_{0}
,
let
\hat{A}_{x}
be the completion of the local ring
A_{x}^{h}

and set
\hat{B}_{x}=\hat{A}_{x}\mathbin{\otimes}_{A_{x}^{h}}B_{x}^{h}

and
\hat{R}_{x,S}=\hat{A}_{x}\mathbin{\otimes}_{A_{x}^{h}}R_{x,S}^{h}
.
For each
\eta\in Y_{1}
and
x\in\eta_{0}
,
let
\hat{\kappa}(\eta_{x})
be the completion of the local ring
\kappa(\eta_{x})^{h}
.
Let
\hat{\mathcal{O}}_{K_{\eta_{x}}}
be the canonical lifting of
\hat{\kappa}(\eta_{x})

to
\mathcal{O}_{K_{\eta_{x}}}^{h}

and set
\hat{K}_{\eta_{x}}=\hat{\mathcal{O}}_{K_{\eta_{x}}}[1/p]
.
For
F^{\prime}\in F^{\mathrm{perar}}
,
define
\hat{\mathbf{A}}_{x}(F^{\prime})
,
\hat{\mathbf{R}}_{\eta,T}(F^{\prime})
and so on
using completions instead of henselizations.
We have morphisms of
F^{\mathrm{perar}}
-algebras

\hat{\mathbf{R}}_{T,\eta}\to\hat{\mathbf{K}}_{\eta_{x}}

for
x\in T
and
\eta=\eta_{x}\in Y_{1}^{x}

and
\hat{\mathbf{R}}_{x,S}\to\hat{\mathbf{K}}_{\eta_{x}}\times\hat{\mathbf{K}}_{\mathfrak{p}}

for
x\in T
and
\mathfrak{p}\in S^{x}
.

We have a commutative diagram of morphisms of sites

\begin{CD}\displaystyle\bigsqcup_{\begin{subarray}{c}x\in T\\
\eta_{x}\in Y_{1}^{x}\end{subarray}}\Spec\hat{\mathbf{K}}_{\eta_{x},\mathrm{et}}@>{}>{}>\displaystyle\bigsqcup_{\eta\in Y_{1}}\Spec\hat{\mathbf{R}}_{\eta,T,\mathrm{et}}\\
@V{}V{}V@V{}V{}V\\
\displaystyle\bigsqcup_{\mathfrak{p}\in S}\Spec\hat{\mathbf{K}}_{\mathfrak{p},\mathrm{et}}@>{}>{}>\displaystyle\bigsqcup_{x\in T}\Spec\hat{\mathbf{R}}_{x,S,\mathrm{et}}@>{}>{}>\hat{\mathbf{U}}_{S,\mathrm{et}}@>{}>{}>\Spec F^{\mathrm{perar}}_{\mathrm{et}},\end{CD}

and a morphism of fibered sites from this diagram to (
10.4.1
).
Let

\displaystyle\hat{\mathbf{U}}_{\hat{T},\mathrm{et}}=\left(\bigsqcup_{\eta\in Y_{1}}\Spec\hat{\mathbf{R}}_{\eta,T,\mathrm{et}}\leftarrow\bigsqcup_{\begin{subarray}{c}x\in T\\
\eta_{x}\in Y_{1}^{x}\end{subarray}}\Spec\hat{\mathbf{K}}_{\eta_{x},\mathrm{et}}\to\bigsqcup_{x\in T}\Spec\hat{\mathbf{R}}_{x,S,\mathrm{et}}\right),

\displaystyle\hat{\mathbf{U}}_{\hat{T},\hat{c},\mathrm{et}}=\left(\bigsqcup_{\mathfrak{p}\in S}\Spec\hat{\mathbf{K}}_{\mathfrak{p},\mathrm{et}}\to\hat{\mathbf{U}}_{\hat{T},\mathrm{et}}\right)

be the total sites.
Then we are in the situation of Section
4.6
.
Let

\displaystyle\pi_{\hat{\mathbf{U}}_{\hat{T}}/\mathbf{U}_{T}}\colon\hat{\mathbf{U}}_{\hat{T},\mathrm{et}}\to\mathbf{U}_{T,\mathrm{et}},

\displaystyle\bar{\pi}_{\hat{\mathbf{U}}_{\hat{T}}/\mathbf{U}_{T}}\colon\hat{\mathbf{U}}_{\hat{T},\hat{c},\mathrm{et}}\to\mathbf{U}_{T,c,\mathrm{et}}

be the natural morphisms.
Let

\begin{gathered}\pi_{\hat{\mathbf{U}}_{\hat{T}},\ast}=\left[\bigoplus_{x\in T}\pi_{\hat{\mathbf{R}}_{x,S},\ast}\oplus\bigoplus_{\eta\in Y_{1}}\pi_{\hat{\mathbf{R}}_{\eta,T},\ast}\to\bigoplus_{\begin{subarray}{c}x\in T\\
\eta_{x}\in Y_{1}^{x}\end{subarray}}\pi_{\hat{\mathbf{K}}_{\eta_{x}},\ast}\right][-1]\colon\\
\mathrm{Ch}(\hat{\mathbf{U}}_{\hat{T},\mathrm{et}})\to\mathrm{Ch}(F^{\mathrm{perar}}_{\mathrm{et}}),\end{gathered}

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \bar{\pi}_{\hat{\mathbf{U}}_{\hat{T}},\hat{!}}&=\left[\bar{\pi}_{\hat{\mathbf{U}}_{\hat{T}},\ast}\to\bigoplus_{\mathfrak{p}\in S}\bar{\pi}_{\hat{\mathbf{K}}_{\mathfrak{p}},\ast}\right][-1]\\
&\cong\left[\bigoplus_{x\in T}\bar{\pi}_{\hat{\mathbf{R}}_{x,S},\ast}\oplus\bigoplus_{\eta\in Y_{1}}\bar{\pi}_{\hat{\mathbf{R}}_{\eta,T},\ast}\to\bigoplus_{\begin{subarray}{c}x\in T\\
\eta_{x}\in Y_{1}^{x}\end{subarray}}\bar{\pi}_{\hat{\mathbf{K}}_{\eta_{x}},\ast}\oplus\bigoplus_{\mathfrak{p}\in S}\bar{\pi}_{\hat{\mathbf{K}}_{\mathfrak{p}},\ast}\right][-1]\colon\end{aligned}

\displaystyle\mathrm{Ch}(\hat{\mathbf{U}}_{\hat{T},\hat{c},\mathrm{et}})\to\mathrm{Ch}(F^{\mathrm{perar}}_{\mathrm{et}})

be the functors as defined in (
4.6.2
) and (
4.6.7
),
where the functors pulled back from
D(\hat{\mathbf{U}}_{\hat{T},\mathrm{et}})
to
D(\hat{\mathbf{U}}_{\hat{T},\hat{c},\mathrm{et}})

are denoted by putting overlines.

For the rest of this subsection,
we assume that
A
is complete,
\mathbf{A}=\hat{\mathbf{A}}
,

\zeta_{p}\in A
and
U_{S}=\Spec A[1/p]
,
in order to use the results of Sections
9
and
10.3
.

Proposition 10.5.1
.

The morphisms

R\bar{\pi}_{\mathbf{U}_{T},\ast}\Lambda\to R\bar{\pi}_{\hat{\mathbf{U}}_{\hat{T}},\ast}\Lambda,\quad R\bar{\pi}_{\mathbf{U}_{T},!}\Lambda\to R\bar{\pi}_{\hat{\mathbf{U}}_{\hat{T}},\hat{!}}\Lambda

in
D(F^{\mathrm{perar}}_{\mathrm{et}})
given by (
4.6.17
) are isomorphisms.

Proof.

We have morphisms of distinguished triangles

\begin{CD}R\bar{\pi}_{\mathbf{U}_{T},!}\Lambda @>{}>{}>R\bar{\pi}_{\mathbf{U}_{T},\ast}\Lambda @>{}>{}>\bigoplus_{\mathfrak{p}\in S}R\bar{\pi}_{\mathbf{K}_{\mathfrak{p}}^{h},\ast}\Lambda\\
@V{}V{}V@V{}V{}V@V{}V{}V\\
R\bar{\pi}_{\hat{\mathbf{U}}_{\hat{T}},\hat{!}}\Lambda @>{}>{}>R\bar{\pi}_{\hat{\mathbf{U}}_{\hat{T}},\ast}\Lambda @>{}>{}>\bigoplus_{\mathfrak{p}\in S}R\bar{\pi}_{\hat{\mathbf{K}}_{\mathfrak{p}},\ast}\Lambda,\end{CD}

\begin{CD}\bigoplus_{x\in T}R\bar{\pi}_{\mathbf{R}_{x,S}^{h},!}\Lambda @>{}>{}>R\bar{\pi}_{\mathbf{U}_{T},!}\Lambda @>{}>{}>\bigoplus_{\eta\in Y_{1}}R\bar{\pi}_{\mathbf{R}_{\eta,T}^{h},\ast}\Lambda\\
@V{}V{}V@V{}V{}V@V{}V{}V\\
\bigoplus_{x\in T}R\bar{\pi}_{\hat{\mathbf{R}}_{x,S},\hat{!}}\Lambda @>{}>{}>R\bar{\pi}_{\hat{\mathbf{U}}_{\hat{T}},\hat{!}}\Lambda @>{}>{}>\bigoplus_{\eta\in Y_{1}}R\bar{\pi}_{\hat{\mathbf{R}}_{\eta,T},\ast}\Lambda\end{CD}

by (
4.6.18
) and (
4.6.20
).
The morphism

R\bar{\pi}_{\mathbf{K}_{\mathfrak{p}}^{h},\ast}\Lambda\to R\bar{\pi}_{\hat{\mathbf{K}}_{\mathfrak{p}},\ast}\Lambda

is an isomorphism by Proposition
5.2.1
.
For any
F^{\prime}\in F^{\mathrm{perar}}
, the morphism

R\Gamma(\mathbf{R}_{\eta,T}^{h}(F^{\prime}),\Lambda)\to R\Gamma(\hat{\mathbf{R}}_{\eta,T}(F^{\prime}),\Lambda)

is an isomorphism by Fujiwara-Gabber’s formal base change theorem

[
Fuj95
, Corollary 6.6.4]
,
[
ILO14
, Exposé XX, §4.4]
,

[
BM21
, Corollary 1.18 (2)]
.
This implies that the morphism

R\bar{\pi}_{\mathbf{R}_{\eta,T}^{h},\ast}\Lambda\to R\bar{\pi}_{\hat{\mathbf{R}}_{\eta,T},\ast}\Lambda

is an isomorphism.

Finally we show that the morphism

R\bar{\pi}_{\mathbf{R}_{x,S}^{h},!}\Lambda\to R\bar{\pi}_{\hat{\mathbf{R}}_{x,S},\hat{!}}\Lambda

is an isomorphism.
Since
S
is the set of all primes above
p
,
we have
R_{x,S}^{h}=A_{x}^{h}[1/p]
and
\hat{R}_{x,S}=\hat{A}_{x}[1/p]
.
The rings
A_{x}^{h}
and
\hat{A}_{x}
satisfy the conditions
listed at the beginning of Section
8
.
Hence we may apply Proposition
10.3.6
,
proving that the above morphism is indeed an isomorphism.
∎

Note that the morphisms

R\bar{\pi}_{\mathbf{R}_{\eta,T}^{h},!}\Lambda\to R\bar{\pi}_{\hat{\mathbf{R}}_{\eta,T},\hat{!}}\Lambda,\quad R\bar{\pi}_{\mathbf{R}_{x,S}^{h},\ast}\Lambda\to R\bar{\pi}_{\hat{\mathbf{R}}_{x,S},\ast}\Lambda

are not isomorphisms.

By this proposition, (
4.6.21
) gives a commutative diagram

\begin{CD}R\bar{\pi}_{\mathbf{U}_{T},\ast}\Lambda\mathbin{\otimes}^{L}R\bar{\pi}_{\mathbf{U}_{T},!}\Lambda @>{}>{}>R\bar{\pi}_{\mathbf{U}_{T},!}\Lambda\\
\Big\|\Big\|\\
R\bar{\pi}_{\hat{\mathbf{U}}_{\hat{T}},\ast}\Lambda\mathbin{\otimes}^{L}R\bar{\pi}_{\hat{\mathbf{U}}_{\hat{T}},\hat{!}}\Lambda @>{}>{}>R\bar{\pi}_{\hat{\mathbf{U}}_{\hat{T}},\hat{!}}\Lambda\end{CD}

in
D(F^{\mathrm{perar}}_{\mathrm{et}})
.
The diagram (
4.6.19
) gives a morphism of distinguished triangles

(10.5.1)

\begin{CD}R\bar{\pi}_{\mathbf{U}_{T},!}\Lambda @>{}>{}>\displaystyle\bigoplus_{x\in T}R\bar{\pi}_{\mathbf{R}_{x,S}^{h},\ast}\Lambda\oplus\displaystyle\bigoplus_{\eta\in Y_{1}}R\bar{\pi}_{\mathbf{R}_{\eta,T}^{h},\ast}\Lambda @>{}>{}>\displaystyle\bigoplus_{\begin{subarray}{c}x\in T\\
\eta_{x}\in Y_{1}^{x}\end{subarray}}R\bar{\pi}_{\mathbf{K}_{\eta_{x}}^{h},\ast}\Lambda\oplus\displaystyle\bigoplus_{\mathfrak{p}\in S}R\bar{\pi}_{\mathbf{K}_{\mathfrak{p}}^{h},\ast}\Lambda\\
\Big\|@V{}V{}V@V{}V{}V\\
R\bar{\pi}_{\hat{\mathbf{U}}_{\hat{T}},\hat{!}}\Lambda @>{}>{}>\displaystyle\bigoplus_{x\in T}R\bar{\pi}_{\hat{\mathbf{R}}_{x,S},\ast}\Lambda\oplus\displaystyle\bigoplus_{\eta\in Y_{1}}R\bar{\pi}_{\hat{\mathbf{R}}_{\eta,T},\ast}\Lambda @>{}>{}>\displaystyle\bigoplus_{\begin{subarray}{c}x\in T\\
\eta_{x}\in Y_{1}^{x}\end{subarray}}R\bar{\pi}_{\hat{\mathbf{K}}_{\eta_{x}},\ast}\Lambda\oplus\displaystyle\bigoplus_{\mathfrak{p}\in S}R\bar{\pi}_{\hat{\mathbf{K}}_{\mathfrak{p}},\ast}\Lambda.\end{CD}

Proposition 10.5.2
.

The objects

\bigoplus_{x\in T}R\bar{\pi}_{\hat{\mathbf{R}}_{x,S},\ast}\Lambda,\;\bigoplus_{\eta\in Y_{1}}R\bar{\pi}_{\hat{\mathbf{R}}_{\eta,T},\ast}\Lambda,\;\bigoplus_{\begin{subarray}{c}x\in T\\
\eta_{x}\in Y_{1}^{x}\end{subarray}}R\bar{\pi}_{\hat{\mathbf{K}}_{\eta_{x}},\ast}\Lambda,\;\bigoplus_{\mathfrak{p}\in S}R\bar{\pi}_{\hat{\mathbf{K}}_{\mathfrak{p}},\ast}\Lambda

are all concentrated in degrees
\leq 2
with cohomologies in
\mathcal{W}_{F}
.
In particular,

R\bar{\pi}_{\hat{\mathbf{U}}_{\hat{T}},\ast}\Lambda,R\bar{\pi}_{\hat{\mathbf{U}}_{\hat{T}},\hat{!}}\Lambda\in\langle\mathcal{W}_{F}\rangle_{F^{\mathrm{perar}}_{\mathrm{et}}}
.

Proof.

This follows from
Propositions
10.3.5
,
9.5.3
,
9.5.2
,
6.2.1
and
6.4.1
.
∎

We will define a trace isomorphism for
R\bar{\pi}_{\hat{\mathbf{U}}_{\hat{T}},\hat{!}}

using the lower triangle of (
10.5.1
) as follows.
This triangle induces a morphism

(10.5.2)

\bigoplus_{x\in T}\pi_{0}R^{2}\bar{\pi}_{\hat{\mathbf{R}}_{x,S},\ast}\Lambda\oplus\bigoplus_{\eta\in Y_{1}}\pi_{0}R^{2}\bar{\pi}_{\hat{\mathbf{R}}_{\eta,T},\ast}\Lambda\to\bigoplus_{\begin{subarray}{c}x\in T\\
\eta_{x}\in Y_{1}^{x}\end{subarray}}\pi_{0}R^{2}\bar{\pi}_{\hat{\mathbf{K}}_{\eta_{x}},\ast}\Lambda\oplus\bigoplus_{\mathfrak{p}\in S}\pi_{0}R^{2}\bar{\pi}_{\hat{\mathbf{K}}_{\mathfrak{p}},\ast}\Lambda.

For any
x\in T
,
\eta_{x}\in Y_{1}^{x}
and
\mathfrak{p}\in S
,
we have isomorphisms

(10.5.3)

\pi_{0}R^{2}\bar{\pi}_{\hat{\mathbf{K}}_{\eta_{x}},\ast}\Lambda\overset{\sim}{\to}\mathfrak{R}_{F_{x}/F}\Lambda,\quad\pi_{0}R^{2}\bar{\pi}_{\hat{\mathbf{K}}_{\mathfrak{p}},\ast}\Lambda\overset{\sim}{\to}\mathfrak{R}_{F_{\mathfrak{p}}/F}\Lambda

by the morphism (
6.2.1
) and Proposition
6.4.1
.
With these isomorphisms, we have

(10.5.4)

\pi_{0}R^{2}\bar{\pi}_{\hat{\mathbf{R}}_{x,S},\ast}\Lambda\overset{\sim}{\to}\mathfrak{R}_{F_{x}/F}\left(\left(\bigoplus_{\eta_{x}\in Y_{1}^{x}}\Lambda\oplus\bigoplus_{\mathfrak{p}\in S^{x}}\Lambda\right)_{0}\right)

by Proposition
10.3.5
.
Also for any
\eta\in Y_{1}
, we have a surjection

(10.5.5)

\pi_{0}R^{2}\bar{\pi}_{\hat{\mathbf{R}}_{\eta,T},\ast}\Lambda\twoheadrightarrow\mathfrak{R}_{F_{\eta}/F}\left(\left(\bigoplus_{x\in T\cap\eta_{0}}\mathfrak{R}_{F_{x}/F_{\eta}}\Lambda\right)_{0}\right)

and the part
\pi_{0}R^{2}\bar{\pi}_{\hat{\mathbf{R}}_{\eta,T},\ast}\Lambda

of the morphism (
10.5.2
) factors through this surjection by Proposition
9.5.5
.

Proposition 10.5.3
.

The morphisms (
10.5.2
), (
10.5.3
), (
10.5.4
) and (
10.5.5
) form an exact sequence

\displaystyle\bigoplus_{x\in T}\mathfrak{R}_{F_{x}/F}\left(\left(\bigoplus_{\eta_{x}\in Y_{1}^{x}}\Lambda\oplus\bigoplus_{\mathfrak{p}\in S^{x}}\Lambda\right)_{0}\right)\oplus\bigoplus_{\eta\in Y_{1}}\mathfrak{R}_{F_{\eta}/F}\left(\left(\bigoplus_{x\in T\cap\eta_{0}}\mathfrak{R}_{F_{x}/F_{\eta}}\Lambda\right)_{0}\right)

\displaystyle\to\bigoplus_{\begin{subarray}{c}x\in T\\
\eta_{x}\in Y_{1}^{x}\end{subarray}}\mathfrak{R}_{F_{x}/F}\Lambda\oplus\bigoplus_{\mathfrak{p}\in S}\mathfrak{R}_{F_{\mathfrak{p}}/F}\Lambda

\displaystyle\to\Lambda\to 0,

where the last morphism (to
\Lambda
) is the sum of the norm maps.

Proof.

We may replace
F
by a large enough finite extension of
F

so that all the fields
F_{x}
,
F_{\eta}
and
F_{\mathfrak{p}}
in the statement are
F
.
Then the statement is a simple combinatorics of the irreducible components of

\mathfrak{X}\setminus U_{S}=Y\cup\overline{S}
.
∎

This proposition and the lower triangle of (
10.5.1
) defines a canonical morphism

(10.5.6)

R\bar{\pi}_{\hat{\mathbf{U}}_{\hat{T}},\hat{!}}\Lambda\to R^{3}\bar{\pi}_{\hat{\mathbf{U}}_{\hat{T}},\hat{!}}\Lambda[-3]\to\Lambda[-3].

This trace morphism and the trace morphism (
10.1.4
) are compatible:

Proposition 10.5.4
.

The morphism (
10.5.6
) and the morphism

R^{3}\bar{\pi}_{\mathbf{U}_{S},!}\Lambda\to\Lambda

of (
10.1.4
) are compatible under the isomorphisms

R^{3}\bar{\pi}_{\mathbf{U}_{S},!}\Lambda\overset{\sim}{\to}R^{3}\bar{\pi}_{\mathbf{U}_{T},!}\Lambda\overset{\sim}{\to}R^{3}\bar{\pi}_{\hat{\mathbf{U}}_{\hat{T}},\hat{!}}\Lambda.

Proof.

Denote
(
10.5.6
)
by
\Tr_{S}
and (
10.1.4
) by
\Tr_{T}
.
By (
10.4.7
) and (
10.5.1
), we have a commutative diagram

\begin{CD}\displaystyle\bigoplus_{\mathfrak{p}\in S}R\bar{\pi}_{\mathbf{K}_{\mathfrak{p}}^{h},\ast}\Lambda @>{}>{}>R\bar{\pi}_{\mathbf{U}_{S},!}\Lambda[1]@>{\Tr_{S}}>{}>\Lambda[-2]\\
@V{}V{}V\Big\|\\
\displaystyle\bigoplus_{\begin{subarray}{c}x\in T\\
\eta_{x}\in Y_{1}^{x}\end{subarray}}R\bar{\pi}_{\hat{\mathbf{K}}_{\eta_{x}},\ast}\Lambda\oplus\displaystyle\bigoplus_{\mathfrak{p}\in S}R\bar{\pi}_{\hat{\mathbf{K}}_{\mathfrak{p}},\ast}\Lambda @>{}>{}>R\bar{\pi}_{\hat{\mathbf{U}}_{\hat{T}},\hat{!}}\Lambda[1]@>{}>{\Tr_{T}}>\Lambda[-2]\end{CD}

in
\langle\mathcal{W}_{F}\rangle_{F^{\mathrm{perar}}_{\mathrm{et}}}
.
Applying
\acute{\mathsf{a}}
, we have a commutative diagram

(10.5.7)

\begin{CD}\displaystyle\bigoplus_{\mathfrak{p}\in S}\acute{\mathsf{a}}R\bar{\pi}_{\mathbf{K}_{\mathfrak{p}}^{h},\ast}\Lambda @>{}>{}>\acute{\mathsf{a}}R\bar{\pi}_{\mathbf{U}_{S},!}\Lambda[1]@>{\Tr_{S}}>{}>\Lambda[-2]\\
@V{}V{}V\Big\|\\
\displaystyle\bigoplus_{\begin{subarray}{c}x\in T\\
\eta_{x}\in Y_{1}^{x}\end{subarray}}\acute{\mathsf{a}}R\bar{\pi}_{\hat{\mathbf{K}}_{\eta_{x}},\ast}\Lambda\oplus\displaystyle\bigoplus_{\mathfrak{p}\in S}\acute{\mathsf{a}}R\bar{\pi}_{\hat{\mathbf{K}}_{\mathfrak{p}},\ast}\Lambda @>{}>{}>\acute{\mathsf{a}}R\bar{\pi}_{\hat{\mathbf{U}}_{\hat{T}},\hat{!}}\Lambda[1]@>{}>{\Tr_{T}}>\Lambda[-2]\end{CD}

in
D^{b}(\mathrm{I}\mathrm{P}\mathrm{Alg}_{u}/F)
.
By Proposition
3.1.5
,
it is enough to show that
\Tr_{S}=\Tr_{T}
in this diagram.
By Propositions
10.5.2
and
3.1.6
, the object

\acute{\mathsf{a}}R\bar{\pi}_{\hat{\mathbf{U}}_{\hat{T}},\hat{!}}\Lambda[1]

is concentrated in degrees
\leq 2
.
By Proposition
10.5.3
,
the
\pi_{0}
of its
H^{2}
is
\Lambda
.
Hence
\pi_{0}H^{2}
of the diagram (
10.5.7
) can be written as

\begin{CD}\displaystyle\bigoplus_{\mathfrak{p}\in S}\mathfrak{R}_{F_{\mathfrak{p}}/F}\Lambda @>{}>{}>\Lambda @>{\Tr_{S}}>{}>\Lambda\\
@V{}V{}V\Big\|\\
\displaystyle\bigoplus_{\begin{subarray}{c}x\in T\\
\eta_{x}\in Y_{1}^{x}\end{subarray}}\mathfrak{R}_{F_{x}/F}\Lambda\oplus\displaystyle\bigoplus_{\mathfrak{p}\in S}\mathfrak{R}_{F_{\mathfrak{p}}/F}\Lambda @>{}>{}>\Lambda @>{}>{\Tr_{T}=\mathrm{id}}>\Lambda\end{CD}

The composite of the upper two horizontal morphisms is the sum of the norm maps
by Proposition
10.1.4
(
2
).
The composite of the lower two horizontal morphisms is the sum of the norm maps
by the definition of (
10.5.6
).
Since the first upper horizontal morphism

\bigoplus_{\mathfrak{p}\in S}\mathfrak{R}_{F_{\mathfrak{p}}/F}\Lambda\to\Lambda

is also the sum of the norm maps and hence surjective,
we know that
\Tr_{S}\colon\Lambda\to\Lambda
is the identity map.
Therefore
\Tr_{S}=\Tr_{T}
in the diagram (
10.5.7
).
∎

Thus we have a commutative diagram

\begin{CD}R\bar{\pi}_{\mathbf{U}_{S},\ast}\Lambda\mathbin{\otimes}^{L}R\bar{\pi}_{\mathbf{U}_{S},!}\Lambda @>{}>{}>\Lambda_{\infty}[-3]\\
\Big\|\Big\|\\
R\bar{\pi}_{\hat{\mathbf{U}}_{\hat{T}},\ast}\Lambda\mathbin{\otimes}^{L}R\bar{\pi}_{\hat{\mathbf{U}}_{\hat{T}},\hat{!}}\Lambda @>{}>{}>\Lambda_{\infty}[-3].\end{CD}

Proposition 10.5.5
.

The above is a perfect pairing.

Proof.

Denote
(\;\cdot\;)^{\vee}=R\sheafhom_{F^{\mathrm{perar}}_{\mathrm{et}}}(\;\cdot\;,\Lambda_{\infty})
.
By Proposition
4.6.4
, we have a morphism of distinguished triangles

\begin{CD}\displaystyle\bigoplus_{\eta\in Y_{1}}R\bar{\pi}_{\hat{\mathbf{R}}_{\eta,T},\hat{!}}\Lambda @>{}>{}>R\bar{\pi}_{\hat{\mathbf{U}}_{\hat{T}},\ast}\Lambda @>{}>{}>\displaystyle\bigoplus_{x\in T}R\bar{\pi}_{\hat{\mathbf{R}}_{x,S},\ast}\Lambda\\
@V{}V{}V@V{}V{}V@V{}V{}V\\
\displaystyle\bigoplus_{\eta\in Y_{1}}(R\bar{\pi}_{\hat{\mathbf{R}}_{\eta,T},\ast}\Lambda)^{\vee}[-3]@>{}>{}>(R\bar{\pi}_{\hat{\mathbf{U}}_{\hat{T}},\hat{!}}\Lambda)^{\vee}[-3]@>{}>{}>\displaystyle\bigoplus_{x\in T}(R\bar{\pi}_{\hat{\mathbf{R}}_{x,S},\hat{!}}\Lambda)^{\vee}[-3]\end{CD}

The left vertical morphism is an isomorphism by Propositions
9.5.6

(and Proposition
3.1.11

if the constant field of
B_{\eta,T}
is not
F
but a finite extension of
F
).
The right vertical morphism is an isomorphism
by Propositions
10.3.5
and
10.2.2

(and similarly Proposition
3.1.11

if the residue field of
B_{x}^{h}
is not
F
but a finite extension of
F
).
Hence so is the middle.
∎

Proposition 10.5.6
.

The statement of Proposition
10.1.5
is true
if
n=1
,
\zeta_{p}\in A
and
U_{S}=\Spec A[1/p]
.

Proof.

This follows from Propositions
10.5.2
and
10.5.5
.
∎

10.6.
Main theorem

Now we can finish the proof of Proposition
10.1.5
.

Proof of Proposition
10.1.5
.

The case
n\geq 1
is reduced to the case
n=1
.
Let
S^{\prime}\subset P
be a finite subset containing
S
.
Let
U_{S^{\prime}}=X\setminus S^{\prime}
.
Then the statement for
U_{S}
is equivalent to the statement for
U_{S^{\prime}}
.
Indeed, we have a morphism of distinguished triangles

\begin{CD}\bigoplus_{\mathfrak{p}\in S^{\prime}\setminus S}R\pi_{\mathbf{A}_{\mathfrak{p}}^{h},!}\mathfrak{T}(r)@>{}>{}>R\pi_{\mathbf{U}_{S},\ast}\mathfrak{T}(r)@>{}>{}>R\pi_{\mathbf{U}_{S^{\prime}},\ast}\mathfrak{T}(r)\\
@V{}V{}V@V{}V{}V@V{}V{}V\\
\bigoplus_{\mathfrak{p}\in S^{\prime}\setminus S}(R\pi_{\mathbf{A}_{\mathfrak{p}}^{h},\ast}\mathfrak{T}(r^{\prime}))^{\vee}[-3]@>{}>{}>(R\pi_{\mathbf{U}_{S},!}\mathfrak{T}(r^{\prime}))^{\vee}[-3]@>{}>{}>(R\pi_{\mathbf{U}_{S^{\prime}},!}\mathfrak{T}(r^{\prime}))^{\vee}[-3]\end{CD}

in
D(F^{\mathrm{perar}}_{\mathrm{et}})
by Proposition
4.3.2
.
The left vertical morphism is an isomorphism
of objects of
\langle\mathcal{W}_{F}\rangle_{F^{\mathrm{perar}}_{\mathrm{et}}}

by Propositions
6.5.1
and
7.2.3
.
Therefore the invertibility of the other two vertical morphisms is equivalent to each other.

Next, to prove the statement of the theorem,
we may assume
\zeta_{p}\in A
.
Indeed, let
A^{\prime}
be the normalization of
A
in
K(\zeta_{p})
.
Let
U^{\prime}_{S^{\prime}}
be the inverse image of
U_{S}
by the morphism
\Spec A^{\prime}\to\Spec A
.
We may assume that
S
contains all primes above
p
and
U^{\prime}_{S^{\prime}}\to U_{S}
is étale by the previous step.
Since
[K(\zeta_{p}):K]
is prime to
p
,
a norm argument shows that the statement for
U^{\prime}_{S^{\prime}}
implies the statement for
U_{S}
.

Now we are reduced to the case
n=1
,
\zeta_{p}\in A
and
U_{S}=\Spec A[1/p]
,
which is proved in Proposition
10.5.6
.
∎

Theorem 10.6.1
.

Assume that
A
is complete and take
\mathbf{A}
to be the canonical lifting system.
Let
n\geq 1
. Let
r,r^{\prime}\in\mathbb{Z}
with
r+r^{\prime}=2
.

(1)

The objects
R\mathbf{\Gamma}(\mathbf{U}_{S},\mathfrak{T}_{n}(r))

and
R\mathbf{\Gamma}_{c}(\mathbf{U}_{S},\mathfrak{T}_{n}(r))

belong to
D^{b}(\mathrm{I}\mathrm{P}\mathrm{Alg}_{u}/F)
.

(2)

The composite morphism

R\mathbf{\Gamma}(\mathbf{U}_{S},\mathfrak{T}_{n}(r))\mathbin{\otimes}^{L}R\mathbf{\Gamma}_{c}(\mathbf{U}_{S},\mathfrak{T}_{n}(r^{\prime}))\to R\mathbf{\Gamma}_{c}(\mathbf{U}_{S},\mathfrak{T}_{n}(2))\to\Lambda_{\infty}[-3]

obtained by applying
\acute{\mathsf{a}}
to the morphism in Proposition
10.1.5
(
2
)
is a perfect pairing in
D(F^{\mathrm{ind}\mathrm{rat}}_{\mathrm{pro}\mathrm{et}})
.

Proof.

This follows from Propositions
10.1.5
,
3.1.5
,
3.1.4
and
3.1.10
.
∎

This finishes the proof of Theorems
1.3.1
and
1.3.2
:
Statement (
1
) of the first theorem is
Proposition
3.1.4
;
Statement (
2
) follows from Proposition
4.3.3
;
and Statement (
3
) is a consequence of the previous two statements.
